# ASCII City quality programme

Fix the reported defects first. Then complete the numbered quality passes in order, as small reviewable changes. People and the Cabin Cove prototype are the current visual baseline, not targets for wholesale replacement. New content in the fixes uses the same standard.

## Fixes first

- [x] Aim-aware, unobstructed interaction selection shared by E and its prompt. People and dropped items must remain selectable beside other interactions, including indoors.
- [x] No city traffic, crowd or night bed on islands, including inside the shelter and Fern House.
- [x] World sounds lose volume and high frequencies with distance and pan from their source; fireworks need believable range and propagation delay.
- [x] Shore water advances and recedes across sand; white/pale-blue foam follows the moving edge rather than overlaying transparent stripes on the sea.
- [x] Televisions have an unlit case/bezel and a separate animated screen with recognisable programmes.
- [x] Refit Fern House: attached kitchen units, enclosed bathroom, reachable toilet/bath, coherent partitions, grounded plants and workshop, ceiling-mounted lighting.
- [x] Black lettering on all island information and name signs, smaller boards, readable close/far text. Remove Fern's HOUSE / FALLS sign.
- [x] Match island waymarks to actual visible sights: birds, tide pools, and a physical fire ring at the Fern camp. Keep markers out of access routes.
- [x] Furnish homes: buy/place/rotate/move/store furniture without blocking exits or intersecting walls. Keep existing workshop equipment compatible.
- [x] Individually switchable lamps and a usable record player, with saved state.
- [x] IDEA furniture shop and a plush shark, using proper fitted furniture and product models.
- [x] Framed pictures: an in-game camera/print or local image upload, with bounded local save storage and no external upload requirement.

- [x] Reflections show only what stands beyond the water: signs in front of the bay (ferry terminal) no longer mirror into it.
- [x] Shore foam rides the water: none on dry sand ahead of the wash, ending at the waterline; at walls and rocks the wave breaks white at the edge and falls back instead of a line sweeping over still water.
- [x] Ferry: remove the boarding-gate location message that stays up for the whole ride; step ashore by walking off, without E.
- [x] Fern House: rotate so the front faces the dock; close the backyard spot that lets you walk in; real external windows like Cabin Cove's; better doors.
- [x] Fern House interior: TV stands in a doorway (lofts too; check all homes); move the washroom sign beside its door; washroom walls should be washroom walls from outside too (copy the loft's bathroom walls) instead of every wall changing on entry; rework the sink (fewer polygons, it may be laggy) and the fridge.

- [x] Motel: move the bathroom entrance away from the shower and arrange the TV in front of the bed, facing the sofa.

## Quality bar for each pass

- Shared measurements for connected surfaces, visible geometry and collision; follow [corridor geometry notes](corridor-geometry-notes.md).
- Rounded profiles where appropriate, real thickness, supported fixtures, clean joins, usable clearance and correct orientation. Texture cannot substitute for silhouette.
- Calm materials with filtered small detail; smooth changes with distance, readable signs without repeated letters, and no flickering overlaps.
- Features have visible, audible and persistent results. E prompts describe the actual selected target.
- Review close/wide and oblique browser views, day/night and relevant weather; walk access routes and use real controls.
- Keep terrain/building caches, culling and render cost under review. Measure representative scenes before adopting broadly.

Give them decayed, aged and abandoned styles (rusted or dented shutters, boarded windows, peeling paint, sun-faded ghost lettering, weeds at the step, flyers and dumped junk) and make them hotspots for graffiti. 45. [ ] Emergency service vehicles: the ones parked outside police, fire and hospital buildings should be real vehicles you can steal; ambulances stealable and drivable, with their own handling (heavy, slow to turn, quick off the line, lights and siren).
46. [ ] Belle Époque ivy: the vines up the sides of the Belle Époque buildings look flat; give them real stems, leaf clusters with depth and light, seasonal colour.

## Checkpoint

The reported-fix batch is implemented and reviewed in Chromium, including shoreline wash/backwash, Fern kitchen/bathroom/living room, IDEA, placed furniture, night lighting, and the 390px phone menu. Regression coverage includes actual E/Q/F controls, photograph save/reload, packed lamp state, workshop refresh, legacy save migration, reachable island sights, and shared tide-pool collision heights.

At home, F opens furnishings; Q unpacks a held purchase, Q rotates during placement, E places, and Esc cancels. Lamps and frames use aimed E. The compact camera captures the view with Q; the album holds eight local photographs. Each home supports 48 movable furnishings. Plush sharks can go on the floor or supported tables. IDEA occupies one existing open hardware-store location and is searchable by name.

The numbered passes above remain the work queue. The new models establish reusable rounded geometry and filtered materials, but no broad category is marked complete merely because these foundations exist.


## Completed chunks

- Shared furniture, first chunk: dining chairs, four-leg tables and upholstered sofas now have rounded solids, connected rails/legs, separate cushions and filtered materials. The shared helpers reach existing apartments, hotels, cafés and other callers. Standard home sofas now use the shared builder. Reviewed front, back, oblique and studio views; existing seating and hotel controls are covered by browser regressions. Beds, shelves, wardrobes, counters, stools and benches remain in pass 1.
- Shared furniture, second chunk: fitted beds, wardrobes, workshop cupboards, TV stands, bedside units and open book/display shelves in all four homes and three hotel tiers now use shared rounded construction. Beds have joined timber frames/headboards, separate mattresses, shaped pillows and tucked bedding aligned with the head. Cabinet doors, drawers and handles face their approach; shelf tops retain the existing display height. Corrected overlapping hotel bedside units and small Belle/motel TV-base wall intersections. Wall/cabinet/bed clearance checks cover all seven plans; existing sleep, amenity and workshop routes remain usable. Warmed local median render times at 177×66 cells: studio 3.9→6.8 ms, loft 4.3→5.4 ms, Fern bedroom 5.2→5.4 ms, suite bedroom 3.7→5.3 ms; these are local observations. Counters, stools and benches remain in pass 1.
- Hotel smoke-test timing follows the actual sleep and fade transitions instead of assuming the animation finishes within fixed wall-clock waits on a busy browser runner.

Tall stools use shaped padded seats, turned metal bases and solid rounded footrest rings; low stools have open legs and joined supports. Existing street-bench pads remain flat; the renderer also accepts their terrain height explicitly. Booths, shell chairs, round/pedestal tables and pouffes remain in pass 1; specialist built-ins also receive their own interior passes.

Lazy Susans carry supported ceramic dishes. Booth seating uses the actual 46 cm cushion height; dining patrons use the height of their chair/stool. - Bathrooms, first chunk: shared sinks, toilets and oval baths now have hollow curved profiles with closed rims and recessed floors/water. Enamel and steel retain distinct finishes; taps, flush handles, feet and plumbing are supported solid shapes. Sink mirror frames find the actual wall plane, with a basin-mounted support if no wall is behind them. Moved hotel sinks fully clear of their partition and brought toilets to the back wall. Shop-bathroom routes, home/hotel amenities, immediate toilet aiming, use/flushing and jail interactions pass. Reviewed front/side/Fern/hotel/jail and 390px day/night views with real touch toilet use. - Interaction follow-up: line of sight derives eye height from the current player state, so a room entered before the next render uses indoor metres immediately. Grand Hotel check-in exercises that transition.

- Interaction integration checks: transparent counter grilles preserve sight; seated head height and jail cab-driver conversations remain selectable; the VIP host keeps the service action; ceiling lamps require a deliberate aim. Shop reachability checks now choose a visible counter approach. Casino staking tests isolate games from patrons and aim at equipment, while person selection has separate coverage. Camera and flatpack art use visible glyphs, including at the hand grip.
- Indoor audio uses the building's actual outdoor location for both nature ambience and sirens. Real Web Audio checks confirm mainland sirens stay silent in an island interior, nearby sources play, moving inside does not change the outdoor source location, and changing buildings clears distant voices.
- Activity feedback (pass 29), fireworks sound: the show stopped launching whenever you were in a room, so Saturday night in a waterfront bar was silent. Shells now keep going indoors and their bursts arrive muffled through the walls via the shared distance profile. Each launch also has a mortar thump from the barge, delayed, panned and dulled with distance like the burst. Chromium: outdoor thump/boom delays, indoor show continues at under 30% gain with ≤900 Hz cutoff; existing calendar and two-hour show checks pass (21 checks). Node 1058/1058. Firework smoke belongs to the smoke chunk.
- Activity feedback (pass 29), fire: Fern's camp fire was never added to `FIRES` (filtered once at load, before the islands are built), so it had no flames or firelight while E offered "cook over the campfire" at a cold ring. It now registers like the coast camps, keeps island-sights' stone ring and logs (no duplicate stones), and burns at night. Night-only camp fires also light whenever you cook over them, by day too (`fireLit`). Lit campfires crackle and snap, and burning cars crackle faster and louder, from where they are through the shared distance profile, muffled indoors. Chromium: lit/cold/cooking states, crackle position, indoor muffling and car vs campfire, plus cooking, island sights, coast home and audio suites (23 checks); night before/after and oblique views inspected (`tmp/fire-fern-*.png`). Node 1058/1058.
- Activity feedback (pass 29), smoke: lit campfires now trail smoke, and Fern House's chimney smokes after dark (the "light evening smoke" the handoff describes had no emitter in the source). Each firework burst leaves a slow cloud hanging where it burst, faint against the night and lit in the colour of the latest burst; at most 24 at once, oldest first. World smoke is emitted only within view distance and never indoors, under the shared 140-puff haze cap. Chromium: cold by day, rising off the lit fire and out of the chimney top after dark, none made indoors, firework cloud cap/order/lighting; existing smoke, calendar, fireworks, fire and cooking checks (13). Views inspected: chimney at dusk, fireworks before/after and at a burst (`tmp/smoke-*.png`). Node 1058/1058.
- Activity feedback (pass 29), damage: a round that struck a wall, the ground or a car showed a 0.09 s spark and nothing else, and "*PANG* off the car" made no sound. Impacts now sound where they land (a clank off a car, a chip and grit off a wall, a thud into the ground), through the shared distance profile outdoors and at room scale indoors. Shots into a building's wall outdoors leave a hole on the exact face the shot crossed (from the cell boundary), drawn only from that side and depth-tested; the last 60 stay for the session. Indoor wall holes are not in this chunk. Chromium: real `pullTrigger` into a wall and a parked car (face, normal, drawn glyph, chip, clank, car damage) plus gun, gun-control and audio suites (16). Close and oblique views inspected (`tmp/damage-holes-*.png`). Node 1058/1058.
- Activity feedback (pass 29), crime: `gun.js` already had an unattended car's alarm calling in gunfire, but no car had an alarm. A car left parked with nobody in it (not a police car) that is shot or knocked now goes off for 20 seconds: a synthesised warble/whoop/beep cycle from where it stands through the shared distance profile, and its head and tail lights flashing together. Hitting it again restarts it; getting in turns it off and clears it; frozen frames hush it with the sirens. Node: alarm rule (parked/driven/police/occupied, expiry, restart). Chromium: a real shot sets one positioned voice that expires, restarts and stops on entry; 30 car, gun, crime and audio checks. Night flash on/off inspected (`tmp/crime-alarm-*.png`). Node 1059/1059.
- Soundscape (pass 30), chunk 1, activity feedback: car explosions, burning aircraft and abandoned-aircraft crashes/ditchings now use the shared distance profile (`worldClip` in `audio.js`): later, quieter and duller with distance, panned, muffled indoors, with a smooth edge instead of a hard 300/400 m cut-off. They previously measured distance from `px,py`, which are furniture coordinates indoors, so a blast on the mainland could play at full volume inside an island shelter. `audioWorldPosition` is now the only listener position: subway stations reached by train, train cars and the lighthouse lamp room also listen from outdoors, and the rotor loop uses it instead of its own copy. Island nature beds and existing distance behaviour are unchanged. Node: sound-distance test covers the new room kinds. Chromium: 27 aircraft/siren/gameplay/transit audio checks, including a new explosion test (near, 300 m delayed/dulled, other-island silent, beside-the-shelter muffled).
- Soundscape (pass 30), chunk 2, music: every recorded bed now runs through its own stereo panner. A playing record player pans toward where it stands in the home (centred as you stand over it, distance gain unchanged), and Nico's rooftop serenade pans toward the bar roof. Your own boombox and every room/district bed stay centred. Chromium: record pan flips with facing in the real furnishing flow; actual bed panners follow the record, the serenade and the boombox (13 furnishing/siren/gameplay audio checks).
- Soundscape (pass 30), chunk 3, machines: laundromats, whose walls of drums are all drawn turning, now have a synthesised washer layer (tumbling thud, slosh, motor hum) over the existing fluorescent hum, calibrated to the recorded beds by measured RMS (CAL 1.0 at gain 1). "Your machine clunks to a stop" now plays a last heavy turn and the door lock clicking free, once, only if you're in that laundromat. Node: the mix runs washers only in laundromats. Chromium: real entry and E to start a wash, layer audible inside and silent after leaving, one finish clunk (11 audio checks). No subjective listening claim.

- Bathrooms, second chunk: the motel shower has a recessed walk-in tray/drain, clear framed side screen, supported rear controls/riser/head, and a mounted shelf/bottle. Its open front and shallow rim are walkable; the side screen blocks movement. Shared WC surrounds discover the real partition gaps, fit both faces through the wall depth, and close above the lintel without narrowing the walking opening. Privacy doors, bathroom accessories and the remaining layout review continue in pass 2.
- Activity feedback (pass 29), skating: the wheels' recorded roll played at one fixed level whenever they turned, from a crawl to flat out, and pushing, braking and scraping a wall were silent. The roll's level and pitch now follow board speed (cruising top keeps the old level). Each push lands with one scuff as the foot meets the street, a dragged foot (S) scrapes while it slows you, and the deck scrapes when you grind along a wall. The shared world profile now falls back to standing eye height if no frame has been drawn yet, rather than producing NaN (found when the crackle ran before a render). Node: roll level by speed. Chromium: real skate steps give one scuff per push, drag scrapes, a wall scrape, and the real loop pitched higher at speed; existing board, trick and audio checks (35 + 10). The real-keyboard "on your feet" trick test failed once under a loaded batch (its fixed 800 ms landing wait) and passed alone twice and in a re-run batch. Node 1060/1060.
- Activity feedback (pass 29), sports: the soccer ball had a kick sound and nothing after it. It now thuds as it bounces (dying away with each bounce), knocks off walls and street furniture by impact speed, gives one touch sound as you dribble into it (not one per frame), and splashes when it goes into the sea, all heard from where the ball is through the shared distance profile. `goods.js` stays pure: it calls audio only when an AudioContext exists, like `boats.js`. The gym's boxing and the fair booths are minigames with their own sounds and were left alone. Chromium: a real kick at a building wall, a dribble and a ball into the sea (4 ball/goods checks). Node 1060/1060.
- Activity feedback (pass 29), workshops: the 32-second Neon batch only made the arcade's generic arpeggio at the end. The game now reports each vent, seal and missed prompt as it happens, and the bench sounds like a bench: a rotor hum whose pitch and brightness follow the power you are balancing, a hiss when you vent, the press thunking down when you seal (misses and the finish keep the arcade blips, as cooking does). The hum stops with the batch and hushes on frozen frames. Node: event sequence for a played and a neglected batch. Chromium: real minigame dispatch, hum rising with power and stopping at the end; workshop, drugs, cooking and activity suites (18). Node 1061/1061.
- Activity feedback (pass 29), cooking: the pan already had its own sounds, but once the minigame closed nothing showed you had cooked, and a burnt dish left no trace. A good dish now leaves a wisp of steam where you cooked; a burnt one fills the air with smoke, which hangs about indoors for half a minute or more, and indoors sets the smoke alarm chirping in threes for eight seconds while you are in that room. The smoke rises at the grill or fire you used, or in front of you at a stove. Chromium: the real panel, recipe and finish path in a room (smoke, chirps only while inside, smoke lingering) and at a grill outdoors (steam only, no alarm); cooking, home and activity suites (11). Room view inspected (`tmp/cooking-burnt-room.png`). Node 1061/1061.
- Activity feedback (pass 29), fishing: the bobber's plop and the strike's splash happen out where the bobber is, but played dead centre at full level; they now come from the bobber through the shared distance profile (later, quieter, to its side), while the cast and reel stay with you. A cast onto ice ("skitters across the ice") or dry ground was silent; it now ticks away over the ice or taps into the dirt, out where it landed. The fishing sound helpers take an optional pan (cooking's callers are unchanged). The rings and spray at the bobber were already there. Node: dry-ground and ice casts report their sounds. Chromium: plop delay/level/pan from an 18 m bobber, ice ticks positioned, the cast's whoosh unpositioned; fishing, competition, cooking and activity suites (16). Node 1061/1061.

- Wildlife (pass 28), street pigeons: the flocks were flat ASCII sprites ('(o>') beside shaped island gulls. A pigeon body now joins the shared animal system (`ANIMAL_BODIES.pigeon` in `ambient.js`): plump body with a paler breast, sheened neck, small head and dark beak with a pale cere, folded wings with two dark bars, a dark-banded tail and pink feet; frames for head up, pecking (head at the ground) and wings up/down. Flocks mix slate, dark chequer and pale plumages at a real pigeon's size; each bird has a heading, shuffles forward along it, turns now and then, and flies off facing the way it goes. Sprites are cached: render median 7.3 ms empty vs 7.1 ms with 18 birds in view. Chromium: body frames, rendered plumage (no ASCII), scared birds' heading; the existing street-life pigeon test. Node 1065/1065. Noted, not changed: island gulls at their current size draw smaller than a pigeon.

Chromium: size order and ranges against a person and a pigeon at the same distance; island sights suite (9). Gulls on Fern's shore and cormorants on the rock inspected. Node 1065/1065.
- Wildlife (pass 28), aquarium fish: every tank and the street windows showed ASCII fish ('><(((o>', a shark of '=' signs). Each species is now a side profile painted in solid colour like the animals, countershaded, with its own fins, eye and markings: teardrop reef fish and sardines with forked tails, banded clownfish, a blue tang with its yellow tail, striped angelfish discs with streaming fins, sharks with a tall dorsal, gill slits and a long upper tail lobe, rays with rippling wings and a whip tail, plated turtles rowing, pulsing jellies trailing tentacles, curled seahorses, octopuses with sucker-dotted arms. Stocking, swimming, depth limits and tank edges are unchanged (shapes are in the tank's own coordinates, so both sides of the glass see a fish the right way round). Ocean-hall render median 6.1 ms before vs 6.7 ms after with the wall filling the screen. Chromium: every species fills a sensible share of its box, eyes lead the way they swim, fish stay inside their runs; the shared aquarium test now detects painted fish (it previously passed in the kelp tank on the kelp's own characters) and watches a few seconds for fish swimming high. Node 1065/1065.
- Wildlife (pass 28), glasshouse animals: the aviary's sixteen birds ('(o>' art and 'v'), the conservatory's ten butterflies ('}{') and its basking lizard ('~=<') are now shaped animals (`gardens.js`): macaw-bright parrots with curved beaks, long tails and beating wings, small finches, butterflies whose wings open and fold, a low lizard with splayed legs that shuffles along its rock. Paths, seed-feeding flurries and timings are unchanged; each faces the way it moves. Sized in metres indoors (parrot ~0.8 m, finch ~15 cm, butterfly ~8 cm, lizard ~35 cm). Chromium: frames (wingbeats, folded wings), every animal a shaped prop facing its travel, no ASCII left. Node 1065/1065.
- Wildlife (pass 28), fish on the line: the player's catch while reeling in ('<o)))><') and the regulars' catches ('<o)><') are now a shaped fish hanging nose-up from the line (`fishing-render.js`): a deep, flat body with a pale belly, a dorsal fin, a forked tail flicking, side on to you. The player's is the catch's own colour and its length follows its weight (a half-kilo fish ~30 cm, length as the cube root of weight, capped at 1.6 m); junk and curios keep their pictures. - Wildlife (pass 28), island butterflies: by day the island flowers' butterflies ('v'/'^') are the shaped butterflies (`island-sights.js`), ~8 cm across, wings beating and facing their drift; by night they stay fireflies' glowing points. Seeded177×66 final paired medians before→after: burger8.8→8.3ms, roastery9.5→8.9ms, bakery6.4→5.8ms; host-load tails varied, so these are scene observations rather than an FPS guarantee. Remaining painted cooking displays continue in pass3. Full CI for the preceding dining/aisle and dog-petting fixes is green.
- Kitchens (pass 3), Thai woks and diner range: removed the painted wok-flame strip and diner's false glowing pass. Thai now has a fitted two-burner range with hollow thin iron woks, real support rings, small local blue flames, joined steel/wood handles, shaped vegetables and curved noodle strands. Liquid edges derive from the same inner bowl profile; alternating local steam uses the existing bounded emitter. The diner has a side-wall-fitted griddle with eight supported burger patties and a clear staff approach. Default taco food is preserved. Three browser checks probe bowl floors/walls, burner and handle contacts, liquid fit, all noodle/vegetable contacts, model bounds, patty support, selective glow, steam timing/location and real E purchases. Four original hotplate checks, three tableware checks, all-shop routes/seats and bundle/parser/lathe/render-cost checks pass. Reviewed wide, close, end and wall-fitted views. Seeded177×66 final paired medians before→after: Thai7.2→7.7ms and diner4.3→4.4ms; the initial diner after sample was6.1ms and tails varied. These are local scene observations, not an FPS guarantee. Dining ledges and the remaining audit continue in pass3.
- Kitchens (pass 3), mounted ledges and bespoke tables: ten dining ledges now have closed bevelled tops, joined steel brackets, wall plates and fasteners meeting actual wall planes. Corrected the taco ledge's rotation through its side wall and moved all three stools inside the front wall. The roastery has a fitted timber/steel trestle table, a proper small café table and supported coffee settings. Izakaya low tables now have legs/aprons, rounded cloth floor cushions and supported meals for seated diners. Three new browser checks probe all bracket/top/wall contacts at four orientations, trestle feet/supports, tabletop settings, room bounds and taco seat placement; real C sit/stand works at all three relocated stools. The three existing tableware checks, all-shop walking/seating sweeps and bundle/parser/lathe/render-cost checks pass. Reviewed low/front/end views and thickened bracket rods after inspection. Seeded177×66 final paired medians before→after: bento6.2→7.2ms, roastery4.5→5.0ms, izakaya5.0→4.2ms; host-load tails varied, so these are scene observations rather than an FPS guarantee. Full CI for the preceding cooking-station chunk is green. Juice-bar fruit displays and the remaining audit continue in pass3.
- Kitchens (pass 3), juice-bar produce: replaced four crates whose lids had fruit glyphs painted on them with open slatted crate shells on stacked bases holding actual fruit. Oranges, apples (dimpled, with stalks on the top layer) and limes pack in square layers; every upper fruit touches the four beneath it and the bottom layer meets the rendered crate floor, so each heap rises above the rim. Bananas are six hands of five curved fingers with green crowns and dark flower ends: four on the crate floor, two bridging them on the lower fingers' tops. Retained the crate footprints. Fruit draws top layer first so the depth test rejects hidden fruit beneath. Three browser checks cover packing contacts, wall clearance, floor support, the banana bridges and curvature, and the room's four crates on clear floor with no painted lids; all kitchen/dining (45), shop route/seating/purchase and bundle/render-cost checks pass. Reviewed wide, close and downward views of each crate by day and at night. Warmed local medians at 212×66 cells, before→after: wide room 5.5→5.9 ms, close orange/lime crates 1.7→5.0 ms, close banana/apple crates 1.7→5.1 ms (about 11k fruit ray tests per close frame); local observations, not an FPS guarantee. A distant single-mound LOD would recover most of the close-up cost if it matters. The remaining dining audit continues in pass 3.

- Reported window-railway repair: the flat painted track and separate bridge have been replaced by closed rail/ballast/deck strips from one sampled oval with straight bridge grades, joined masonry approaches, seated piers and continuous guards. The river is recessed beneath both crossings. Train bodies pitch between their two actual wheelsets; shaped wheels meet the rendered rail heads. Moved town buildings outside the swept train and raised tunnel clearance for the chimney. Cached spatial bounds and reusable per-column row lists replace the all-pieces-per-cell loop; moving parts update separately. Four new browser checks probe every rail join/midpoint, deck/pier/guard contacts, full-lap body/scenery clearance, actual wheel-to-rail contacts and cached ray hits against an uncropped reference. All 12 railway/street-story browser checks and bundle/parser/render-cost/pure-story checks pass. Reviewed front, both oblique sides, grades, tunnel, night and phone views. Final ABBA local medians at177×60 cells, before→after: stationary close14.5–15.4→9.9–10.4ms, moving close14.4–15.0→11.7–12.5ms, side12.9–14.2→10.1–10.5ms. Street views stayed around10ms; the 20m distant view rose from9.7–10.0 to10.7–11.0ms. Tails varied; these are local scene observations rather than an FPS guarantee. Added AGENT_COORDINATION.md for the Claude/Codex ownership split; pass3 remains open.

- Home lighting (pass4), fitted lamps: shared pendants and IDEA's movable floor lamp now have closed hollow shade shells, frosted warm-white solid bulbs, separate sockets, joined supports and ceiling/weighted-base mounts. The floor lamp has three shade supports and a physical rotary switch; pendant mounts meet their actual ceilings and remain above walking routes. Materials keep metal/enamel housings unlit, warm the shade lining and filter fine cloth weave with distance. Cached profiles and once-per-draw switch reads avoid rebuilding shapes or looking up home state per cell. Two new browser checks probe open shade axes, real shell thickness/normals, visible glass, support/socket contacts, model bounds, selective emission and all four home types. Original buy/place/move/pack, independent switch, photographs and record/save controls pass (five combined browser checks); bundle/parser/render-cost checks pass. Reviewed close, side, underside, switched-off, night and wide views; removed noisy weave and glyph-based supports after inspection. ABBA177×66 medians before→after: close floor3.5–5.7→5.2–6.5ms, pendant2.2–2.7→2.8–3.2ms, Fern wide2.9–3.2→3.1ms, loft3.1→3.5–4.0ms. Host tails varied; these are local scene observations rather than an FPS guarantee. Pass4 remains open for electronics and decoration.
- Kitchens (pass 3), coolers, sacks and crates: the last painted box props in the dining rooms are built objects. The shared drinks cooler (kebab, takoyaki, taqueria, bodega, motel) is a steel carcass with a lit back, a lit COLD DRINKS header, a vented plinth, shelves holding one front row each of cans and bottles in facings of three, framed glass doors with sparse glints and mounted handles, facing into the room from its wall (the takoyaki and kebab coolers now stand 5 cm off the wall, not 7-10). Bakery flour is a cross-stacked pile of five paper sacks with printed bands, and the pizzeria's prep counter carries an opened sack with its mouth rolled over; the roastery's coffee is two open jute sacks with rolled rims round pale green beans and printed stripes. Yakitori's empties are a stack of lattice crates topped by an open crate of brown bottles in a divided grid; the table and both seats are upturned crates, the seat tops at the actual sitting height. Stencilled words on curved sacks and grazing sack tops were tried and removed: at room distance they rendered as doubled or garbled letters. Three browser checks cover cooler wall/room facing, product shelf support, clear glass and handle placement in all five rooms, sack layer contacts, the upright sack on its worktop and bean/rim heights, bottle cells and floor contact, crate seat heights; all shop, kitchen/dining (57) and life-polish (11) browser checks and the Node suite pass. Reviewed front, oblique and downward views in all seven rooms. Warmed local medians at 212×66 cells, before→after: kebab 2.4→4.5 ms, bodega 1.8→5.5 ms (a 2 m cooler, ~130 products), yakitori 4.2→4.7, roastery 3.5→4.2, bakery 3.6→4.0 ms; local observations, not an FPS guarantee. The bodega's soda crates, milk-crate seat and market crates still use the painted crate and belong to the shop-interiors pass.
- Kitchens (pass 3), mahjong, tea and sushi bench: the mahjong parlour's five and the tea house's four tables were slabs with tiles painted on top. A shared table now carries a felt-lined wooden tray on a joined four-leg table; the tiles are real runs, each one closed box with seams between tiles and white faces under yellow backs: four walls two high, set in a pinwheel square that stops short of each corner, each player's thirteen tiles standing upright facing their seat with printed marks, and their discards face up in front of them. The minigame finds tables as before. The parlour's tea counter is a fitted cabinet holding three printed red thermos flasks (steel foot and shoulder, cork, gold band) beside poured cups of tea. The sushi chefs' bench is the shared prep bench with a clean board of nigiri pairs and salmon blocks (no flour, unlike the dumpling bench). Two browser checks cover every tile run on the felt, inside the rim and clear of the others (first draft overlapped at the wall corners; caught and fixed), both rooms using the new tables, flasks standing on the worktop and nigiri on the board; Node 1069/1069 and 190 browser checks (shops including mahjong seating, ascii-city, counter staff, kitchen displays, tableware, placement, held items) pass. Reviewed table, tea counter, tea house and sushi bench views. Warmed local medians at 212×66 cells, before→after: mahjong table close 4.6→5.7 ms, parlour wide 4.2→5.0, tea counter 1.6→3.0, tea house 4.0→4.3, sushi 3.4→4.5 ms; local observations, not an FPS guarantee.

- Home electronics (pass4), record console: IDEA and saved home record players now have fitted cabinet boards, joined feet, fabric-fronted speaker baffles and nine supported record sleeves. The turntable has a separate bevelled plinth, metal platter, thin vinyl annulus, spindle through its real centre hole, rotating non-emissive label, knobs, counterweight, cartridge and needle. Play seats the needle on the actual vinyl surface; stop lifts it clear and parks the arm on a rest derived from the same shaft geometry. Retained placement footprints and saved item/track/audio controls. Profiles/materials are cached; near details draw first so depth rejects hidden cabinet panels. Four before/after render-order comparisons preserve all ink and depths exactly. Three new browser checks probe supports and bounds at four rotations, real needle/rest contacts and actual E/track-selection/save/reload audio state. These and the three original furnishing/control checks pass, along with bundle/parser/render-cost checks. Reviewed front, side, low cabinet, playing/stopped and wide night views; thickened the thin tonearm after inspection. Final ABBA177×66 local medians before→after: close7.6–8.6→8.4–8.5ms, side4.7–5.4→6.0–6.1ms, Fern wide3.0–3.7→3.7–8.5ms (the slow after pass also slowed the unchanged loft control). Tails varied substantially, so these are scene observations rather than an FPS guarantee. Pass4 remains open for the remaining electronics and decoration.

- Home decoration (pass4), framed photographs: replaced the rounded plank and surface image with four closed bevelled oak rails sharing mitred corners and a real opening, recessed backing/mat/print/glass layers, four wall-contact pads, retaining tabs and hanging wire/pin hardware. The glass leaves the photograph clear except for sparse filtered glints. Existing saved image data, display proportions, selection and wall placement remain compatible. Two new browser checks probe corner seams, openings, layer depths, clear glass, wall/rail contacts and bounds at all four rotations, plus full-image corner samples/orientation and selection changes. Those and three original furnishing/photo/save/control checks pass; bundle/parser/render-cost checks pass. Reviewed front, oblique, empty/selected, day/night, wide and reverse model views. ABBA177×66 local medians before→after: front3.3–4.7→4.7–9.0ms, side2.8–7.2→3.6ms, wide2.6–2.7→2.8–2.9ms. The unchanged loft control ranged3.1–5.7ms and tails varied substantially; these are scene observations rather than an FPS guarantee. Remaining home electronics, books, plants and keepsakes continue in pass4.

- Home decoration (pass4), palms and terracotta pots: the movable IDEA palm and native Fern/loft/Belle plants now have fitted closed pots, rolled rims, saucers, filled soil, three rooted canes and nine arching fronds with 90 thin solid leaflets. Shared skeleton points join the fronds and leaves; the complete rotated crown fits the existing placement envelope, including flush wall placement. Calm clay and filtered leaf veins replace painted foliage. Cached profiles also draw the real model on Belle balconies at outdoor scale; placing/moving/packing now invalidates that balcony's furniture cache. Three new browser checks probe real pot/soil/root contacts, both sides and normals of every leaflet, outdoor scale, arbitrary saved rotations, real Q/E/F placement and save/reload, and balcony cache refresh. Those and three original furnishing/photo/light/audio controls pass, alongside bundle/parser/home-data/render-cost checks. Reviewed front, close pot/fronds, reverse, wide, balcony and night/mobile views. Earlier ABBA177×66 local medians before→after: full palm2.0–3.2→2.9–5.3ms, pot1.6–1.7→5.4–6.1ms, Fern wide2.2–2.6→3.1–3.2ms, Belle balcony11.1–11.7→12.1–12.8ms; unchanged studio6.7→6.5–6.9ms. A repeat under concurrent host load was substantially noisier (Fern wide4.4–5.0→6.0–6.7ms, balcony16.0–18.2→16.2–19.8ms); these are local observations, not an FPS guarantee. A descriptor-reuse experiment preserved every sampled pixel/depth but showed no measured benefit and was omitted. Pass4 remains open for the remaining electronics, books and keepsakes.
- Kitchens (pass 3), closing audit: rendered all 29 dining rooms (diner, café, noodle bar, bar, tea house, karaoke and the 23 eateries) from the doorway into contact sheets, with no page errors, and scanned every room and kitchen helper for props still painted onto boxes. Every cupboard, worktop, appliance, item of cookware, dish, food display and seat in those rooms is now a built model; home and hotel kitchens were fitted earlier in the pass and cooking feedback landed under pass 29. Pass 3 is checked. Handed on, not done here: the bar's back-bar bottles are the shared `shelves: true` wall painter, which also stocks the store, florist, books, thrift and pharmacy shelves (pass 8 for the bar, pass 7 for the shops); the seafood restaurant's painted fish tank (pass 28); the bodega grill, deli case and soda crates, the gas station's hot-dog roller and the market crates (pass 7); the street-food carts (pass 11/14) and the pier food stall (pass 25); the Thai spirit house and the paper lanterns (pass 8). The shared `KITCHEN_PREP` collision box still stops at the worktop, below its board and food.

- Home decoration (pass4), wall clock: IDEA now sells an oak clock that hangs through the existing wall-placement controls. A turned annular case surrounds a recessed dial, twelve raised indices, separate thin solid hour/minute hands, a central pin and clear glass; back pads meet both the housing and wall. Hands follow game time through noon and midnight. E reads the exact current time, and placement, moving, packing and saves use the existing furnishing data. Cached profiles and hand geometry avoid rebuilding static shapes each frame. Two browser checks probe the actual rim opening, fitted layers, wall contacts, bounds at five rotations, hand thickness/normals/separation and time changes, then buy from IDEA and hang/read/save/reload/pack with normal controls. These and the three original furnishing/photo/light/audio checks pass; bundle/parser/home-data/render-cost checks pass. Reviewed close, oblique, wide, noon, night, IDEA display and mobile views; broadened and flattened the hands after the first review showed broken silhouettes. At177×66 cells, ABBA local medians without→with a clock: close1.3–2.4→3.8–4.0ms, oblique1.0–2.4→2.3–4.0ms, wide1.5–3.9→3.4–4.1ms; unchanged studio6.5–10.4→6.3–7.2ms. This measures the additional object against a bare wall, with variable host tails, not a replacement or an FPS guarantee. Pass4 remains open for radios, books, keepsakes and the remaining audit.

- Home books and usable shelves (pass4): shelf volumes now have separate hard covers, joined spines and recessed page blocks, with filtered paper detail and cached closed profiles. Every board and top of every open home bookcase exposes the actual support plane and clearance, including the extra loft/Belle bookcases. Aim at a board and E or drop puts a fitting inventory item there; aim at a displayed item to take it back. Placement checks footprint, board edges, existing books/items, nearby furniture and the board/ceiling above. Structural back/side panels and boards block taking an item through the case. Food, drinks and other small carried objects can be displayed; furniture uses its existing unpacking controls. The former single fixed row at1.07m is removed. Relative board coordinates save with items, old keepsakes find valid surfaces without losing their inventory data, and the bounded display capacity is48 per home subject to physical space. Layouts cache until contents or room props change. Storage menus use the selected board's placement callback and reset it for ordinary storage. Four new browser checks cover book thickness/joins/recesses/support and bounds at five rotations; native volumes in all four home types; every shelf board's actual rendered support and rejection cases; and real E/drop/save/reload/take/menu controls on an extra Belle shelf. Legacy furniture/keepsake eligibility and malformed placement restoration are checked. These and existing furniture seating/layout, furnishing/photo/light/audio, home layout and held/dropped item checks pass (24 distinct focused browser checks). Bundle/parser, home-data, inventory, geography/save and render-cost checks pass (36 Node checks). Reviewed native books close/front/side/wide/night and populated shelves with the moving-day lamp, apple and duck in desktop/phone/day/night views. Two ABBA177×66 runs varied substantially under shared host load: before→after close6.0–14.6→6.0–15.6ms, wide3.0–6.6→2.5–5.6ms, four keepsakes3.8–8.8→3.3–7.7ms; unchanged hotel control5.2–15.3→4.9–9.2ms. These observations do not establish a performance improvement or an FPS guarantee. This chunk covers physical shelves in player homes; public/shop shelves remain under the scope question and their interior passes. Pass4 stays open for the remaining electronics and decoration audit.

- Bar back bar (pass 8, first chunk, Claude): the bar's back wall no longer uses the shared `shelves: true` painter. A built back bar (`city/bar-backbar.js`) stands on the wall face behind the bartender: a recessed plinth, a timber carcass with a door per bay and stand-off pulls, a stone worktop, full-height stiles from worktop to canopy with boards fitted stile to stile at shared heights and front plane, a smoked mirror back that brightens under the lamp, and a canopy with a diffuser strip along its front whose brightness follows its screen coverage, so it fades rather than flickering far off. 161 turned bottles in four shapes (whisky, wine, gin, liqueur) with coloured glass, labels and caps stand in groups inside each bay, clear of the stiles and below the board above; the worktop keeps a mat of upturned tumblers in each bay. At 19 cm deep it keeps the keeper's 18 cm clearance and an open staff aisle; stools, WC door, jukebox and the keeper's E shop prompt are unchanged. The shared painter still stocks the store, florist, books and similar shelves (pass 7). New `city-bar-backbar` browser test (3): placement against the wall, clerk/aisle/stool/WC access and real E shop; every bottle supported at its base radius, inside its bay, under the board above and apart from its neighbours, every tumbler on its mat; boards meet stile faces exactly, one front plane, all parts inside the collision box. Mutating a board height or bottle depth fails it. Node 1069/1069; shops, bathroom doors/quality, counter staff, placement, freecam, notoriety, gameplay audio and courier browser suites pass. Reviewed wide, close, oblique, from behind the counter, far, and night. Render cost, warmed local medians of 61 frames at 177×60, two ABAB runs, main → this branch: wide 4.7 → 6.5–6.7 ms, oblique 4.6–4.8 → 7.7–8.5, close 2.0–2.1 → 4.6–4.9, night 6.0–8.5 → 6.1–6.2; diner control 4.2–4.7 → 4.0–4.4. Long bevelled boards were split per bay and hidden carcass parts use the column-clipped box after a first draft cost twice as much obliquely. Local observations, not an FPS guarantee.
- Browser reliability: #206's full CI passed Node and three browser shards; the remaining shard failed the older roof traversal check because it assumed1.5s of wall time always advances enough simulation time. The game deliberately caps simulation steps at50ms. The real-keyboard test now waits for the neighbour roof, street transition, landing, sprint takeoff position and simulated jump interval; it also chooses a neighbour whose actual roof matches the map height and clears carried motion between teleports. All traversal, fall-damage, short-jump and fire-escape assertions remain. The focused check passes normally and with90ms of deliberate rendering work per frame.

- Restaurant interiors (pass8), bar back bar: replaced the bar's painted, glowing shelf glyphs with a fitted wall-backed cabinet, three supported boards, joined brass guards/brackets/handles,45 closed shaped bottles in three profiles and12 actual hollow tumblers. Board tops define every vessel base and guard post. Cabinet depth leaves the existing staff aisle intact; the top clears the sign, and the collision bounds include the handles without entering the wall. Bottles and hardware use room light; dark cup interiors and lighter rims make the openings readable. Profiles/materials cache at construction, and counters/people draw first to reject hidden shelf pixels. Four representative views match every character, colour and depth before/after that draw-order change. Two new browser checks probe actual support contacts, closed caps, open glass mouths, normals, emission, bracket/guard contacts and every part's bounds, then exercise real E shopping. The existing counter-staff and all-shop/bathroom-route sweeps pass; bundle/parser/render-cost checks pass. Reviewed close, wide, oblique, night, phone and glass openings from above. ABBA177×66 local medians before→after: wide5.8–9.0→6.7–6.9ms, customer counter2.6–2.8→8.0–8.1ms, oblique4.1–4.2→7.3–15.5ms, bottle close0.9→6.5–12.9ms; unchanged café control5.6–5.8→6.3–6.6ms. Detailed close views cost more than the formerly painted wall, and host tails varied; these are local observations rather than an FPS guarantee. Pass8 remains open for lanterns, the Thai spirit house and the remaining interior audit.

- Restaurant interiors (pass8), native hanging paper lanterns: the eight existing yakitori/izakaya/sushi drum props now use a rounded hollow paper shell, separate fitted upper/lower hoops, a cross frame, socket, warm glass bulb, cord and ceiling rose. The bulb neck seats into the socket; the frame meets its hoop and cord; every fixture reaches its room's actual ceiling. Hardware stays unlit while paper and bulb glow; filtered rib shadows and fine fibres retain a quiet surface at distance. Closed profiles cache at construction. Two new browser checks probe open shell axes, real2.5mm wall thickness/normals, hoop/paper overlap, bulb/socket/cord/mount/frame contacts, full rendered bounds, emission separation and all eight native fixtures; floor clearance stays unchanged. Those, the all-shop/bathroom-route sweep and bundle/parser/render-cost checks pass. Reviewed front/reverse, whole support, above/below, wide, night and phone views; strengthened filtered folds/glow after the first view read too smooth. Representative ABBA177×66 medians before→after: close8.3–9.1→7.2–17.9ms, underside2.1→9.7–10.2ms, yakitori wide5.8–6.1→5.5–6.0ms, sushi wide4.5–4.8→4.1ms; unchanged pho control4.7–5.4→4.5–4.8ms. Hollow close-ups add cost and host tails varied; these are scene observations, not an FPS guarantee. Painted ceiling lantern patterns and the remaining restaurant props still belong to the pass8 audit.

- Restaurant interiors (pass8), Thai spirit house: replaced the stacked roof blocks and floating flower glyphs with a closed pitched roof built from four panels sharing one folded eave profile, fitted front/back gables, gold moulding/ridge/finial, framed open chamber, tiny altar/figurine, hollow offering cups, bevelled tray and curved pedestal. Seven shared garland points define the rope and42 shaped marigold core/petal solids; both end pins penetrate the actual tray face. Cached convex planes, lathe profiles and materials keep construction out of the render loop. Two new browser checks probe every roof seam and gable/roof contact, pedestal/tray/floor/post/altar/figurine/cup supports, cup openings, rendered bounds, garland/pin attachments, flower normals/emission and native wall clearance. Those, the all-shop/bathroom-route sweep and bundle/parser/render-cost checks pass. Reviewed full/front/side/oblique, roof, garland, wide, night and phone views; widened the gold moulding after inspection showed broken thin strokes at normal distance. Final ABBA177×66 local medians before→after: front1.5–2.7→3.5–4.1ms, oblique1.3–1.8→3.7–4.3ms, garland1.3–3.2→4.4ms, wide2.7–4.0→3.0–3.1ms; unchanged pho control5.1–5.4→4.8–5.1ms. Detail close-ups add cost and host tails varied; these are local observations rather than an FPS guarantee. Pass8 remains open for the ceiling lantern patterns, izakaya sake wall and remaining restaurant audit.

- Restaurant interiors (pass8), mirrored bar integration (#210): retained #208's open shelves as the default and use Claude's mirrored cabinet for CYAN-lit bars. Both models remain separate constructors, appended after counters/people for depth rejection; all #209 lantern and #211 shrine work is preserved. Corrected the mirrored cabinet depth to .18m and its actual parent bounds to include .216m handle projection, retaining the keeper's .18m clearance. Upturned tumblers now have a real downward opening, inner wall and closed base. Cached bottle/glass profiles, bevel planes and timber materials replace per-draw construction; the bounding-cylinder reject includes the full lathe's numerical tolerance. Five mirrored-bar checks cover support/packing/joins, actual bounds including rods at cardinal and oblique angles, hollow cups and thousands of optimized-versus-reference ray probes; both open-bar tests, the six-room counter-staff check, all-shop/bathroom routes and seven bundle/parser/render-cost checks pass. Reviewed wide/counter/oblique/bottle/night/phone views with no page errors. Four seeded default-bar views preserve every character, foreground, background and depth value from main. The smoked mirror is a local material rather than a world reflection. Final ABBA177×66 local medians current-open→mirrored: wide7.1–12.8→7.0–10.8ms, counter8.3–17.2→6.7–6.8ms, oblique10.6–14.7→6.2–7.4ms, bottle close6.3–6.6→6.0–6.7ms; unchanged cafe control6.4–6.6→6.4–7.6ms. Host-load tails varied; these scene observations do not establish an FPS improvement. Full CI for #207–#209 and #211 is green. Pass8 remains open for ceiling lantern patterns, izakaya sake displays and the restaurant audit.

- Restaurant interiors (pass8), ceiling lantern layouts: removed flat glowing O marks from every lantern-style ceiling. Yakitori/izakaya/sushi retain their eight authored feature lanterns; noodle bar, tea house, noodle house, jade shop and herbalist now have32 smaller physical paper lanterns, fitted once when the room is built. Each has the closed thin shell, separate hoops, socket/bulb, cord and ceiling rose from #209. Their lower edges are2.412m high; full surrounds clear wall/door cells, bathroom regions and tall prop bounds. Three lantern browser checks cover real geometry and native/layout fixtures in all eight rooms, including unchanged floor collision and ray-probed ceiling contacts; the all-shop/bathroom-route sweep, bundle freshness, duplicate-function, parse and render-cost checks pass. Reviewed all five new rooms plus the authored yakitori layout, close/reverse/above/below, night and phone views with no page errors. - Restaurant interiors (pass8), izakaya sake wall: replaced the painted bottle bands with two bevelled timber boards, six wall-mounted support plates with joined arms/braces, continuous front guards and42 sealed bottles in four curved profiles. Bottle feet and guard posts derive from the board tops; end guard posts connect to the full rail span. All fitting faces stay on/inside the actual wall plane, with the original noren, sign and staff aisle retained. Closed profiles/materials prepare once; bottles use the verified bounding-cylinder lathe reject. Two new browser checks probe real cap/base surfaces, whole-foot support, packing, headroom, rendered bounds, wall/board/bracket/brace/guard contacts, nonemissive labels, close seals versus distant filtered ink, and real E bartender service. Those, all-shop/bathroom routes, bundle/parser/duplicate-function and render-cost checks pass. Reviewed wide/front/side/end/above/below/labels, an oblique underside view, night and phone views. Increased the printed-label filter range after close views initially read as blank paper. Final ABBA177×66 local medians before→after: wide7.3–15.4→9.6–14.4ms, front1.6–3.7→7.0–11.4ms, oblique4.1–12.0→8.7–14.0ms, underside1.6–2.7→3.9–6.8ms; unchanged pho control5.7–7.2→5.0–8.3ms. Physical close-ups add cost and host-load variance is substantial; these are scene observations, not an FPS guarantee. #210 full CI is green; ceiling-layout CI continues. Pass8 remains open for the remaining restaurant audit.

- Shop interiors (pass 7, Claude), shelf islands: the 'S' cells in the store, books, grocer, hardware, pharmacy, video, liquor, gas, bodega, drugstore and toy shop grids were wall blocks with goods painted on their faces. At room build (`city/shop-gondolas.js`, one line in `makeRoom`) each rectangle of them becomes floor with a built gondola standing on the old footprint: a recessed plinth, a pegboard spine, four steel shelves on both long faces at shared heights, full-depth end uprights and a top cap. Stock is solid facings sized for the shop (grocery and pharmacy packs with label bands, hardware bins with open tops, toy boxes, book and tape spines in pressed runs with a line between spines, wine as one turned bottle per row front), each on its shelf and below the one above, with the odd sold-out gap. The collision box is the old block's, so routes and clerk access are unchanged; the dead 'S' branches in the shop painters and `roomWall` are gone. The placement test's drugstore viewpoint sat inside an island and now moves to a free spot. New `city-shop-gondolas` browser test (2): every block becomes one gondola on exactly its old cells with no 'S' left, enough stock, every part inside the footprint and under the top, every facing on a shelf top and under the next shelf, four shelves per gondola. Lifting a facing off its shelf fails it. Node 1069/1069 and the full npm suite pass. Render cost, warmed medians of 61 frames at 177×60 looking down an aisle, two ABAB runs, main → this branch: store 3.3–3.6 → 4.6–4.7 ms, books 4.1–4.3 → 4.4–4.5, grocer 3.2–3.7 → 4.6–5.0, hardware 3.6–5.5 → 5.0–5.7, pharmacy 2.2 → 3.5, video 3.3–3.9 → 5.3–5.5, liquor 2.8–2.9 → 5.1–5.4, gas 4.2–4.4 → 5.6–6.2, bodega 3.6–4.0 → 6.2–6.8, drugstore 3.6–4.3 → 5.8–6.2, toys 3.6–3.9 → 4.9–5.1. A first draft with a box per spine cost 46 ms in the video shop; spine runs, pre-built facings and nearest-first drawing brought it down. Local observations, not an FPS guarantee.

- Shop interiors (pass 7, Claude), back-wall shelves: the shared `shelves: true` wall painter (rows of glyphs on the back wall of the store, florist, drugstore and thrift shop) is gone. `def.shelves` is now a spec, and `makeRoom` stands a built wall unit on the back wall face from it (`SHOP_WALL_SHELVES` in `city/shop-gondolas.js`): a back panel, uprights flush with the unit's ends and one every metre, four shelves per bay fitted upright face to upright face, a set-back plinth under each bay and a top cap, steel for the store and drugstore, timber for the florist and thrift shop. It shares the islands' stock code, with two new kinds: the florist's terracotta pots with leaves and blooms, and the thrift shop's bric-a-brac of boxes and turned jugs. At 20 cm deep it leaves the clerk 20 cm and keeps the store's back aisle open; the thrift unit stops short of the fitting room, and the antiques and tailor trades in the same room keep their own walls. The books room only carried the flag; its painted bookcase walls are a later chunk. New `city-shop-wall-shelves` browser test (2): one unit per shop on the wall face with wall behind its whole length, the clerk and aisle clear, no shelves for antiques or tailor, no `shelves: true` left; every part inside the unit, shelves meeting upright faces at the back panel and front, every facing on a shelf top, under the shelf above, inside its bay and its depth. Widening the bays fails it. Render cost, warmed medians of 61 frames at 177×60, two ABAB runs, #223 → this branch: store 4.0–5.2 → 4.7–5.0 ms, florist 4.2–4.4 → 6.1–6.3, florist oblique 3.3–6.1 → 5.4–6.8, drugstore 1.8 → 3.0–3.6, thrift 2.4–2.7 → 4.0–4.1, thrift oblique 2.7–3.9 → 4.3. Local observations, not an FPS guarantee.

- Shop interiors (pass 7, Claude), bookshop bookcases: the books room's `booksWall` painter (spines painted on every wall to 2.6 m) is gone. Three built timber bookcases stand on the back wall and both side walls, 2.15 m tall with five shelves, from the same wall unit as the other shops, which now takes a wall side, its own levels and height, and a list of runs in `def.shelves`. The side cases butt against the front of the back case, and the right-hand run stops at 4.3 m, short of the till. Book spines stand in pressed runs, a line between spines and a title band on each. The `city-shop-wall-shelves` test now checks every unit in its own frame: the collision box from the wall face to its depth along its whole run, wall behind it, no overlap between runs, the clerk clear, and in the bookshop the aisles beside each case open; the stock checks run on all five rooms. Moving the left case 3 cm off the wall fails both tests. Render cost, warmed medians of 61 frames at 177×60, two ABAB runs, #224 → this branch: books wide 5.0–7.8 → 4.5–5.4 ms, books looking left 5.0–6.0 → 4.3–4.4, books corner 5.5–8.7 → 4.8–4.9, store control 4.3–4.9 → 4.5–4.6. The cases hide the floor and wall behind them, which balances their own cost. Local observations, not an FPS guarantee.
- Shop fridge walls (pass 7, Claude): the shared `fridgeWall` painter drew lit glass-door fridges with glyph stock onto walls in the grocer, deli, liquor store (left wall and the beer cave), corner store and gas station. Those walls now carry built runs of the pass 3 glass-door cooler (`city/shop-fridges.js`), 36 cm deep on their wall faces so the aisles in front stay walkable: the grocer's dairy run and back drinks run (the back run owns the corner), the deli's drinks, the liquor store's beer fridges under their sign and across the cave's back wall (its cases moved in front, the side walls left as frosted insulated panels), the corner store's right wall and the gas station's back wall. `KITCHEN_DRINKS_COOLER` gains a header label and a stock option: dairy runs stock milk cartons and yoghurt pots, beer runs brown and green bottles and cans. For runs this long the cooler's full-width rails, shelves, header, grille and back are now column-clipped boxes, and every product's box is built once and only moved each frame; together these took a 7 m pair of runs from +9 ms to about +2.5 ms. The gas station's occasional wet-floor sign moved out of the fridge run, and the unused painter is removed. New `city-shop-fridges` browser test (2): each shop has its runs and labels, backs on its wall along the whole run, someone can stand in front of every door, nothing else stands inside a run, stock is full, the dairy run holds cartons and pots, and the old wall painters no longer draw fridges. The first drafts' overlaps with the grocer's baskets, the gas station's coffee counter and its wet-floor sign were caught and fixed. The existing cooler test now accepts the plain-box shelves as supports. Node 1069/1069; all-shop routes and cooler suites pass. Reviewed the grocer dairy and aisle, the liquor cave and the gas station back wall. Render cost, warmed medians of 61 frames at 177×60, two ABAB runs, main → branch: grocer aisle 3.6–3.8 → 5.9–6.2 ms, grocer door 3.7–4.2 → 5.4–5.8, store 5.0–5.6 → 5.2–5.5, gas 4.2–4.3 → 5.7–5.8, liquor 3.7–4.0 → 6.1–6.3, deli 7.8–8.0 → 9.3–9.5. Local observations, not an FPS guarantee.
- Shop display cases (pass 7, first chunk, Claude): the shared `glassCase` painted its goods as glyphs on the glass top and front of a two-box case. It is now one built model (`city/shop-display-cases.js`): a plinth, a timber or steel cabinet with door panels on the staff side, a chrome slab, a velvet bed, four chrome uprights and top rails joined at the corners, and clear glass that only writes narrow filtered glints, so the goods behind stay visible from every side. `caseGoods` names what a case holds, each kind with its own small solid pieces laid in two rows on the bed: rings, gems and pendants (pawn, jeweller), watches on straps (pawn), skate wheels, trucks and bearings (skate), pipes, lighters and tins (tobacco), roots, herb bundles and dishes (herbs), body jewellery studs, rings and barbells (tattoo), trinkets (antiques), and buns and rolls (the airport café). Row spacing follows each kind's widest piece turned any way. The two deli cases, whose meats and salads were also painted on the glass, use the existing built `KITCHEN_FOOD_CASE` deli kind. Collision boxes are unchanged. New `city-shop-display-cases` browser test (2): the eleven cases in seven shops each hold at least their listed goods, every piece inside the bed and resting on it, the front glass clear in 17 of 20 sampled cells, collision unchanged; both delis use the food case; no case blocks a clerk. Moving the rows outside the bed fails it. Node 1069/1069; all-shop routes, kitchen displays and the aircraft/terminal suite pass. Reviewed jeweller, pawn, skate, tobacco and deli cases close and wide. Render cost: a first draft with bevelled plane-set slabs cost up to 10 ms extra close up; the large slabs are now column-clipped boxes. Warmed medians of 61 frames at 177×60, three ABAB runs, main → branch: jeweller wide 3.1–4.8 → 3.5–3.9 ms, jeweller close 2.6–4.3 → 2.8–3.1, pawn 3.8–4.0 → 4.8–6.9, tobacco close 3.2–3.3 → 3.6–4.0, skate 3.9–4.0 → 4.5–5.2. Local observations, not an FPS guarantee.
