- Restaurant lamps and parlour chairs (pass 8, Claude): the remaining painted-drum lamps and line-rod chairs in the restaurant files are built in `city/restaurant-lamps.js`. The seven round red lanterns in the dumpling house, dim sum hall and noodle house (the eight akachochin/sushi lanterns were done in #209) are a globe on filtered vertical ribs between flared gold caps, lit from inside and brightest where you look straight through, with a gold knot and fringed tassel below. The kissaten's three Tiffany lamps are domes of leaded coloured glass in segments and bands under a brass cap, the bulb glowing through the open rim from below. The pizzeria's three and the chicken shop's two heat lamps are enamelled shades with the bulb and its glow under the rim. Every lamp hangs on a cord from a ceiling rose. The ice-cream parlour's wire chairs have a padded round seat on a wire ring, four splayed legs from the floor to the seat ring, a low ring exactly where the legs pass, and a round hoop back on two posts; patrons still sit on them at the same height. New `city-restaurant-lamps` browser test (2): every lamp in the six rooms reaches its ceiling, has no gap down its stack, stays above 1.6 m and on one axis; every chair leg stands on the floor and meets the seat ring, both rings pass through the legs, the hoop is above the seat, seat height unchanged and patrons seated. The first draft's leg ring missed the legs; restoring it fails the test. All-shop routes, hot line, native dining fittings and placement pass. Reviewed lanterns, Tiffany lamps, heat lamps and chairs. Render cost, warmed medians of 61 frames at 177×60, two ABAB runs, main → branch: dumplings 4.7–5.0 → 5.0–5.3 ms, dim sum 5.4 → 6.2–7.8, kissaten 5.8–5.9 → 5.5–5.7, pizzeria 3.9–4.7 → 3.6–4.0, ice cream 6.3–6.5 → 6.6–7.0, chicken 5.5–6.1 → 5.6–6.1. Local observations, not an FPS guarantee.

- Restaurant wall fixtures, second set (pass 8, Claude): the ice-cream parlour's painted 'V' cone rack and the burger joint's painted pass are built in `city/eatery-wall-fixtures.js`. The cone rack is two steel rails on wall plates with six wire rings each; in every ring a stack of four turned wafer cones (hollow, a pressed diamond grid filtered with distance) rests with the bottom cone's 2.8 cm waist on the ring's 2.7 cm hole, and the lower row's stacks finish below the upper row's tips. The burger pass keeps the hatch opening in the wall painter (now a dim kitchen with the griddle glowing low instead of '~' noise) and builds the fixture in front of it: a steel frame standing on a steel pass shelf on two brackets, a heat-lamp hood in front of the frame with two red lamps glowing under it, and a ticket rail above the hood with four order tickets; it is narrowed to clear the shake machine and soda fountain. Both keep the clerk's 18 cm clearance. New `city-eatery-wall-fixtures` browser test (2): every part in front of the wall, cone waist against ring hole, stacks clear of the row above, holders on the rails, painted cones gone; pass frame standing on the shelf, hood clear of the frame, lamps between shelf and hood, tickets above the hood, machines clear, kitchen seen through the opening, the clerk's E shop prompt. It caught shelf brackets buried 2 mm in the wall in the first draft; a wider ring fails it. All-shop routes, drinks, displays and counter staff pass. Reviewed the rack front and close, and the pass from the counter and close. Render cost, warmed medians of 61 frames at 177×60, two ABAB runs, main → branch: cone rack 2.3–2.5 → 2.9–3.1 ms, ice-cream door 3.6–3.8 → 4.3–4.4, burger pass 4.3–5.9 → 4.6–4.7, burger door 4.4–4.6 → 4.7–4.9. Local observations, not an FPS guarantee.

- Restaurant wall fixtures, first set (pass 8, Claude): three objects that were painted onto restaurant walls are built in `city/dining-wall-fixtures.js`. The pho shop's turning wall fans are two oscillating fans on the side walls: a wall plate and arm up to a pivot post, a motor housing, a white rim with back and front grilles of concentric wire rings (radial spokes that thin fell back to line glyphs a few metres off), three blue blades turning on a spinner well inside the rim, and a badge; each head sweeps 0.55 rad either side of straight out. The kissaten's painted clock is one wall clock using the home `HOME_WALL_CLOCK` fixture unchanged (turned rim, dial, raised indices, game-time hands under glass), standing off the side wall by its own depth. The roastery's painted foliage is four trailing plants: a terracotta pot with soil on a wall bracket (plate, arm, brace) and three vines of leaves spilling over the pot's front rim and falling back toward the wall. Wall painters now leave plain wall where each fixture was. New `city-dining-wall-fixtures` browser test (3): fan plates on the wall face, no fan part reaching into the wall or ceiling at any of 40 sampled times across a sweep, blades moving, sweep limit, blade tips inside the cage; the clock's back on the wall face and its hands following `tod`; plant pots clear of the wall, every part in front of it and above 0.9 m, leaves below the pot top, nothing else where the vines hang; each old painted spot is plain wall. Fan pivot or pot mutations into the wall fail it. All-shop routes, coffee and native dining fittings pass. Reviewed the fans close and from a table, the clock, and the plants. Render cost, warmed medians of 61 frames at 177×60, two ABAB runs, main → branch: pho fan view 2.3–3.5 → 2.3–2.5 ms, pho door 4.6–4.7 → 4.4–5.3, kissaten clock 1.8–3.3 → 2.6–3.0, roastery plants 1.7–1.9 → 2.5, roastery door 5.6–5.9 → 5.6–5.9. Local observations, not an FPS guarantee.

- Karaoke stage, speakers and screen (pass 8, Claude): the stage was a painted box, the PA speakers boxes with rings painted on the front, and the lyrics were painted straight onto the back wall. `city/karaoke-stage.js` builds a platform from four pieces that meet without sharing a face (a steel front nosing over a front board with a strip light in the room's neon, a black-carpeted deck and a ply riser behind them), keeping the old footprint and the singer standing on the deck. PA cabinets have rounded edges, steel corner caps, a perforated steel grille proud of the front with a badge, a top carrying handle and rubber feet. A floor wedge monitor sits on the stage front with its sloped grille turned up to the singer. The lyrics moved to a wall-mounted screen on brackets: an unlit case and a separate picture with a slow colour wash, letters sized so every line fits inside the bezel, the sung part lighting magenta. The wall painter keeps only the glitter; the unused shared `SPEAKER` helper is removed (karaoke was its only caller). New `city-karaoke-stage` browser test (3): stage pieces never overlap and the deck is the top everywhere, footprint unchanged, singer on the deck, wedge on the stage front clear of the singer; speakers on the floor beside the stage, screen backed onto the wall above the stage, every lyric line fits, wall no longer paints the screen; the sung part shows magenta mid-line and not before, every seat reachable. Mutating the riser back to full depth or the letter width fails it. All-shop routes and gameplay audio pass. Reviewed wide, close, side and monitor views. Render cost, warmed medians of 61 frames at 177×60, two ABAB runs, main → branch: wide 2.5–3.5 → 2.7, close 2.3–2.6 → 2.9–3.0, side 2.7 → 3.0, door 2.5–2.7 → 2.8–3.0 ms. Local observations, not an FPS guarantee.
- Sushi island counter (pass 8, Claude): the kaiten island was four painted boxes with moving dashes on top. `city/sushi-island.js` builds each side from one set of measurements: a pale hinoki carcass with battened outer fascia on a recessed plinth, an eating ledge outside the belt and a serving ledge inside it, steel guide rails each side and a slatted steel belt whose slats slide round at the plates' own speed. Long sides own the corners; rails, belt and ledges meet face to face with no overlaps. Each of the 16 seats has a hot-water tap (steel body, red push button, spout over the ledge) and a lidded green tea canister on the outer ledge in front of its stool. Collision boxes are exactly the four boxes it replaces; plates, chefs, stools and service are unchanged. New `city-sushi-island` browser test (2): no slab overlaps any other, every sampled point of the band's footprint has exactly one top piece (0 gaps), footprints match the old boxes, painted boxes gone; plates on the belt at every 5 cm of the loop, taps and canisters fully on the outer ledge, a stool in front of each seat, customer lane open and the chef's E shop prompt. A corner-seat overhang the test caught in the first draft is fixed. Kitchen displays, tableware, all-shop routes and lanterns pass. Reviewed wide, corner, close and end-on. Render cost, warmed medians of 61 frames at 177×60, two ABAB runs, main → branch: wide 5.1–5.3 → 6.2 ms, corner 6.2–6.3 → 7.5–7.8, close 5.2–5.4 → 6.3–6.4, end 1.8–1.9 → 2.2–4.1. Local observations, not an FPS guarantee.

- Dim sum live seafood tank (pass 8, Claude): the banquet hall's tank by the door was two painted boxes with '<' '>' fish and 'o' bubbles on the glass. `city/seafood-tank.js` builds it against the wall: a steel cabinet with two doors and stand-off pulls, steel rails and front corner posts framing clear glass (sparse filtered glints only), a hood with its lamp under the front lip, a mottled gravel bed, a blue-lit back panel, end panes that show the water edge-on from inside and stay clear from outside, and a rippled water surface that only shows where the ripples catch the light. Three mottled groupers (body, wagging tail, dorsal fin, eyes) swim ellipses along the tank so they turn through the cross-tank direction instead of flipping; two dark live lobsters lie on the gravel with segmented tails, fans, arms, claws with yellow rubber bands, walking legs down to the gravel and swaying feelers; bubbles rise from an air stone in the back corner. Same footprint and collision as before. New `city-seafood-tank` browser test (3): wall placement, clear front lane and footprint, painted box gone, front glass clear in 34+ of 40 sampled cells; every swimming/lobster/bubble part inside the glass and between gravel and water at 109 sampled times; fish heading follows the path without flips. Mutating the swim loop or a lobster position fails it. All-shop routes, lanterns, native dining fittings, steamers, tableware and ledges pass. Reviewed close, wide, oblique and night. Render cost, warmed medians of 61 frames at 177×60, two ABAB runs, main → branch: tank close 1.7–1.8 → 3.8 ms, wide 3.9–4.1 → 4.8–5.0, oblique 2.9–4.4 → 3.9–4.0, door 4.9–5.1 → 5.0. Local observations, not an FPS guarantee.
- Home electronics (pass4), fitted televisions: replaced the front-face cutout case with four joined closed bezel pieces, actual screen recess/jambs and a fitted back plate. Four flat pads meet the supporting cabinet; four sloping legs join the pads and lower frame. Added a seated power button and filtered rear vent marks while retaining the original dimensions and animated programmes. Static planes, bounds and materials prepare once; hotel rooms construct their TV once with their own room state, rather than rebuilding it each render. Three browser checks probe bezel openings/seams/front/back contacts, screen depth, all rendered bounds at five orientations and two scales, actual native cabinet support in all four homes and three hotel tiers, case/screen material separation, real E toggles and zero render-time TV construction. Deliberately raising the pads or narrowing a jamb makes the focused geometry check fail. Five existing TV animation, home doorway, hotel amenity/window and motel layout checks pass; bundle freshness/duplicate-function, classic-script parse and render-cost checks pass. Reviewed close/front/wide/oblique/back/underside/night/phone views and all seven native rooms. ABAB177×66 local medians main→after: front3.3–3.6→3.6–6.4ms, oblique3.5–4.0→3.2–4.9ms, underside4.5–4.6→4.9–6.1ms, wide2.1→2.1–2.4ms, native studio4.9–5.0→4.9–5.2ms, native hotel suite3.7–3.8→3.8–3.9ms; unchanged cafe control5.5–5.6→6.0–6.3ms. Extra support geometry can add close-view cost and host tails vary; these observations do not establish an FPS improvement. No shared clock/worktop/ellipsoid/KITCHEN helper or Claude-owned source changed. Pass4 remains open for the remaining electronics/decor audit, followed by pass5 homes and pass6 hotels.

- Shop interiors (pass 7, Claude), grocer floor: the grocer's painted boxes are built (`city/grocer-produce.js`). Produce stands are three tiers of slatted timber crates stepping up away from the door, each tier on its own riser, each crate packed with one of nine kinds of fruit or veg in square layers, every upper piece resting in the hollow of four below (the pass 3 juice-bar crate's layout and shading, at grocer sizes), and a white price card clipped to every crate front. The watermelon bin has a false floor with striped melons packed on it; the basket stack is five plastic baskets resting one on another with the top one's handles folded onto its rim; the stockroom's banana crate is the pass 3 crate; the staff's upturned crate now matches the seat on it. The checkout is a laminate counter on a set-back plinth with the belt running toward the till in a channel between steel rails, the weighing plate flush with the top, the till on its post with its screen to the cashier and the card reader on the customers' side. The FRUIT grocer's third stand had been commented out by a mid-line comment since it was written; it now stands. New `city-grocer-produce` browser test (2): stands and crates on their risers stepping up away from the door, every piece inside its crate walls and on the floor or in a hollow of four, a card on every crate front, all parts inside the stand; melons inside the bin, baskets resting, handles on the rim; till and reader on their sides with nothing through the counter top; nothing overlapping the new models, and the aisles between and around them free. Moving the melon bin into the aisle or spacing the fruit layers wrongly fails it. Render cost, warmed medians of 61 frames at 177×60, two ABAB runs, before → after: stands 3.6 → 5.8–6.1 ms, stand close up 3.9–6.1 → 5.2–5.6, checkout 2.1–2.2 → 2.5–4.2, fruit shop 4.4–4.5 → 5.4, from the door 6.0–6.5 → 7.4–8.0. Local observations, not an FPS guarantee.
- Home decoration (pass4), shelf keepsakes: displayed rubber ducks and ordinary snow globes now use small 3D models instead of camera-facing inventory pictures. Ducks have a flat supported belly, joined rounded body/head/tail/beak and seated eyes. Globes have a turned timber base, supported snow/trunk/tree and a closed thin glass shell rendered through sparse filtered highlights; their contents remain visible. Both use the exact existing reserved shelf radius/height and face out from the board, preserving placement, saves and taking. Shapes/materials cache per inventory object, shifted profiles update only when shelf height changes, and offscreen models cull before drawing. Two focused browser checks probe actual support, all part bounds at three rotations, duck joins/normals, glass thickness/transparency, globe content support/containment, cache reuse and real E pickup from a populated shelf. Raising the duck base deliberately makes the focused check fail. Both existing whole-board placement/headroom/book/edge/save/drop/take checks and home-data/bundle/duplicate-function/parse/render-cost checks pass. Reviewed close, wide, oblique, reverse, above, night and phone views; corrected an eye-name collision that had made glass opaque and adjusted its edge coverage after inspection. Final ABAB177×66 local medians main→after: duck close3.9–7.4→4.1–7.3ms, globe close2.1–2.2→3.0ms, wide3.0–3.1→3.1–3.2ms, oblique3.8–3.9→4.1ms; unchanged suite control3.8–4.0→4.1ms. The globe adds close-view cost, and host-load variance affects tails; these are scene observations rather than an FPS improvement claim. #225 television full CI is green. Other carried/displayed item families retain the common inventory renderer for pass27; pass4 remains open for the remaining decoration audit.

- Shop interiors (pass 7, Claude), corner shops: the gas station and the bodega have no painted boxes left (`city/corner-shops.js`). A shared shop counter (set-back plinth, laminate carcass, a panel per metre on the customers' face, an overhanging top) takes any direction; the gas station has three (the till counter along the aisle, coffee and hot food), the bodega one. On the gas counter: a till on its drawer with its screen to the clerk, the pump console with its six lit buttons (green while that pump fills) facing the clerk, a turned glass jar of jerky that stays clear through its sides, the restroom key on its hubcap. A nested stack of paper cups by the coffee; a chest freezer with its lid, handle and ICE decal; an ATM with a hooded screen, keypad shelf, card light and cash slot; a peg stand of crisps with every bag puffed on its hook; a wire newspaper rack of three trays; the wet-floor sign is a turned yellow cone. The oil shelf under the windows is a front-wall run of the shop wall unit, with quarts and screenwash jugs. In the bodega the counter carries a steel-framed plexiglass screen with a pass-through under the middle pane; the grill is the pass 3 flat-top (the deli case moves 5 cm to clear it), and the soda and milk crates are the pass 3 crates, the top soda crate open with its bottles. New `city-corner-shops` browser test (2): three counters, everything on a counter standing on its top, till and console to the clerk, ATM to the shop, every part inside its model's box, bags clear of the base, papers on trays, no overlaps, no `BX(` left in either def, aisles free; the screen on the counter between clerk and shop, its panes clear, the pass-through there. Sinking the jar or the screen 2 to 5 cm fails it; the first run caught the ATM keypad and the paper trays standing outside their boxes. Render cost, warmed medians of 61 frames at 177×60, two ABAB runs, #228 → this branch: gas counter 3.7–4.1 → 5.0–5.2 ms, coffee 3.3 → 3.7–3.8, crisps 7.0–7.9 → 8.0–8.7, from the door 6.4–6.7 → 7.5–7.7, front 3.5–4.2 → 4.0–4.1; bodega counter 2.6–4.1 → 3.1–3.2, door 5.5–6.9 → 5.6–5.8, crates 4.1–4.3 → 5.7–5.8, grill 3.7–3.9 → 4.3–4.4. Local observations, not an FPS guarantee.

- Shop interiors (pass 7, Claude), record shop, tattoo parlour, hardware and pharmacy: no painted boxes left in these four (`city/record-shop.js`, `city/shop-fittings.js`). Record shop: two LP bins, timber cabinets on plinths with a trough let into the top, the LPs standing in it in two rows of runs with every sleeve's edge its own colour, white divider cards with their letters standing in the gaps between runs; the back wall's painted sleeves are 81 sleeves stood face-out on three timber ledges behind their lips, each with its disc, stripes or letter; CDs and tapes are a right-wall run of the shop wall unit; the counter, till and listening cabinet are the shared builders. Tattoo parlour: the till on the glass case, portfolios and binders as stacks lying flat and resting on what they're on, the low table a pedestal table, the rug a model. Hardware: the counter, a key cutter with its wheel turning on edge on a spindle from the motor housing, the till, the bulkhead over the garden opening, the lumber stood on end against the wall (it had stood 30 cm off it), six turns of garden hose resting one on another, four compost bags lying one on another. Pharmacy: the dispensary counter, a blood-pressure machine with its cuff ring at arm height facing its seat (now a stool), the consultation desk with a modesty panel and a monitor facing the stool. New `city-specialist-shops` browser test (3): LP runs on the cabinet top inside the trough, cards between runs, face-out sleeves on their ledges, clear of the wall and lip and under the next ledge; tattoo till and stacks resting, the table and rug there; hardware and pharmacy things on what they stand on, hose turns and bags resting, boards against the wall, the BP machine facing its seat, no `BX(` left, aisles free. Moving the face-out sleeves into the wall, widening the bin rows or lifting a binder fails it; the first run caught the face-out wall drawing at absolute coordinates and the lumber off the wall. `city-shop-wall-shelves` covers the CD run. Render cost, warmed medians of 61 frames at 177×60, two ABAB runs, #229 → this branch: records wide 3.6–5.3 → 4.3–4.8 ms, bin 2.7–2.8 → 4.1–4.7, back wall 2.1 → 2.3, CDs 4.7–4.8 → 4.2; tattoo lounge 3.4–4.3 → 3.4–3.7, door 4.0–4.2 → 4.7–6.1; hardware counter and garden within noise of 3–7 ms (the hose, isolated, about 0.5 ms); pharmacy door 3.9–6.5 → 4.1–7.0, BP machine 1.3–2.3 → 2.4–2.5. Local observations, not an FPS guarantee.

- Shop interiors (pass 7, Claude), counters and fittings in ten more rooms: the toy shop, sports shop, video store, liquor store, phone shop, pawn shop, the thrift room's thrift, antiques and tailor trades, and the herbalist have no painted boxes left. Their counters are the shared shop counter (the toy shop's panels in four colours), the liquor store's screen is the plexiglass screen with its pass-through, tills face the clerk. New builders in `city/shop-fittings.js`: a rubber mat (under the sports shop's bike and the pawn shop's tellies), the video store's steel returns box with its hooded slot and label, the phone shop's queue-ticket screen on a pole and its repair bench (the small desk), a chest of drawers for the antiques shop moved back onto the wall (it had stood 10 cm off it), the tailor's sewing machine on its table with the needle going, a slatted crate of LPs for the thrift shop, and the herbalist's tea-room table on legs with plump floor cushions whose tops are the seats. The `city-corner-shops` test gains two (four in all): every counter with the clerk behind it and tills and screens on its top, the returns box facing the room, the mat; the dresser on the wall, the sewing machine on its table, the crate, tea table height and cushion seats, the queue screen facing the room, no `BX(` left in any of the ten. Raising the sewing machine or the liquor screen 2 to 5 cm fails it. Render cost, warmed medians of 61 frames at 177×60, two ABAB runs, #230 → this branch: toys 4.2–4.4 → 4.3–5.5 ms, liquor 3.5–5.4 → 4.3–4.4, video 1.9–2.0 → 1.9–3.1, antiques 2.9 → 3.4–3.7, herbs 3.7–6.6 → 3.9–4.1, phones 2.5–2.6 → 2.8–3.0. Local observations, not an FPS guarantee.

- Shop interiors (pass 7, Claude), the last shop rooms: no shop def paints a box any more. The market hall's six stalls are built (`city/market-hall.js`): a timber counter each, two posts on the stallholder's edge holding a striped awning with a valance, and the goods on top: fish laid on crushed ice in a steel tray, crates of fruit and veg (the grocer's), cuts of meat on trays, cheese wheels and wedges, zinc buckets of flowers, loaves in wicker baskets; the coffee stand is a counter with the pass 3 espresso machine, and the crates are pass 3 crates. The fruit shop's tiered stands are the grocer's stand turned to face the room from each side wall (`PRODUCE_STAND` now takes a facing), with a table of striped melons and pineapples. Motel office: counter, an ice machine with its bin door and panel, a wall rack of brochures in pocketed tiers, a coffee table. Welder's: steel workbenches, a bench vice and a grinder with two wheels on edge, a cantilever rack with bars lying on its arms, a half-made gate of tube with a scroll, the boss's desk. Tyre shop: counter and monitor, a coffee vending machine. Gacha arcade: a change machine with a coin cup. Delicatessen: the slicer on its counter (a meat slicer with the blade on edge, turning), the window ledge is the pass 3 wall ledge. Deli: its back counter had stood mostly inside the back wall; it is now a shallow counter on the wall face with the slicer on it, and the take-a-number dispenser is on its pole. Tailor: a cutting table with chalked cloth and a rule, the sewing machines on their own tables, a standing fitting mirror, a curtain drawn back in folds on a rail. Antiques: a tea table between the armchairs, suitcases with straps and handles lying one on another. The 24/7 store's hot dog roller has turning rollers with sausages resting between them. The showroom and estate agent's desks are small desks. The shared `armchair` helper (thrift room, tailor, antiques, a café) is now an upholstered armchair at the same seat height. `city-specialist-shops` gains three tests (seven in all): stall goods on the counter top inside its footprint, posts on the stallholder edge under the awning, stallholders behind, the fruit stands facing the room; slicer, vice and monitor resting, the brochure rack on its wall, steel bars on the arms, the deli counter on the wall face; roller, reading chair seat, sewing machines on tables, mirror facing the room, suitcases resting, armchair seats, desks there, no `BX(` in any of these defs. Moving the awning posts to the customers' edge, the deli counter off the wall or the roller off the counter fails them. Render cost, warmed medians of 61 frames at 177×60, two ABAB runs, #231 → this branch: market 3.0–3.2 → 4.5–4.7 ms, market side 2.2–2.3 → 3.3–3.5, fruit shop 3.7–3.9 → 4.6–4.9, welder's 3.1–3.3 → 4.2, tailor 3.3 → 3.5–3.8, antiques 2.0–2.2 → 2.4–2.6, deli 8.1–8.8 → 6.2–6.4. Local observations, not an FPS guarantee.
- Leisure interiors (pass 10, Claude), cinema, arcade and casino: their painted boxes and sprites are built (`city/leisure-fittings.js`). The cinema's eight rows are seats with plush cushions and backs on armrested standards, end panels flush with the row ends, the backs away from the screen; the seat rows keep their sitting data. Each arcade cabinet has side panels, a back, the coin door, a control deck with its stick and three buttons, the screen set back in its bezel still playing, and a lit marquee; the cabinets face the aisle where you stand to play. The casino's cashier cage is brass rods standing on the counter under a head rail; the blackjack tables are a wood base, a padded rail and felt with the dealt hands as cards lying on it and chips stacked at the seats; the roulette table has its wheel turning in a timber bowl with the ball running round and the number layout on the felt; the slot machines, which were flat sprites, are cabinets with a lit topper, three reels turning behind a window, a coin tray and the lever, facing their stools. Blackjack, roulette, slots, the arcade and cinema seating keep their interactions. New `city-leisure-fittings` browser test: rows, seats and backs; cabinets facing a free spot with their screens and games; every part of every model inside its collision box; cards on the felt; slots facing their stools; cage bars on the counter; no `BX(` or `SP(` left in the three defs. Turning the slots round fails it; the first run caught the row end panels, the blackjack chips and the slot lever standing outside their boxes. The arcade, cinema-seat, casino games and casino break-in browser tests pass. Render cost, warmed medians of 61 frames at 177×60, two ABAB runs, main → this branch: cinema 3.3 → 4.6 ms, arcade 2.6–4.0 → 3.4–3.5, blackjack 3.4–3.6 → 4.4–4.7, casino wide 3.0 → 4.3–4.4, slots 2.3–3.1 → 3.3–3.4. Local observations, not an FPS guarantee.

- Leisure interiors (pass 10, Claude), the club, aquarium and cathedral: their painted boxes and sprites are built. The Velvet Rope: a stage with its boards and a chasing light strip along the nosing, three chrome poles standing on it up to the ceiling on their flanges, the cocktail tables as round pedestal tables each with a candle flickering in a glass, low velvet chairs with buttoned backs carrying their seats, and the VIP booth (velvet walls, a ceiling, a red curtain in folds parted at its middle on a rail, a lit VIP sign) set clear of the back wall it had stood into. The aquarium's touch pool is a tiled basin with sand at the bottom, starfish, urchins and anemones on it, and water you see down through but for its ripples; the gift shop's plush sharks lie round a turning stand of three shelves. The cathedral (`city/cathedral-fittings.js`): two stone sanctuary steps, the altar under a cloth hanging down its front, the gold cross standing on it, turned gold candlesticks with candles and flames, the altar rail of turned balusters with a kneeler, the pulpit as a turned tub with a book rest, the font as a stone bowl on a pedestal with water in it, the votive stand in three tiers of tealights lighting as many as visitors have lit, twenty pews with book ledges, carved ends and finials and a kneeler for the row behind, and three chandeliers of candle arms hung on chains from the vault. `city-leisure-fittings` gains three tests (four in all): poles on the stage under the ceiling, candles on tables, chairs carrying seats, the booth facing its host and clear of the walls; creatures on the sand inside the basin under clear water; the cross on the altar, the pews keeping their rows, chandeliers from the vault, the votive count; no `BX(` or `SP(` in the three defs. Raising the cross off the altar fails it; the first run caught the booth inside the back wall. The club's table test in the main suite now finds the built round tables by their top height. The Velvet Rope, aquarium, fish species and cathedral browser tests pass. Render cost, warmed medians of 61 frames at 177×60, two ABAB runs, #233 → this branch: club 2.5–2.8 → 3.3–3.7 ms, club VIP 2.2–2.3 → 3.4–3.6, touch pool 2.2–2.4 → 2.1–2.2, cathedral altar 3.5–3.7 → 4.9–5.6, nave 4.4–4.9 → 6.2–6.6. Local observations, not an FPS guarantee.
- Home decoration (pass4), fitted plush shark and bed support: the placed IDEA shark now stays inside its existing0.48×0.96×0.35m placement envelope. Reproportioned joined fins/tail and seated eyes, filtered cloth/embroidered mouth/gills and a closed flat belly replace overhanging fins and the floating underside. Cached model parts/materials prepare at construction; the legacy homePlushShark entry point and common ellipsoid helper remain unchanged for other callers. Also repaired plush placement on native beds: the old path skipped drawn bed models, and their conservative boxes include pillows. Cached rendered bed pieces now supply the actual flat duvet plane. Full-footprint samples and higher-piece bounds reject pillows, folds and edges; other furniture still blocks placement, and ray-hit state is fully restored. Three focused browser checks probe flat support, connected parts/eyes and bounds at five rotations on floor/bed, real IDEA buy/place/save/reload/pack and wall-flush placement, and supported/rejected candidates on all four home beds with one geometry capture per bed. Oversized-fin and disabled-bed-support mutations both fail the intended checks. All three existing furnishing/light/photo/music browser regressions, home-data/bundle/parser/render-cost checks pass. Reviewed front/side/oblique/above/reverse/low/wide, actual bed placement, night and phone views; widened the filtered mouth seam after the initial view broke into isolated pixels. ABAB177×66 local medians main→after: front3.0–4.4→2.7–5.2ms, oblique2.5–3.0→3.1–4.4ms, low2.7→3.1–5.7ms, wide1.8–1.9→1.8–3.8ms, bed4.6–4.7→4.6–4.7ms; unchanged suite control4.1–4.5→4.1ms. Close views can cost more and host tails vary; these are scene measurements rather than an FPS improvement claim. #227 keepsake full CI is green. Pass4 remains open for its closing audit, then passes5/6 home/hotel layouts.

- Home decoration (pass4), closing audit: reviewed native studio/loft/Belle/Fern interiors plus a furnished Fern living area from both sides by day and at night, with no page errors. Fourteen existing lamp, record-console, frame/photo, palm, clock and book geometry/control checks pass on the final tree; combined with the three new plush/bed checks and three furnishing/photo/light/music regressions,20 home browser checks pass. The audit's remaining actionable decoration defects were the plush envelope/contact and skipped native bed support, fixed in the preceding chunk. Existing native and movable lighting, TVs, records, clocks, books, plants, frames and home-specific keepsakes are complete for pass4. There is no native home radio fixture; painted radio stock belongs to the shop pass7. Other displayed item families use the common inventory artwork and continue in pass27. Doors/window surrounds, ceiling/wall trim, room layouts and further built-in character continue in passes5/6, rather than holding the decoration audit open. Codex's next queue is pass5 home openings/layouts, then pass6 hotels; Claude retains shops, restaurants and leisure/cathedral.

- Street objects (pass11), vending machines (Claude, `claude/street-vending`): the street and club drink, snack and cigarette machines are built in new `city/street-vending.js` instead of painted on one box. A rounded steel shell sits on a recessed plinth. Its door frames a lit header (shared `worldText`, so letters never double and become block glyphs up close), a glass window, the control column (READY display, keypad, coin and note slots, coin cup) and a chute with a hanging flap and push lip. Up close and in front, the glass is clear but for a filtered glint, and shows the lit back, lining, strip light, four shelves with price lips, and 20 cans or packets (40 packs) standing on them. Further off or from behind, the stock is painted on the glass and the inside isn't built. The footprint, collision, E prompts and indoor scale (sc 10) are unchanged; every part stays inside VM_HL×VM_HW×VM_H. Reviewed close/oblique/wide by day, at night and in the Velvet Rope. Median render ABAB vs main: close 6.5 to 7.9 ms; oblique, night, wide and far within noise. The new browser test fails on floating stock, opaque close glass, stock built from behind and a proud display.
- Codex review of #233 (pass10): corrected the gaming-table bases stopping 4cm below their tops, and joined each slot-machine lever to its cabinet with a pivot. A new regression probes the actual rounded surfaces and continuous handle attachment in four orientations; it fails against both original defects and passes after repair. Existing model-bounds and arcade, cinema-seat, casino-game and casino-break-in checks pass. Reviewed close/wide/oblique/low/night views of cinema, arcade and casino with no page errors. Independent ABAB177×66 measurements against main were noisy under concurrent suite load: blackjack3.1–4.3→4.4–6.5ms, slot close1.8–1.9→3.5–3.6ms, arcade close2.6–2.9→3.0–5.3ms. These support no speedup claim; Claude's earlier isolated measurements remain above. Current home fixes/modules/notes were preserved and the bundle rebuilt.

- Codex review of #234 (pass10): the altar, candlesticks and priest now stand on the sanctuary's actual 30cm elevation, with the cross height derived from the altar; font water is above the bowl floor and visible; the votive tiers have solid risers, pew kneelers have floor feet, and the pulpit book rest uses a unit rotation and seats on the rim. The new rendered-geometry regression rejects the original sunken furniture, hidden water, floating tiers/kneelers and invalid book-rest rotation/bounds; it passes after correction. All six leisure geometry checks, seven existing club/aquarium/cathedral gameplay/route regressions and seven bundle/parse/render checks pass. Reviewed close/wide/oblique/night cathedral/club and clear touch-pool/font/plush views without page errors. Independent ABAB177×66 against current main (#233) under concurrent full-suite load: nave5.3–5.6→8.7–10.8ms, altar6.1–7.1→7.4–12.0ms, font3.9–4.1→7.5–9.5ms, pool3.4–3.9→6.8–10.4ms. Close pew/stand tails were especially noisy; these measurements confirm added cost and do not establish a stable frame-rate limit or speedup. Claude's earlier isolated measurements remain above. All home changes and both agents' appended notes/module lists preserved; generated bundle rebuilt.

- Home layouts (pass5), fitted window casings: native studios, lofts and Belle homes now have closed projecting jambs/rails, recessed joined glazing bars and supported bevelled sills, using timber, dark metal and painted finishes respectively (`city/home-windows.js`). The painter supplies the existing real neighbourhood view through the clear panes instead of drawing duplicate frames. All pieces/materials/rays prepare once per window and reuse their view boxes. Opening sizes are retained; the studio's second opening moves17cm along its wall so the sill clears the full-width native bookcase, with no shelf/save relocation. Fern/Cabin Cove remain on their own window builders. Two focused browser checks probe actual wall contact, all bounds and normals, corner/bar/sill joins, clear panes and object reuse in all four orientations/three finishes; native three-family fitting counts, real-view delegation, furniture clearance and camera/entry/bedroom routes. Deliberately detaching a jamb or restoring the painted frame each fails the intended check; the furniture check caught the initial studio clash before correction. Nine existing home-layout/Fern/TV and two native room/Belle-purchase-save-balcony checks pass (13 home browser checks total), plus seven bundle/parse/render checks. Close/wide/oblique/low/night/phone views reviewed. ABAB177×66 local medians against main #234: studio close4.2–4.6→6.1ms, studio wide5.3–5.4→5.5–5.6, loft close4.2–4.3→5.3–5.4, loft wide4.2→4.8, Belle close4.3–4.4→5.5, Belle wide3.5→4.2; unchanged suite control4.1–4.2→3.9–4.2ms. Fitted geometry adds measurable cost, especially close up; no speedup claim. Pass5 continues with doors/trim/layout character, then pass6 hotels.

- Codex solo review of #215 (seafood tank): integrated current main, preserving the home/window/leisure work and both agents' notes/module lists before rebuilding. Expanded the tank bounds to contain the real door pulls, bounded every bubble's peak by its own diameter, cached the fixed cabinet/door/rail/hood profiles and lobster materials, and replaced the aliasing gravel colour checker with filtered tonal grain. Added geometry has a measured cost; no speedup claim. #233, #234 and #237 updated full CI is green. Next #217 karaoke fittings.

- Street lights (pass12), the street lamp up close (Claude, `claude/street-lamps`): within 5 cells the standard lamp is now turned and joined, not stacked boxes (new `city/street-lamps.js`; `drawLamp3D` moved out of `city/city-sprites.js`, which keeps the shared `STEEL` parts).
  - Base and pole: a flared cast base, then a fluted pole tapering from 10.5 to 7.8 cm radius up to a finial, with a collar part way up. The flutes fade with distance.
  - Swan neck: one smooth tube curve in ten joined pieces from the pole top out to the drop.
  - Drop and lantern: the drop rod runs into the lantern cap. The lantern has six frame bars round its glass, between a domed cap and a finial. It glows warm after dark under the existing halo.
  Past 5 cells the billboard takes over. The neck reads as the same fine arc either side of the switch, and Belle lamps are unchanged. The new browser test fails on a gap in the neck, a drop short of the cap, an untapered pole and unlit glass at night. Median render ABAB vs main: close, whole, night, street and down-street all within noise.

- Utilities (pass12), downpipes and meters on the street fronts (Claude, `claude/facade-utilities`): new `city/facade-utilities.js` runs on the assembled city after `world-bootstrap.js` and uses the architecture's own street faces.
  - Placement: a downpipe runs just inside each end of a street front (its corner or party wall), so it never crosses the lettering centred on the front.
  - Route: each pipe works its way up from the pavement. It steps out round a thin string course with offset bends and back in, and stops with its hopper head just under the top cornice or any deep moulding. It keeps the street geometry's own clearance throughout.
  - Parts: brackets every couple of metres (not on the bends) and a shoe turned out at the foot, which pours water in the rain. Belle fronts and landmarks are left to their own detailing.
  - Meters: about half the pipes have an electric meter (grey, with a dial) or a gas meter (yellow, its pipe rising out of the pavement) beside them at hand height.
  The new browser test fails on ignoring the cornice, not stepping round a moulding, water when dry and a pipe short of the pavement. Removing the door check changes nothing, because the pipes only sit at front ends, but the test still asserts no pipe crosses a door. Median render ABAB vs main: shopfront, street, avenue and Chinatown views up to about +1 ms, mostly within noise.
- Street objects (pass11), drains and grates (Claude, `claude/street-drains`): flush ironwork is drawn into the floor beside the manhole covers (new `city/street-drains.js`, one hook in `city/city-render.js`).
  - Gully grates: in the gutter against the kerb, two per block side, never on a bridge or in a lane. Bars run across the gutter inside a solid frame.
  - Rain runoff: in rain a thin rivulet runs along the kerb within half a cell of each grate, rippling in toward it. Dry streets show none.
  - Tree grates: cast iron in a 90 cm pit round each street tree on the pavement, with soil round the trunk. Pits stay inside the 0.3 pavement band. The street fitting leaves two trees in the road (a waterfront poplar mid-street at 385, 314; a brownstone oak in the gutter at 340.6, 241.7). They get no pit, and are noted for pass 18.
  - Ventilation grates: beside every subway entrance, clear of the steps.
  Every bar pattern blends to an even tone once its bars are smaller than a cell, and lane pixels skip the lookups. Median render ABAB vs main: looking straight down at a grate 6.4 to 7.2 ms; wide, down-street, brownstone trees and rain within noise. The new browser test fails on a grate on the pavement, runoff when dry, unfiltered bars at distance and pits dug into the road.


- Street objects (pass11), kerb fixtures (Claude, `claude/kerb-fixtures`): the streets gain fire hydrants (1,214), litter bins (1,333), mailboxes (479) and bollards (480) on the kerb line where the lamps stand. Placement (new `city/kerb-fixtures.js`) runs on the assembled city after `world-bootstrap.js`. Each fixture keeps clear of the walking line (0.12/1.88), street geometry, lamps, signals, trees, benches, vending machines, el pillars and stairs, carts, subway entrances and gas forecourts. A blocked spot slides along the kerb up to 0.3 cells or is dropped (35 of 3,541 dropped, 4 slid). Hydrants sit near corners with their pumper nozzle to the road; bins beside the first lamp; mailboxes face the pavement; bollards line landmarks and some downtown kerbs. Colours follow the district: red hydrants (yellow in industrial), green or grey bins, blue mailboxes (red in the Shotengai). The models are in new `city/kerb-fixtures-render.js`:
  - hydrant: a turned lathe with two side outlets, a pumper nozzle, caps and an operating nut;
  - bin: a ribbed drum with a rolled rim, the bag folded over it, rubbish inside and litter on top;
  - mailbox: legs, a body with a half-cylinder top, the chute door and handle, the collection-times plate and MAIL lettering via `worldText`;
  - bollard: a domed post with a reflective band.
  Past 2.5 cells only the main body is drawn, and a fixture smaller than a character isn't drawn at all. On foot they're solid (`kerbAt` in `free`), and a kicked ball stops at them. Median render ABAB vs main: close views +0.5 to 1 ms (hydrant 6.5 to 7.0, bin 5.8 to 7.0); wide and down-street within noise. The new browser test fails on fixtures off the kerb line, missing walk collision, a backwards mailbox and the hydrant nut drawn 7 m up (a real bug it caught).

- Street objects (pass11), utility cabinets (Claude, `claude/utility-cabinets`, stacked on #238): 696 street cabinets join the kerb fixtures. Grey signal controller cabinets stand at most junctions, on the kerb beside the first signal pole and half a cell back from the corner. Green telecom cabinets stand on some mid-block kerbs. Each is built: a concrete plinth, a rounded steel body under an overhanging cap, two doors to the pavement with a seam, handle, hinges and label, and louvred vents high on each end. They keep the kerb-fixture rules: kerb line, walking line clear, solid on foot, every part inside the box. The kerb test now also fails on a signal cabinet away from any signal, a cap past the box and a telecom cabinet facing the road. Median render ABAB vs the kerb-fixtures base: close, wide and down-street all within noise.

- Street objects (pass11), shelters (Claude, `claude/street-shelters`, stacked on #240): 232 advertising shelters stand on downtown, midtown, Chinatown and waterfront pavements. The city has no buses, so they're street shelters, not bus stops. Each is set back 3 cm from the kerb line so its back stays clear of the walking line, and opens to the road. It's built from four steel posts, a roof with a lip, rails and a glass back with a frosted band at eye height, a glass end screen, and a lit advert panel at the other end. The panel shows an advert word on both faces, reading left to right from either side; it was mirrored on one face until review caught it.
  - Standing under the roof keeps the rain off the camera (`shelterRoofAt` in `rainFx`).
  - The bench inside is a real street bench (sit with C).
  - The back, advert panel and glass screen are solid on foot; the open front lets you in.
  The kerb test now also fails on non-solid walls, rain under the roof, a missing bench and a set-back the wrong way. Median render ABAB vs the cabinets base on a noisy machine: shelter close-ups about +1 ms; wide and down-street within noise.
- Street utilities (pass12), overhead lines (Claude, `claude/overhead-cables`, stacked on #241): timber pole lines carry wires down one side of every street in the Docks and the Yards (480 poles, 368 spans), as `pole` and `guy` kinds in `city/kerb-fixtures.js` with their models in `city/kerb-fixtures-render.js`. Poles are placed before the other kerb fixtures, so hydrants and bins make way for them.
  - Each pole is creosoted timber, tapering, with a chamfered top. A crossarm on a steel brace reaches out over the road, with a porcelain insulator under each of the three wires, and the telephone cable runs on a bracket below. The arm reaches the road side so the wires keep at least 30 cm clear of the lamp posts that share the kerb line.
  - Lines along the avenues are carried at 8.4 m and those along the cross streets at 6.2 m. Where they cross over a junction they pass at least 0.5 m apart, and no wire sags below 4.4 m over the road. Wires sag 1.2% of the span (the cable 30% more), skip the el, and stay clear of buildings, street geometry, lamps and trees.
  - The end of each line is held by a guy wire to an anchor on the kerb line, with a yellow guard sleeve; its foot is solid on foot. 118 poles carry a transformer can on the building side, its leads jumping up beside the pole to the first wire. 205 have a service drop sagging back to a bracket on the wall behind.
  - Wires are near-black lines, dark against the sky and the walls alike; the first mid-grey ones vanished against the facades and read as rain streaks against the sky. Past 100 m a wire is too fine to see, so spans aren't drawn there.
  The new browser test (`city-overhead-lines`) fails on crossing lines at one height, service drops ending short of the wall, insulators off their wires and wires drawn past 100 m. Removing the street-geometry check from placement changes nothing (no span meets any today); the test still checks every wire against it. The kerb test now exempts the overhead arm and the guy wire from the footprint check, which the new test covers. Median render ABAB vs `claude/street-shelters`: from the road beside a pole 9.0 ms became 9.7 to 10.4 ms; looking up at a pole with its can 6.1 became 7.5 to 7.7; down a Docks street and a midtown control within noise.
- Restaurant lamps and wire chairs (pass8), attachment follow-up: every lamp's tassel, caps, shade, cap, cord and rose already meet, and the chair's legs, rings, posts and hoop join. The one gap was the chair back's inner ring, which floated inside the hoop. Four short flat bars in the hoop's plane now hold it. The browser test fails on a floating inner ring (the old code fails it).

- Ice-cream and burger wall fixtures (pass8), attachment follow-up: the burger pass's ticket rail had nothing holding it to the wall. It now has two wall plates with standoffs into the rail. The heat-lamp hood hung 1 mm in front of the hatch frame; its back now meets the frame's face. Stacked wafer cones floated 2 mm above the cavity floor of the cone below; the step is now the cavity floor height, so each tip stands on it. The browser test fails on an unheld rail, a hood off the frame or a floating cone (the old code fails both).

- Restaurant wall fixtures (pass8), attachment follow-up from Codex's review: the pho fans' grille rings and hub badge floated, and the spinner had no shaft. Each grille now carries its rings on four flat radial spokes from the hub (front) or motor face (back) into the rim, and a shaft runs from the motor into the spinner. The roastery vines started in mid-air outside the pot rim, and their leaves cut back through the pot. Each vine now roots in the soil, crosses over the rim, and hangs straight down in front of the pot past its base before falling back toward the wall. The browser test now fails on a spoke short of the hub or rim, a ring off its spokes, an unshafted spinner, an unrooted vine, a vine through the rim or a leaf inside the pot (the old code fails both).

The new actual-hardware/cache/negative-time check fails on the original bounds, six per-draw profile builds and negative-time lyric errors; all four karaoke checks and four bundle/parse checks pass. The Tiffany and heat-lamp rims now open into closed thin shells, with actual luminous bulbs seated in sockets that meet the upper cap/shade; the outer cases remain nonemissive. Native sushi and chair boxes remain deliberate blocking/seat footprints, rather than being inflated to decorative rail/back heights. Next is shop stack #221–#232; Claude remains on pass12.

- Codex review of #221 (shop display cases): integrated all reviewed restaurant work through #220. Ring stones now sit on their bands instead of floating above the central hole; an actual downward-ray check samples their supporting contact in the native jeweller cases. Goods, frame and cabinet geometry/materials prepare once per case; drawing only translates their templates and retains the existing thin-tube LOD. No shared geometry helper edits or collision changes. The new support/cache check fails on the original implementation; independently restoring the floating stone or rebuilding profiles fails it. All three case checks, the kitchen-display/aircraft-terminal regressions, five shop-route/seating checks and four bundle/parse checks pass. Jeweller close/wide/oblique/night plus pawn, skate, herb and tobacco cases reviewed. Built goods add measured cost; timings vary locally, with no speedup claim. #219 final full CI is green. Next #222 shop fridge walls; Claude retains pass12.

- Codex review of #222 (shop fridge walls): integrated current main through #221, keeping both built case and fridge replacements while removing their obsolete painters in the one source conflict. Claude's shallow fridge geometry, dairy stock, prepared product profiles and shared-cooler changes retained without further shared-helper edits. Added an actual-rendered-stock check across all seven runs (over1,000 products): shelf contact, side/back/glass containment, clearance below the next shelf, native stock count and profile reuse. Lowering shelves, rebuilding stock profiles per draw, and moving the runs off their walls independently fail the intended checks. All six fridge/shared-cooler checks, five shop/route/seating checks and four bundle/parse checks pass. All five rooms inspected, including dairy close/oblique/night, the grocer corner and clear-aisle wide view. Replacing wall paint with stocked cabinets has a material render cost; these local measurements are not an FPS guarantee. Next #223 shelf islands; Claude keeps pass12.

- Codex review of #223 (shop gondolas): integrated current main through #222. Hardware bins now have closed thin rims, real recessed floors/inner walls and three supported stacks of metal washers; practical32cm-deep bins sit at the shelf fronts. The existing tray ray is called unchanged through a local rotation wrapper, so the native north/south aisle has correct surfaces and normals. Stock depth order now prepares for the exact camera direction and is reused during stationary/translated views, updating on a turn. Two new actual-rendering checks fail on the old solid lids and repeated sorting; separate always-sort, stale-on-turn, filled-lid and floating-content mutations are rejected. All four gondola checks,17 shop/routes/placement/case/fridge regressions and seven bundle/parse/render-cost checks pass. Cached grocery/book/wine views match the pre-review built prototype exactly. Physical stock adds cost versus wall painting; caching avoids repeated sorting, not a demonstrated whole-frame speedup. Next #224 back-wall shelves; carry these cavity/order checks through its factored goods builder. #220 full CI is green. #221's only failed shard is the old random café conversation test placing the player inside the front wall at a front-row patron; reproduced with free=false/empty prompt and a clear-floor approach restoring E: talk. A separate focused test repair follows; its failed shard was retried. Claude retains pass12.

Deterministically populate the native seats, deliberately cover a front-row patron, choose a clear walkable approach and aim at the seated/standing person. The café/station conversation and counter-service assertions remain. The corrected check passes twice; restoring the old wall placement fails the new walkability assertion. Both existing aimed-interaction checks and four bundle/parse checks pass. This is test-only; game geometry and interaction rules are unchanged. #222 final full CI is green. - Interior closing-audit queue: keep passes7/8 open for the remaining painted ceiling strip/spot/pendant marks as well as the already recorded paper-flag cord joins and noodle-pulling lines. - Shop interiors (pass 7, Claude), fruit shop wallpaper: `fruitWall` filled whole 50 cm squares of the paper with orange `@` glyphs, so from across the counter the wall showed big orange slabs. The paper now carries small oranges (7 cm across, a glint on the shoulder, a leaf) in a half-drop repeat: one centred in every other 25 cm square, each row half a step along from the last (a first scattered version looked untidy). New `city-fruit-wallpaper` browser test samples every 50 cm square of a 12 m run every 1 cm: no square more than 20% fruit, both oranges and leaves present, and the paper the same half a metre along, half a metre up and a quarter along and up. The old wallpaper and the scattered one both fail it. Render cost, warmed medians of 61 frames at 1280×720, two ABAB runs, main → after: facing the wall close up 0.9 → 1.1–1.2 ms, the room from the counter 2.2–2.5 → 2.5–3.1, from the door 3.4–3.7 → 3.4–3.6 (another test run shared the machine, so treat these as rough).

- Signs and shopfronts (pass13), fascia boards and awnings (Claude, `claude/shop-fascias`): the midtown, downtown, brownstone and residential shops (about 1,180, the architecture families) painted their names flat on the wall with nothing behind them. Each named street face now has a fascia board standing 12 cm off the wall round the same lettering (2,673 boards: 1,462 boards, 607 boxes, 604 painted) (new `fascia` details from `architectureShopfront` in `city/architecture.js`, shaded in `city/architecture-render.js`). The Chinatown and Shotengai merchants already had 3D boards, awnings and blades and are unchanged.
  - Brownstones paint theirs, gold leaf on a dark board (green, blue, grey or black; brick vanished against the brick walls in review). Downtown it's a dark box with the name in the shop's colour, lit from inside after dark while open. Midtown and the apartment blocks use a board in the shop's colour with white letters. Each has a frame round the lettering, and the name reads left to right from the street on all four facings.
  - `architectureSignSpec` holds each family's lettering height, size and band in one place. The painted lettering past `ARCH_FASCIA_VIS` (120 m) and the board up close use the same numbers, so the handover doesn't move or resize the name. Up close the wall behind the board stays bare.
  - 386 brownstone and midtown shopfronts (about 60% of those wide enough; not downtown towers, not apartment blocks with entrance canopies) get a striped awning: a thin sheet sloping from the wall at 3.25 m to 2.75 m over a metre's depth, darker underneath, and a valance with a scalloped hem down to 2.55 m. You walk under it, it stops short of the walking line, and it stays clear of the lamps, trees and vending machines.
  - Boards are skipped where they'd run into a low building's cornice (a 3.8 m gas kiosk keeps its painted name). The hardware shop that becomes the IDEA store after the city is built refits its board to the new name instead of showing the old one.
  The new browser test (`city-shop-fascias`) fails on lettering painted behind the board up close, a board shifted off its lettering, an awning you walk into, lettering on the back face, a board up in a cornice and a renamed shop keeping its old board. `city-storefront-signs` now counts board lettering up close as well as painted lettering. Median render ABAB vs main: square on to a midtown shop with its awning 7.8 to 8.5 ms became 9.2 to 10.2 ms; a brownstone shop 7.0 to 7.5 became 7.9 to 8.2; down a midtown street, down a downtown street and a Docks control within noise.

- Signs and shopfronts (pass13), the generic shopfronts (Claude, `claude/oldpath-shopfronts`, stacked on #248): the shops still on the generic facade in `city/city-render.js` (Docks warehouses and workshops, sty 8; midtown and Docks stucco shops, sty 15; brick shops in the brownstones and the Docks, sty 2) painted their name over and over round every wall, and an awning flat on the wall under it. New `city/shopfronts.js` finds their faces with the architecture face finder (`SHOPFRONT_FACES`, moved by `world-bootstrap.js` with the cell behind each face) and gives each street face one board with the name on it: 671 boards (351 warehouse boards in the shop's colour, 210 lit boxes, 110 painted). Apartments and shops closed for good keep their old fronts.
  - The name is painted once per street face from further off, at the board's height and size; up close the band behind the board is bare, and side and back walls carry no name.
  - 223 open shops get the same striped awning as the architecture families, set a little lower to fit under the generic band (3.1 m sloping to 2.7 m, valance to 2.55 m). The painted awning is gone where there's a real one, leaving the transom. Warehouses get none over their loading doors.
  - From far off only a few rows sample the facade, and the generic code only reached the band for rows above z .32, so the lettering vanished past about 100 m. It now checks from .28 and lets `wallText` pick the row, as the architecture faces do.
  The fascia test now covers the generic faces too. It fails on lettering painted behind the board up close, an awning over a warehouse door, and faces not moved with the city's layout (their far lettering ends up 48 cells away). Median render ABAB vs #248: square on to a Docks diner 7.5 to 7.9 ms became 6.8 to 7.2 ms; a brownstone-district diner 7.0 to 7.7 became 5.8 to 6.4 (the old band sampled glyphs 3 by 3 per cell); a midtown tattoo parlour, a midtown street and an architecture control within noise.

- Signs and shopfronts (pass13), shop window displays (Claude, `claude/shop-windows`, stacked on #249): every shop window was flat painted glass. New `city/shop-windows.js` (`shopWindow`) puts a display bay 70 cm deep behind the glass of the midtown, downtown, brownstone, residential and generic shops when you're within 35 m (`SHOP_WINDOW.near`); further off the windows stay as they were.
  - The bay has a plinth along its floor in the shop's colour (its front darker, in its own shadow), the shop's goods standing on it at 45% depth, and the lit shop beyond with its shelves. Walk past and the goods slide against the back. After dark, while the shop is open, a spotlight shines over each item from the bay's ceiling.
  - What's in the window follows the shop: loaves and cakes for the bakeries, cafes and diners; mannequins for thrift, tailors and shoes; book stacks; glowing screens for video, records and the arcade; bottles for liquor, juice and the drugstore; bunches of flowers on their stems for the florist and herbs; boxed goods for the rest. Heights vary a little item to item.
  - Neon shops keep their glow, apartment lobbies their doors, warehouses their loading doors, and a closed shop its shutter.
  The new browser test (`city-shop-windows`) drives `shopWindow` along chosen rays: plinth front versus top, goods versus the shop beyond, the goods holding still against a sliding back, the side wall at a steep angle and the spotlights after dark. It also checks every family shows displays up close but not from 45 m, and that neon shops and warehouses get none. It fails on no near limit, goods painted on the back, no plinth front, displays in a neon shop, a family missing its displays and no spotlights. Median render ABAB vs #249, two runs: square on to a midtown noodle bar's window 8.3 to 11.1 ms became 9.4 to 10.1 ms; a brownstone florist 6.7 to 7.3 became 7.6 to 9.6; a residential herb shop and a generic record shop +0.2 to 0.8; a midtown street within noise.
- Signs and shopfronts (pass13), roller shutters (Claude, `claude/shop-shutters`, stacked on #252): a shut shop (closed for the night or for good) drew the same flat grey stripes over its whole ground floor in every district, with nothing round them. New `city/shop-shutters.js` (`shopShutter`) draws a roller shutter in each opening instead, given the opening's sides, sill and head by each facade: the brownstone, downtown and residential windows and doors (through `architectureShutter` and `shopDoor`), the Chinatown and Shotengai merchants, the Belle Epoque doorways, the generic fronts and the Docks warehouses' roll-up doors.
  - The curtain sits 8 cm back in the reveal; within 35 m a ray at an angle meets the guide rail's channel at the side or the underside of the housing first. A housing at the head, the curtain's edges in their rails, a heavier bottom bar with a padlock in the middle.
  - Up close (each slat two rows or more on screen) the slats show their lips catching the light and their creases in shadow; further off they run together into an even `=` texture rather than aliasing into a brick pattern.
  - Most are galvanised; about three in seven are painted green, blue or red, one colour per shop (from its name and building) rather than per bay.
  - The Shotengai's own front (`shotengaiFront`) still has its old stripes, but every Shotengai shop is drawn by the merchant facade, so that line never runs for a shop.
  New browser test (`city-shop-shutters`, 2): along chosen rays, the housing, lit lips against shadowed creases, the even texture from further off, the rail channel at a steep angle, the curtain edge, the bottom bar and padlock, one paint per shop and more than one paint across the city; and for each family (brownstones, downtown, residential, generic, warehouses, merchants) a shop shut at night draws its shutters up close and none while it is open. It fails on no reveal, flat slats, no far texture, no padlock, paint per call, every shutter grey, no housing, the families falling back to flat stripes, shutters down while open, and the generic fronts or warehouses keeping their old stripes. Median render ABAB at 23:30 vs #252, two runs: 3 m from a downtown shop 8.1 to 9.1 ms became 8.8 to 9.5; a brownstone 8.2 became 8.6 to 8.7; a residential shop 6.7 to 7.0 became 7.1 to 7.2; a warehouse 5.7 to 5.9 became 6.4 to 6.5; from 15 m and along a midtown street within noise.
- Signs and shopfronts (pass13), doors on the generic shopfronts (Claude, `claude/generic-doors`, stacked on #253): the shops on the generic facade (brick and stucco, styles 2 and 15) were all windows with no way in, and every face without a built awning still had the old striped awning painted flat across its whole ground floor. Now each face with a board (`SHOPFRONT_FACES`) has the shared glazed double door (`shopDoor`: frame, kick plate, push bars, the shop lit behind it, its own shutter after hours) centred in the bay nearest the middle of its board, and the band where the painted awning was is a plain transom like the one under a built awning. New browser test (`city-generic-doors`): for 40 boarded generic shops open by day, the door is drawn looking at the board from 10 m, and over six faces without an awning no `/` is left in the transom band. Removing the door or putting the painted awning back fails it. Render cost against #253 by day, warmed medians, three ABAB runs including one in reverse order: 10 m from a brick shop 6.9 to 8.2 ms against 7.0 to 8.1; 10 m from a stucco shop 6.1 to 7.8 against 6.4 to 7.8 (one 10.4 to 10.9 outlier per run followed the run position, not the branch); 3 m and a midtown street within the same noise.
