Nine independent body/door/hinge/pull/vent/cache/collision/label mutations fail the intended checks. Reviewed12 clear-floor close/wide/oblique/back/cap/label/pull/night views. ABAB177×66 main→after medians: signal-close 4.4–4.6→5.3–5.4ms; signal-oblique 4.2–4.4→4.8–5ms; signal-wide 5.4–5.5→5.3–5.5ms; signal-night 5.6–5.8→6.6ms; signal-back 4.7–4.9→5.5–5.9ms; signal-cap 3.7–3.8→4.7–4.9ms; telecom-close 4.4–4.7→5.5–5.8ms; telecom-oblique 5.3–5.5→6.1–6.3ms; telecom-wide 6.2–6.3→6.3–6.5ms; telecom-night 6.2–6.3→7.2–7.6ms; telecom-label 5.5–5.6→7.7–7.9ms; telecom-pull 5.2–5.4→7.2–7.4ms. Added cabinets have measured close-view cost; no general speedup claimed. Reserved Claude source and shared helpers untouched. Next #241→#246, then the other authorized chains and passes5/6/9; hotel/motel washrooms remain queued to start closed and auto-close, with homes unchanged.

- Codex review of #241 (street shelters): integrated main through #240 and retained Claude's placements, usable street benches and open entrances. Fixed floating advert boxes with closed supporting feet and heads meeting the canopy; end glass now sits in joined upper/lower channels with a closed free-edge frame. All fixed surfaces/materials use the reviewed kerb cache. Rendering and collision share exact surface boxes, including all four posts; the grounded wall check skips the overhead canopy while finite-height balls meet it. Rain cover uses the real rounded canopy footprint and terrain height. Clear glass has filtered surface glints instead of slash glyphs; cached adverts react to day/night and keep readable scalable lettering on both faces. Eight focused kerb/shelter browser checks and three furniture/bench/booth controls pass, plus three node bundle/parse/render-cost files. Eleven independent missing-support/frame/post-collision/terrain/cover/entrance/glint/rain/bench/night mutations fail. Reviewed11 clear-floor close/wide/oblique/back/canopy/inside/both-advert-face/night/glass-edge views. ABAB177×66 main→after medians: shelter-close 5.6–9.7→5.8–6.4ms; shelter-oblique 4.7–5.4→5.4–6.1ms; shelter-wide 4.4–6.1→4.5–4.7ms; shelter-night 8.4–9.3→5.6–5.7ms; shelter-back 5.1–6→7.6–8ms; shelter-canopy 3.6–8.7→5ms; shelter-inside 3.5–4.6→5.2ms; advert-outside 3.9–4.4→6.1–6.3ms; advert-inside 6.4–6.8→8–8.2ms; advert-night 4.9–5.1→6.4–6.5ms; glass-edge 5.9–6→7.6–7.8ms. Background occlusion and substantial host variance affect these samples; detailed surfaces add close-view work, with no general speedup claim. #240 full CI is green; #238's earlier run was cancelled, superseded by #240's successful run including its changes. Reserved active Claude files and shared helpers untouched. Next #246 overhead cables, then the remaining authorized chains and passes5/6/9.
- Street objects (pass11), drains and grates (Claude, `claude/street-drains`): flush ironwork is drawn into the floor beside the manhole covers (new `city/street-drains.js`, one hook in `city/city-render.js`).
  - Gully grates: in the gutter against the kerb, two per block side, never on a bridge or in a lane. Bars run across the gutter inside a solid frame.
  - Rain runoff: in rain a thin rivulet runs along the kerb within half a cell of each grate, rippling in toward it. Dry streets show none.
  - Tree grates: cast iron in a 90 cm pit round each street tree on the pavement, with soil round the trunk. Pits stay inside the 0.3 pavement band. The street fitting leaves two trees in the road (a waterfront poplar mid-street at 385, 314; a brownstone oak in the gutter at 340.6, 241.7). They get no pit, and are noted for pass 18.
  - Ventilation grates: beside every subway entrance, clear of the steps.
  Every bar pattern blends to an even tone once its bars are smaller than a cell, and lane pixels skip the lookups. Median render ABAB vs main: looking straight down at a grate 6.4 to 7.2 ms; wide, down-street, brownstone trees and rain within noise. The new browser test fails on a grate on the pavement, runoff when dry, unfiltered bars at distance and pits dug into the road.
- Street lights (pass12), the street lamp up close (Claude, `claude/street-lamps`): within 5 cells the standard lamp is now turned and joined, not stacked boxes (new `city/street-lamps.js`; `drawLamp3D` moved out of `city/city-sprites.js`, which keeps the shared `STEEL` parts).
  - Base and pole: a flared cast base, then a fluted pole tapering from 10.5 to 7.8 cm radius up to a finial, with a collar part way up. The flutes fade with distance.
  - Swan neck: one smooth tube curve in ten joined pieces from the pole top out to the drop.
  - Drop and lantern: the drop rod runs into the lantern cap. The lantern has six frame bars round its glass, between a domed cap and a finial. It glows warm after dark under the existing halo.
  Past 5 cells the billboard takes over. The neck reads as the same fine arc either side of the switch, and Belle lamps are unchanged. The new browser test fails on a gap in the neck, a drop short of the cap, an untapered pole and unlit glass at night. Median render ABAB vs main: close, whole, night, street and down-street all within noise.
- Street lights (pass12), signals and pedestrian heads (Claude, `claude/street-signals`): `drawSignal` moves to new `city/street-signals.js` and is built.
  - Pole: up close, a turned pole on a flanged foot meeting the head; a rod further off.
  - Head: a rounded housing with a backplate on each face (black, with a white border). Each lens sits under a cowl, a hood with two cheeks, open below. You still see the lamps only from along the street.
  - Pedestrian head: out on a bracket, facing across the junction. A white walking figure shows while walkers may start (`walkSig`). An orange hand flashes for the rest of the green and the amber, and holds steady on red. The figure is drawn dot by dot once its dots are a character or more, otherwise the head shows a solid patch of the lamp's colour.
  - Push button: a yellow box on the pole at hand height, facing the kerb.
  The new browser test fails on WALK during the whole green, a pedestrian head facing away, missing cowls, a hand that doesn't flash and a walking figure in orange. The existing signal and traffic tests pass. Median render ABAB vs main: close, corner, junction and avenue all within noise.
- Street lights and utilities (pass12 follow-up), posts through awnings (Claude, `claude/lamp-awning-seams`, from main): a playtest found a traffic signal rising through a Chinatown shop awning on Mercer St. Across the city 72 signals and 3 lamp posts stood inside merchant awnings. The street fitting had moved them clear in the source layout, but the junction signals are rebuilt after the neighbourhoods are laid out (`world-bootstrap.js`) and never fitted again, and three lamps met awnings from another source block at a seam. After assembly, each awning (and Shotengai timber eave) a lamp post or a signal (pole and head) stands in is cut back to clear it by a hand's width: its end pulled in, or its front edge drawn back, whichever keeps more of it. The posts, and the lamps' pools of light, stay where they are; each awning stays fixed to its wall.
  The new browser test (`city-awning-posts`) fails on any lamp or signal in an awning, an awning moved off its wall, an awning cut to nothing, or fewer than 20 cut back. It fails without the signals in the check, without the trim, and when the front edge is drawn back without keeping the awning on its wall. Load-time only; nothing changes per frame.
- Washrooms (pass 2/8 follow-up), doors and the diner (Claude, `claude/washroom-doors`, from main): a playtest found the diner's washroom opening straight onto the end of the serving counter. An audit of all 22 restaurants, bars and shops with washrooms found the wider problem: every pocket door started open, so each toilet faced straight out into its dining room.
  - A washroom you don't live in (restaurants, bars, shops, the terminal, and, as asked, hotel and motel rooms and the hotel lobby) now starts with its door shut. Open it with E as before; it slides shut by itself once you're 1.8 m clear and outside, and never while you're inside or beside it. Your own home, and anyone else's flat, keeps its doors however they were left (saved as before).
  - The diner's washroom now opens south onto the tables instead of east onto the counter's end. Its WC sign moved to the wall beside the new door (the old spot would have shown it on two faces). The left side now has one table, lower down, so the way from the street door to the washroom is clear both on foot and for the cell-based routing pursuing police use.
  - A washroom's partition walls, seen from the room, are now solid painted plaster above the brick wainscot. The rooms' walls are faint dots on dark, so the partitions used to read as empty space with a door frame floating in it.
  `city-bathroom-doors` now checks that public doors start shut, open, stay open while you're inside or beside them, and close once you're clear, across the diner, bar, pizzeria, gas station, hotel lobby and hotel and motel rooms. It checks that homes keep their doors, and that the diner's door faces the tables, away from the counter, with a route to it. The existing door tests now start from a shut door. The route tests for the shops, the terminal and the motel room open the washroom door first, as you would with E. The native-dining test expects the diner's six settings (three tables). It fails on doors starting open, no closing behind you, homes treated as public, and the old diner layout.
- Street utilities (pass12), service hatches (Claude, `claude/service-hatches`, stacked on `claude/street-drains`): cellar hatches set flush in the pavement beside about a third of shop doors (not apartment entrances), against the wall on one side of the door, in `city/street-drains.js`.
  - Each is a pair of diamond-plate leaves 80 by 70 cm in a solid frame, with a dark seam down the middle, a pair of hinges at each end and a lifting ring either side of the seam. It sits 1 to 8 cm out from the wall, so it never reaches the walking line at 12 cm, and skips spots with no wall behind, no pavement under it, a vending machine on it or another door beside it.
  - The diamond plate filters to one even sheen once its 11 cm pattern is smaller than a cell (a 6 mm pattern aliased into noise up close, so it was coarsened after the visual review).
  Median render ABAB vs `claude/street-drains`: looking straight down with a hatch filling the view 5.7 to 5.9 ms became 6.4 to 7.6 ms; a wide pavement view 6.3 to 6.6 became 6.6 to 7.7 (590 cheap lookups, mostly noise); a second hatch and a down-street control within noise. The new browser test fails on a hatch in the walking line, hatches at apartment doors and an unfiltered plate far off.
- Signs and shopfronts (pass13), fascia boards and awnings (Claude, `claude/shop-fascias`): the midtown, downtown, brownstone and residential shops (about 1,180, the architecture families) painted their names flat on the wall with nothing behind them. Each named street face now has a fascia board standing 12 cm off the wall round the same lettering (2,673 boards: 1,462 boards, 607 boxes, 604 painted) (new `fascia` details from `architectureShopfront` in `city/architecture.js`, shaded in `city/architecture-render.js`). The Chinatown and Shotengai merchants already had 3D boards, awnings and blades and are unchanged.
  - Brownstones paint theirs, gold leaf on a dark board (green, blue, grey or black; brick vanished against the brick walls in review). Downtown it's a dark box with the name in the shop's colour, lit from inside after dark while open. Midtown and the apartment blocks use a board in the shop's colour with white letters. Each has a frame round the lettering, and the name reads left to right from the street on all four facings.
  - `architectureSignSpec` holds each family's lettering height, size and band in one place. The painted lettering past `ARCH_FASCIA_VIS` (120 m) and the board up close use the same numbers, so the handover doesn't move or resize the name. Up close the wall behind the board stays bare.
  - 386 brownstone and midtown shopfronts (about 60% of those wide enough; not downtown towers, not apartment blocks with entrance canopies) get a striped awning: a thin sheet sloping from the wall at 3.25 m to 2.75 m over a metre's depth, darker underneath, and a valance with a scalloped hem down to 2.55 m. You walk under it, it stops short of the walking line, and it stays clear of the lamps, trees and vending machines.
  - Boards are skipped where they'd run into a low building's cornice (a 3.8 m gas kiosk keeps its painted name). The hardware shop that becomes the IDEA store after the city is built refits its board to the new name instead of showing the old one.
  The new browser test (`city-shop-fascias`) fails on lettering painted behind the board up close, a board shifted off its lettering, an awning you walk into, lettering on the back face, a board up in a cornice and a renamed shop keeping its old board. `city-storefront-signs` now counts board lettering up close as well as painted lettering. Median render ABAB vs main: square on to a midtown shop with its awning 7.8 to 8.5 ms became 9.2 to 10.2 ms; a brownstone shop 7.0 to 7.5 became 7.9 to 8.2; down a midtown street, down a downtown street and a Docks control within noise.

- Signs and shopfronts (pass13), the generic shopfronts (Claude, `claude/oldpath-shopfronts`, stacked on #248): the shops still on the generic facade in `city/city-render.js` (Docks warehouses and workshops, sty 8; midtown and Docks stucco shops, sty 15; brick shops in the brownstones and the Docks, sty 2) painted their name over and over round every wall, and an awning flat on the wall under it. New `city/shopfronts.js` finds their faces with the architecture face finder (`SHOPFRONT_FACES`, moved by `world-bootstrap.js` with the cell behind each face) and gives each street face one board with the name on it: 671 boards (351 warehouse boards in the shop's colour, 210 lit boxes, 110 painted). Apartments and shops closed for good keep their old fronts.
  - The name is painted once per street face from further off, at the board's height and size; up close the band behind the board is bare, and side and back walls carry no name.
  - 223 open shops get the same striped awning as the architecture families, set a little lower to fit under the generic band (3.1 m sloping to 2.7 m, valance to 2.55 m). The painted awning is gone where there's a real one, leaving the transom. Warehouses get none over their loading doors.
  - From far off only a few rows sample the facade, and the generic code only reached the band for rows above z .32, so the lettering vanished past about 100 m. It now checks from .28 and lets `wallText` pick the row, as the architecture faces do.
  The fascia test now covers the generic faces too. It fails on lettering painted behind the board up close, an awning over a warehouse door, and faces not moved with the city's layout (their far lettering ends up 48 cells away). Median render ABAB vs #248: square on to a Docks diner 7.5 to 7.9 ms became 6.8 to 7.2 ms; a brownstone-district diner 7.0 to 7.7 became 5.8 to 6.4 (the old band sampled glyphs 3 by 3 per cell); a midtown tattoo parlour, a midtown street and an architecture control within noise.

- Signs and shopfronts (pass13), shop window displays (Claude, `claude/shop-windows`, stacked on #249): every shop window was flat painted glass. New `city/shop-windows.js` (`shopWindow`) puts a display bay 70 cm deep behind the glass of the midtown, downtown, brownstone, residential and generic shops when you're within 35 m (`SHOP_WINDOW.near`); further off the windows stay as they were.
  - The bay has a plinth along its floor in the shop's colour (its front darker, in its own shadow), the shop's goods standing on it at 45% depth, and the lit shop beyond with its shelves. Walk past and the goods slide against the back. After dark, while the shop is open, a spotlight shines over each item from the bay's ceiling.
  - What's in the window follows the shop: loaves and cakes for the bakeries, cafes and diners; mannequins for thrift, tailors and shoes; book stacks; glowing screens for video, records and the arcade; bottles for liquor, juice and the drugstore; bunches of flowers on their stems for the florist and herbs; boxed goods for the rest. Heights vary a little item to item.
  - Neon shops keep their glow, apartment lobbies their doors, warehouses their loading doors, and a closed shop its shutter.
  The new browser test (`city-shop-windows`) drives `shopWindow` along chosen rays: plinth front versus top, goods versus the shop beyond, the goods holding still against a sliding back, the side wall at a steep angle and the spotlights after dark. It also checks every family shows displays up close but not from 45 m, and that neon shops and warehouses get none. It fails on no near limit, goods painted on the back, no plinth front, displays in a neon shop, a family missing its displays and no spotlights. Median render ABAB vs #249, two runs: square on to a midtown noodle bar's window 8.3 to 11.1 ms became 9.4 to 10.1 ms; a brownstone florist 6.7 to 7.3 became 7.6 to 9.6; a residential herb shop and a generic record shop +0.2 to 0.8; a midtown street within noise.

Closed porcelain bushings join transformer leads to tapered cans; service brackets meet their actual wall planes and get correct flat normals. Culling includes the arm/drop/guy attachments: close visual review caught a bracket disappearing while looking away from its pole, now covered by an actual raster check. Guy-foot collision follows the sloped wire/guard and low anchor instead of an invisible filled box; placement bounds follow that actual reach. Thirteen independent cache/coordinates/endpoints/bushing/lead/wall/foot/guard/anchor/cull/crossing-height/drop-wall/attachment-cull probes fail correctly. Gullies/vents/tree ironwork stay flush with their native street surfaces and clear of lanes/steps. Rain runoff skips bridge segments where no gully exists. Bar coverage uses a smooth footprint filter and the real45% iron-area average, with rendered tones following partial coverage rather than abruptly switching at one distance. - CI fishing-audio clock repair (Codex): #246 passed Node and browser shards1/3/4; shard2 failed only the bobber-delay assertion because its mocked playback callback subtracted a live audio clock that could advance between scheduling and observation. Reproduced the original failure by stalling that callback40ms. The intercepted-voice fixture now holds one audio timestamp and restores the context's live clock afterward; the same40ms stall passes, as do two clean focused runs. Existing distance-delay, attenuation, stereo, ice-tick and local-cast assertions stay unchanged. Four independent missing-delay/no-attenuation/reversed-pan/centred-ice mutations each fail. Three node bundle/parse/render-cost files pass. Test-only: no runtime/audio/bundle change or render-cost change. #239 is merged as0a35222; next #245 service hatches, then remaining independent/fascia PRs and passes5/6/9.

Hatch indexing covers only the actual plate cells rather than nine neighbouring cells. Crossed diamond ridges filter continuously to their real32.76% area average; the physical seam averages its true width rather than expanding to a dark pixel, and small rings/hinges fade at distance. - #242 review after concurrent #262 integration (Codex): merged731b1c9 into the lamp review, preserving all signals/utilities/fascias/old-path fronts/windows/shutters/doors/awnings/bathroom/wallpaper changes and main's superset manifest order, then rebuilt. The reviewed lamp model and focused regressions remain byte-identical to d7f30d2. The original coarse checkbox is retained under the append-only notes rule; this completion entry is authoritative. Next civic interiors (pass9), beginning with apartment-lobby entry/lift and then remaining mailboxes/walls and the other civic rooms.

- Civic interiors (pass9), apartment-lobby entrances (Codex, codex/apartment-lobby-entrances): replace painted public glass doors and roof lift with the unchanged prepared public fitting builders: closed frames/leaves, grounded thresholds, joined push bars, supported lift controls and front-only scaling EXIT/ROOF plaques. Native grid/collision, existing lobby props/mailboxes, private-home behavior, actual roof travel and both exits remain intact. The clear entrance uses this real apartment building's exterior with room-centred parallax. Three focused geometry/cache/contact/native/view/text/real-roof/E+walking-return browser checks and three Node files pass; five isolated wrong-anchor/floating-lift/old-paint/missing-view/omitted-geometry defects fail assertions. Added nearby detail~1.5–2.3ms; no broad speedup claim. Next the lobby's old wall/mailbox patterns and fitted finishes, then other civic rooms.

Thirteen isolated sill/head/bore/pane/trim/lever/hinge/old-paint/sign/text/context/native-omission/invisible-wire defects fail assertions. Reviewed16 final cell/wide/close/oblique/night/pane/lever/hinge comparisons without errors; macros use actual freecam in clear3D space, keeping the prisoner in the legal cell. Serial ABAB177x66 main9097d37: cell4.0–4.3→4.2–4.3ms, leaf.9–1.0→3.1–3.3, pane.8→3.5–3.6, oblique1.4→2.8–2.9. Added detail costs~0–2.8ms locally, no speedup claim. Next west high window and wall/floor/ceiling finishes, then jail closing review.

- Civic interiors (pass 9), jail west privacy window (Codex, codex/jail-west-window): replace painted strokes with8 cached closed casing/sill/mullion pieces from unchanged HOME_WINDOW_CASING, plus7 prepared recessed privacy-pane/round-bar/plaque parts. Five rods bury into the actual frame; diffuse glazing follows the existing day/night outlook without another exterior scene render. Mounted BLOCK C text scales, restores rotated wall context and preserves every far oblique letter once in the correct order. Three new geometry/cache/support/roundness/recess/native-pane+bar+frame/day-night/text checks, three retained final door controls and three Node files pass. Twelve isolated frame/bar/pane/old-paint/static-weather/native-frame/native-bar/text/context defects fail assertions. Reviewed16 cell/close/wide/oblique/night/sill/bar/plaque comparisons without errors; actual freecam keeps the player in the legal cell. Next wall/floor/ceiling finishes and jail closing review.

- Civic interiors (pass 9), jail wall finishes (Codex, codex/jail-wall-finishes): replace coarse wall glyphs with filtered world-fixed cinder mortar and57 cached grounded mop-base/ceiling strip parts. Unchanged homeWallMoulding uses actual bar/sill/band blockers and the final door cut. Calibrated mortar stays readable across character rows; tally scratches overlay the same surface instead of replacing a rectangular material patch. Two new actual-geometry/cache/fixture-clearance/filter/world-coordinate/native-mortar/native-strip/tally checks, six final door/window controls and three Node files pass. Twelve isolated fitting/material/coordinate/tally/native-draw defects fail assertions; material-sensitive probes were repeated after calibration. Reviewed18 close/wide/oblique/night/tally/base/ceiling/door/window comparisons without errors. Serial ABAB177x66 main6a602d9: wide3.9–4.1→4.2ms, wall1.1→1.4–1.5, base.9–1.0→1.2, ceiling3.5–3.7→3.7–4.0. Added detail costs~0–.5ms locally, no speedup claim. #343 full CI green41001141916. Next jail floor/ceiling/caged lights and closing audit, then gym/laundry/storage/museum. Stopping here at the user's request to conserve weekly usage; concrete resumption notes are appended to AGENT_COORDINATION.md.

- Civic interiors (pass 9), jail floor and lights (Claude, claude/jail-floor-lights): replace the speck-glyph floor and painted ceiling lamps. New `city/jail-floor-lights.js`: filtered painted concrete with 3 m saw cuts and worn patches, a real world-space yellow line whose coverage uses the pixel's extent across the line (an isotropic footprint swelled it into a wedge down the corridor), plain board-marked ceiling, and ten cached caged bulkhead lamps at the old points: steel plate on the ceiling, lit closed glass dome, two crossed wire hoops and a ring, wire ends joined or buried in the plate. `jailFloor`/`jailCeil` and their switch cases are gone; the room keeps `floor: 'jail'`, so footsteps are unchanged. Three browser checks cover glyph-free filtering, footsteps, the line's width and continuity in the actual view, lamp placement, plate/cage joints, dome inside the cage, headroom and lit glass in view; seven isolated source mutations each fail them. Serial ABAB at 177×66 against main 5c995cd: corridor 5.2-5.3→4.6-5.4 ms, cell 3.5→2.9-3.1, ceiling 6.2-6.7→3.4-3.5, wide 5.8→4.8; the old ceiling painter looped over every lamp for every pixel. Local observations, not an FPS guarantee.

- Civic interiors (pass 9), jail closing audit (Claude): first jail interior quality pass complete after #338-#344 and the floor/lights chunk. Reviewed twelve final views, day and night: cell front, inside a cell, corridor both ways, desk, exit door, west window, oblique bars and a lamp, with no page errors and nothing painted in view. Not changed: `jail.js` still holds superseded painters behind the fitted wrappers, and `city-jail-guard-desk`'s 'desk appears from the legal jail cell' check fails on this Windows machine on exact main as well as this branch, because from 9 m the desk changes only 0-1 cells against its `changed>2` threshold; CI has passed it. Remaining pass 9 rooms: gym, laundry, storage, museum.

- Checklist audit of passes 4, 5 and 6 (Claude): ticked after checking Codex's closing audits against current main (a0a5a8d) rather than on their wording. The 147 home, hotel, motel and washroom browser tests were rerun: 146 pass, and the one failure (`city-home-french-doors` casing-head) depends on the character grid, not the game: the head is drawn at every width tried and the test passes at 1170 and 1400 wide. Reviewed day, night and reverse views of the studio, loft, Belle home, all three hotel room tiers and the motel, hotel and Grand lobbies, with no page errors. Also ticked the two 27 October resume items: `city-home-refresh-structure` and the skirting and panelling suites pass. Scope notes: 'switches' means the rotary switch on each lamp and its E control, as homes have no wall switches; homes have no radio fixture. Known remainder left to its own pass: lobby signs drop to mixed large and small letters from an oblique angle (standard HOTEL board), which belongs with the reserved global lettering. The 26 October reported-fix boxes above stay unticked: their fix notes exist, but this audit did not recheck them.

- Civic interiors (pass 9), gym shell (Claude, claude/gym-shell): replaces the speck floor, painted strip ceiling, glyph mirrors, wainscot and painted name. New `city/gym-shell.js`: rubber mats with filtered 1m joints, plain ceiling with six fitted battens, plaster walls with a real-coverage orange band, calm tinted mirrors on both side walls with closed aluminium rails, end bars and joint bars from one shared layout, a mounted board for the name and slogan, the prepared paired entrance and a fitted base. The glass does not reflect the room; real reflections are not attempted. Footsteps still resolve as carpet. Native grid, keeper, ring and treadmill positions are unchanged. Three browser tests; fourteen isolated mutations all fail them. Removed floor flecks and a mirror sheen after review: both quantised into blotches on the character grid. Serial ABAB, 177x66, exact main, local observations and not an FPS guarantee: entry 2.9-3.1 to 4.4-4.5 ms, west 2.2-2.3 to 2.3-2.4, ceiling 1.7-1.8 to 1.5-1.6, wide 3.2-3.3 to 4.4-4.6 (one noisy first baseline run excluded). The added cost is the fixtures, and the old room was unfinished black.

- Civic interiors (pass 9), gym equipment (Claude, claude/gym-equipment): `city/gym-equipment.js` replaces the glyph treadmill, dumbbell rack, ring and front desk, and removes the old builders from `city/rooms.js` and `city/gym.js`. Treadmills are closed decks with a moving filtered belt, uprights buried in the deck and console, and a lit screen with a session bar; runners stand exactly on the belt. Dumbbell heads rest on the rack rails, with size set by bay and tier. Ring ropes end inside the corner pads, posts stand on filtered canvas, and the printed roundel fades before grazing views can fragment it. Ring collision now reaches the post tops, so you can no longer jump onto the canvas: E is the way in. The desk is the fitted service counter on the old footprint. A heavy bag hangs on a joined chain, because the trainer's line already promises one. The sports shop's treadmill and rack get the same models. Four browser tests; sixteen isolated mutations all fail them; shop and activity browser suites 13/13. Serial ABAB against the shell commit at 177x66, local observations and not an FPS guarantee: entry 4.2-4.5 to 4.2-4.3 ms, racks 2.6-2.7 to 1.8, ring 2.5-2.8 to 2.4-2.6, wide 4.3-5.1 to 5.8-5.9.

- Civic interiors (pass 9), gym closing audit (Claude): first gym interior quality pass complete with the shell and equipment chunks. Reviewed twelve final views, day and night: door, ring, treadmills, racks, bag, desk, exit, north-east, west and south-west, with no page errors. The trainer, ring prompt, fight and session paths still pass through the existing gym gameplay test. Remainders left to their own passes: mirrors do not reflect (not attempted); the board's slogan mixes letter sizes obliquely (reserved global lettering); the sports shop's own walls and clothes rail remain glyph work for pass 7.

- Civic interiors (pass 9), laundromat and closing audit (Claude, claude/laundry-quality): the machines were painted on the walls; `city/laundry-quality.js` stands them in the room. Ten washers on the bays `machineHere()` already counts, each with cabinet, plinth, console, coin plate, status lamp and a real round porthole (new small `laundryPortRay`: flat face and rim). The drum reads the wash state when drawn: your load tumbles white with a yellow lamp, then shows clean with a blinking green one; other machines run or sit idle and their loads settle to a steady colour with distance. Eight stacked dryers on the side walls are scenery with no prompt. Also a wire cart on castors, the fitted folding counter, vinyl check, tile and plaster walls, four battens, a base, the paired entrance and a mounted board. `wallTitleBoard`, `wordEntranceExterior` and `civicPlaster` are now shared with the gym. Behaviour changes: the old bay half inside the west wall no longer answers E (no machine stands there), the board's tube no longer flickers, and the folding table moved 50cm west so the route stays clear. The walk-up test caught the east dryers walling off machine 11; they moved 25cm so every machine is reachable. Three browser tests; 20 of 21 isolated mutations fail them, the survivor being a footstep line that changed nothing and is now deleted. Existing laundromat gameplay test passes. Reviewed nine views, day and night, with no page errors. Serial ABAB against the prior chunk at 177x66, local observations and not an FPS guarantee: door 3.1-3.2 to 4.8-4.9 ms, washers 2.0-2.1 to 2.5, dryers 2.4 to 2.9-3.1, north-east 2.8-2.9 to 4.8-5.1. Remainder: the pocket at the west end of the machine row is plain wall.

- Civic interiors (pass 9), self storage and closing audit (Claude, claude/storage-quality): `city/storage-quality.js` replaces the glyph shutters, floor, ceiling and sign. Shutters moved from 1.2m bays to one whole door on each exposed locker face (60 doors, on the 1m cells), so none is cut at a block corner. Each door has filtered wide ribs and a real cached handle and padlock; outer walls are now painted block rather than more shutters, and the attendant's counter is the fitted service counter on its old footprint. Also concrete with filtered saw cuts, nine battens over the aisles, a base, the paired entrance and a mounted board. The doors still don't open: your unit is reached through the attendant, as before. Rib pitch went from 9cm to 20cm with an early fade, because the finer ribs beat against the character rows into moire. Two browser tests; 15 of 18 isolated mutations fail them, and of the survivors two were redundant lines now deleted and one exposed a loose lock check now tightened. Reviewed nine views, day and night, with no page errors. Serial ABAB against the prior chunk at 177x66, local observations and not an FPS guarantee, with noisy baselines: door 2.7-3.1 to 2.8 ms, aisle 2.2-3.4 to 2.0-2.9, cross 2.6-3.4 to 2.3-3.7, far end 2.2-2.4 to 1.9-2.8, so no measurable added cost. Remainder: unit numbers use the shared large block lettering up close (reserved global lettering).

- Civic interiors (pass 9), museum shell (Claude, claude/museum-shell): `city/museum-shell.js` replaces every wing's glyph walls and floors. Egypt keeps its carved bands as filtered tinted reliefs that settle to a flat band at distance, on sandstone paving; the gallery's canvases are glyph-free and framed in closed gold parts with a plaque card; gems stay velvet and carpet; the hall has marble with a filtered chequer that goes to its average once a pixel spans most of a square, and basket parquet returns in the gallery. Twelve battens, skirting, cornices and the paired entrance by day. Night keeps the heist's darkness: no battens or pale trim, ceiling, frames and walls dim, and beams still land on a glyph-free floor. Fixed an old bug: a painting's seed used the wall cell, so canvases had a vertical seam where they crossed a cell; it now seeds from the wall line and bay. The closing review then caught a second seam of my own: at integer wall positions the face test picked the wrong axis and dropped a column of marble through the picture; it now reads the face from the wall cell, with a real-view check across the canvas. A painting now exists only where its whole span lies on one gallery wall. Three browser tests; 16 of 17 isolated mutations failed them on the first pass, and the survivor exposed a too-weak test now tightened. The existing day and heist gameplay test passes. Serial ABAB against the prior chunk at 177x66, local observations and not an FPS guarantee, first baseline noisy: door 2.8 to 3.2 ms, hall 2.1 to 2.6, egypt 2.7 to 2.6-2.7, gallery 3.0-3.1 to 3.1, gems 3.0 to 2.8-2.9. The T. rex, Anubis, sarcophagus, cases and ropes are still sprites and glyphs: next chunk.

- Civic interiors (pass 9), museum exhibits and closing audit (Claude, claude/museum-exhibits): `city/museum-exhibits.js` replaces the ASCII sprites. The T. rex is one joined skeleton of about 55 cached parts: spine, ribs, pelvis, legs, arms, a hinged jaw and steel armature, all inside the plinth footprint, with feet on the plinth. Anubis is a modelled statue, the sarcophagus has a banded lid and a mask, and each case is a pedestal, a clear steel-edged glass box and the piece: a brilliant-cut Star on a velvet cushion, or an Orrery whose planets really orbit. Ropes sag between brass stanchions, and a stolen piece leaves a STOLEN card. Everything dims with `museumLit()`. The rex plinth, sarcophagus and pedestal colliders keep their old heights, because the guards' sight check reads them and bones and glass must not hide you. Behaviour changes: Anubis now has a real collider, so it can block a guard's sight, and it moved 60cm nearer the wall; velvet ropes stay walk-through because the heist needs the cases. Three browser tests; all 24 isolated mutations fail them; the museum day and heist test passes. Reviewed eighteen views across the shell and exhibit chunks, day and night, with no page errors. Serial ABAB against the prior chunk at 177x66, local observations and not an FPS guarantee: door 3.3-3.4 to 3.4-3.6 ms, rex 2.9-3.5 to 2.9, egypt 2.4 to 3.0, gems 2.8-2.9 to 3.2, orrery 2.4 to 2.9 (with one noisy high run per view for the last three).

- Pass 9 closing audit (Claude): hospital, bank, jail, gym, laundry, storage, museum and apartment lobbies all have completed first passes. Reran all 30 civic browser suites on the final tree: 84 pass, and the one failure is the known grid-dependent `city-jail-guard-desk` check, which passes in CI. Pass 9 is ticked. Known remainder, left to its own passes: large block lettering on boards and unit numbers (reserved global lettering) and non-reflecting gym mirrors.

- Shared floors (passes 7, 8 and 10, Claude, claude/shared-floors): a scan of every room showed civic rooms, homes and hotels near 0% glyphs but shops and restaurants at 10-50%, with `tile` and `wood` floors alone 55% glyphs. Root-cause fix: `city/shared-floors.js` replaces the `tile`, `wood`, `concrete` and `carpet` painters in one place, across about 40 room definitions. Tile checks fade to their average once a pixel spans most of a tile, concrete has filtered saw cuts, carpet keeps its loud flecks up close and goes plain before they shimmer, and wood is the fitted loft board. Style names are unchanged, so footsteps and room definitions are too. Two browser tests sample twelve real rooms; eleven of twelve isolated mutations failed them, and the survivor exposed a weak grout check, since tightened. Walls and ceilings of the same rooms are still glyph fields: next chunk. The per-room passes remain open.

- Shared walls and ceilings (passes 7, 8 and 10, Claude, claude/shared-walls-ceilings): `city/shared-walls.js` replaces what every room's walls fall back to (a panelled timber dado, a rail and plaster, posters as plain sheets, the neon band), the plain glass door, and the shared `pendant`, `disco` and `dark` ceilings. Any `strip` room now gets the fitted battens, not only listed ones; the station and trains keep their own tubes. With the shared floors, the mean glyph share across the 107 rooms scanned fell from 17.4% to 7.4%. Rooms' own wall painters still use glyphs where they take over, and those remain per-room work: the aviary, conservatory, courier, cafe and lamp room lead the list. Behaviour changes: disco spots are now 1m patches whose colours step as a whole, and the door's mullion and head are flat paint. Two browser tests; 14 of 15 isolated mutations failed them, and the survivor exposed a weak rail check, since tightened. `city-interior-ceiling` passes 4/4 and the shop suite passes. Serial ABAB of both shared chunks together against the commit before them, 177x66, local observations and not an FPS guarantee: store 7.9-8.0 to 7.7-7.9 ms, diner 5.2-5.3 to 4.9-5.4, cafe 5.2-5.8 to 5.3-5.9, barber 4.9-5.1 to 4.6, garage 3.7 to 4.8-4.9 (it gained fitted battens), arcade 4.5 to 4.3-4.4, bar 7.0-7.1 to 6.8-7.0, books 6.7-6.9 to 7.3-7.4, grocer 9.8-9.9 to 9.6-9.7, karaoke 4.4 to 4.4-4.5. No broad speedup claim; only garage and books measurably cost more.

- Grid-dependent tests (Claude): `city-jail-guard-desk` and `city-home-french-doors` passed in CI but failed on some character grids, because each needed a thin or mostly hidden part to land on exact cells from one exact camera. Neither was a game bug. The desk check now looks from several legal spots at the cell front, and the door check nudges a few rows either side of the part. From inside, the door's head is meant to sit behind the room-side surround head, so that one case now asserts the head draws and the surround covers it. Both still fail if the part really goes.

- Shop interiors (pass 7), wall painters and remaining shared styles (Claude, claude/shop-walls): every specialist shop's wall painter loses its glyph fields, keeping its layout and character: jeweller mirrors and gilt, tobacco cigar shelves and packets, deli tile and chalkboards, thrift and antiques pictures, tattoo flash, koi mural and ink, tyre hazard stripes and block, florist vines, pet tanks and fish, pawn shelves, toy boxes, sports jerseys, shoes and rackets, welding steel, tailor bolts and pinstripe, gacha characters, herb drawers and jars, record posters, and the shared front window. Fine repeats filter or settle to a flat tone with distance. Also the `checker`, `lino`, `court`, `kids`, `boards`, `hinoki`, `oak`, `saltillo` and `gloss` floors, `tin`, `spots` and chandelier ceilings, and the shared bathroom tile. Behaviour changes: jersey numbers are white patches rather than digits, tattoo flash designs are blocks of ink rather than symbols, and the pawn shop's WE BUY GOLD and the jeweller's streaks no longer flicker or glint. Two browser tests; all 15 isolated mutations fail them. With props removed, the 18 shops tested show no painted marks beyond lettering. Serial ABAB against main at 177x66, local observations and not an FPS guarantee: jeweller 2.0 to 1.5-1.6 ms, tobacco 4.9 to 4.5-4.6, deli 3.8-4.0 to 3.8-3.9, tattoo 5.7-5.8 to 5.3-5.5, tyre 4.4-4.5 to 4.2-4.3, florist 5.8-5.9 to 5.6, sports 4.4-4.5 to 4.2-4.3, thrift 5.5 to 4.9-5.0. Mean glyph share across 107 rooms is now 6.1%. Props still painted with glyphs (gacha machines, florist pots, tyre stacks, bicycles, ball bins) are next.

- Shop interiors (pass 7), remaining walls and props, and closing audit (Claude, claude/shop-props): the last wall painters (liquor, video, hardware, market hall, grocer stockroom, gas, pharmacy, drugstore, phones, realty, barber, bodega, fruit) lose their glyphs, along with wine, bottle, VHS, phone-case and box shelves and the gas station's view out of its window. Props too: pot plants, bouquets, tyre stacks, gacha machines, ball bins, the humidor and cold-room doors, fitting-room curtains and the video store's bead curtain. The shared `solid` box finish now uses tones for panels and trim in every room that uses it, and ignores its old `top` glyph. Behaviour change: the cold-room door's frost specks are gone, leaving clear glass and the COLD BEER sign no longer flickers. All shop suites rerun on this tree, with results in the PR; 31 shops in `city-shop-walls` show no painted marks beyond lettering with their props removed. Mean glyph share across 106 rooms is 5.7%, and shops now run from 0.1% to 4.3%. Pass 7 is ticked as a first quality pass. Known remainders: lettering on shop signs (reserved global lettering), a few thin rods drawn as line glyphs (bicycles, clothes rails, pendant cords), which is the shared sub-cell line technique rather than a painted field, and small goods inside display cases.

- Restaurants, cafes and bars (pass 8), walls and closing audit (Claude, claude/restaurant-walls): all 27 restaurant wall painters lose their glyphs, keeping their layouts, murals, signs and menus. Cafe brick, bagel subway tile, roastery white brick and kebab tile get filtered joints through a shared `coursedJoint`; the rest keep their colours and lose fine detail such as panel lines, sprinkles and hand-painted marks, which is a real simplification. The karaoke room's glitter becomes dark plum wall, and dim sum carpet medallions now fade before they break up. Earlier chunks in this pass already fitted the fixtures, lamps, tables, counters and paper lanterns. DINER stayed reserved and untouched: its scan was already 0.1%. In `city-shop-walls`, 58 shops and restaurants now show no painted marks beyond sign and menu punctuation with their props removed. Pass 8 is ticked as a first quality pass. Known remainders: reserved global lettering, disco ceiling spots that streak at grazing angles, and thin line-glyph rods. Not timed separately; restaurant rooms only lost glyph drawing.

- Global sign lettering (Claude, claude/sign-lettering, at the user's request: letters should fill their sign, small text only when necessary): block letters now show four font pixels per screen cell using 15 new block cells drawn as exact rectangles in both renderers, so a letter stays whole down to 2 cells by 2.5 rows instead of garbling near that size. Below that, `wallText` and `signLine` set the word tight on one row, centred, instead of spreading its letters over the sign's width; columns along an angled wall use the exact integral so no letter is lost. Each `wallText` sign decides its size once per view, so oblique signs no longer split into large and small, and each tight word is fixed per view so a column shows exactly one letter (CI's different screen grid caught both a lost letter and a repeated one before this). Fill: title boards fit both lines to the board (`wallTitleBoard`), the bank name fills a taller board, hotel EXIT and ROOF plaques use their height, room titles are 25% larger, and shop fascias grow from about 40cm to 1m lettering, with the board standing proud of the string course. A word like LAUNDRY now reads in blocks from across the street. The reserved global lettering is now Claude's. Tests that pinned `#` or spread-out letters were updated to assert the new behaviour. New `city-sign-lettering` browser test: 12 of 13 isolated mutations fail it, the survivor being a redundant bounds check now deleted, after two weak checks were tightened. Full CI is green. Serial ABAB against main at 177x66, local observations and not an FPS guarantee: hotel 4.0-4.2 to 4.1 ms, gym 5.8-6.2 to 5.9-6.1, bank 4.8 to 4.7-4.8, burger 6.4-6.5 to 6.2-6.6, tattoo 6.2-6.3 to 6.1, gas 8.1-8.7 to 8.4-8.6; street views 9.6-9.7 to 9.7-9.9, 6.2-6.6 to 6.5-6.6 and 8.5-8.7 to 8.4-9.0. No broad speedup claim. Remainders: signs drawn by their own routines (graffiti, ticker tapes, the stock board and the old `oneCell` paths in `city-render.js`) keep their current lettering; the font is still 3x5, so M, W, N and H stay similar; and real lamps can hang in front of a title.

- Coast and islands (pass 19), Cormorant Rock (Claude, at the user's request: real birds, better structures and textures, nests of black birds on the spire tops, birds circling): the two flat-topped boxes become five sea stacks along the seaward ridge (`CORMORANT_SPIRES` in `islands.js`, built in `island-sights.js`), each three or four stepped tiers of eight-sided frusta with weathered face offsets, every tier standing on the one below and stepping back to leave a ledge. Their planes are the drawn surface and the walking, aircraft and shot collision. The stack material (`islandStackShade`) has warped bedding planes with filtered partings, vertical joints, small lichen spots low down, white rims and guano runs down each face from every ledge, and whitewashed tops; it fades with daylight like the ground. Twig nests (revolved heaped rims round a hollow, white-splashed) crowd every summit and the wider ledges, half sat, a third with their owner standing beside them. The cormorant is a new animal body (`ANIMAL_BODIES.cormorant`): black with bronze wings, a hooked grey bill, a yellow throat patch, a hunched stance with the tail propped behind, a wings-out drying pose, a sitting pose, flapping and gliding flight with the neck straight out. Sixteen birds, mostly cormorants with a few gulls, circle each of the three tallest stacks just above its top. A figure covering under about half a cell isn't drawn (people far off don't flicker), so beyond a few dozen metres a bird is painted as a few solid cells at its true place and size (body plus wings that rise and fall, or a short upright when perched) instead of vanishing; nothing is drawn in characters. The rock's ground loses its 'o'/':' gravel for bare jointed grey stone with a wet tide band, whitewash round the stacks, a worn path, lichen and sea-pink cushions. Two existing checks encoded the old layout and were updated: the roost now expects at least ten stack birds instead of exactly two, and the shared shot check fires at an obstacle from the side its ground falls away (two stacks stand on the seaward slope, where a level shot from uphill starts underground). New `city-cormorant-rock` browser test (4): tiers taper, step back, stand exactly on each other and block walking; three or more nests on every summit, each fully on rock and not inside a tier above, birds sitting in their nests, the cormorant's six poses with wings spread to dry; circlers round a stack above its top and moving, the flock painting the sky from the landing (and nothing when removed); stack faces varied with guano and no glyphs, the ground mostly bare rock with no gravel glyphs. All four fail on main. Island, wildlife, coast and fixture suites (69) and bundle/render/terrain/world Node checks (47) pass; the Fern Ridge telescope still clears the ridge to the stacks. Reviewed landing, path, close, offshore, raised aerial, summit and night views. Local medians of 61 frames at 161×66, ABAB main → branch: landing 7.1–7.3 → 7.3–8.1 ms, close 4.8–7.0 → 5.1–6.4, offshore 6.6–6.9 → 7.0–7.4, raised aerial 8.0–8.9 → 9.5–11.5; Fern control 7.9–10.4 → 7.6–9.3. Local observations, not an FPS guarantee.

- Skate shop quarter pipe (Claude, claude/skate-shop-quarter-pipe, at the user's request: an actual ramp, not a staircase): the twelve flat-topped plywood slices became one curved surface. New `quarterPipeRay` in `city/shops.js` traces the ramp's box less a cylinder along its width, so the riding face is a true 1.25 m radius arc from the floor to the coping, with a continuous normal. The face is plain birch ply, darker as it steepens, with faint sheet joins that fade with distance; the side panels and deck lose their `#` and `=` glyphs. Footprint, coping and deck are unchanged. Skating indoors is still off. New `city-skate-shop-ramp` browser test drops rays along the ramp and requires every height on the arc, strictly rising, the curved face, normals turning from up to steep, slope shading, and a side ray meeting the curve rather than a riser.
- Leisure interiors (pass 10, Claude, claude/aviary-quality), the aviary: was 69% glyphs, now none. New `city/aviary-quality.js`. The mesh is no longer an `x` checker: steel posts every 2 m stand on a concrete kerb up to a roof rail, with rails every 1.75 m and roof ribs on a 2 m grid, all built and drawn as rods so they keep at least a cell's width and stay continuous at distance. Between them, the planting and sky outside are seen dim and cool through the mesh, which is an average rather than a pattern. The grass, gravel path, pond and its ring of flat stones are plain surfaces whose mottle and ripples fade with distance. The four sprite trees are modelled at their old spots: a tapering trunk (`aviaryTrunkRay`, a true cone) with collision, limbs from it into a crown of seven leaf clumps lit by their normals and mottled up close. Birds, the keeper, seed and the pond's collision are unchanged, and footsteps still resolve as `aviary`. New `city-aviary-quality` browser test: under 1% glyphs from five views by day and night with no sprite trees; trunk taper, collision and top; limbs from the trunk ending in the leaves; crowns up off the ground and clear of the pond; leaves lit from above; the frame's posts on the walls, ribs across the roof, walk-through, and the pond still blocking. Restoring the sprite trees or removing the taper fails it. The Botanical Gardens, aviary bird and shaped-animal tests pass. Render cost, warmed medians of 61 frames at 161×66, two ABAB runs, main → this branch: door view 4.0–4.7 → 3.8–4.1 ms, corner under a crown 4.1–4.3 → 5.2–5.7, looking up 3.4–3.7 → 3.5–3.6; long rails are drawn in 1.2 m lengths so each rod's screen bounds stay tight. Local observations, not an FPS guarantee. Remaining in the aviary: the keeper's counter and the birds are already built; the trees here are the first modelled trees in the game, and pass 18 can reuse or replace them.
- Leisure interiors (pass 10, Claude, claude/conservatory-shell), the conservatory shell: with its plants taken out the glasshouse was 42% glyphs, now 0.1% (its sign lettering). In `city/gardens.js`, edited in place because tests call these painters directly. White glazing bars every 1.2 m with transoms every 1.5 m stand on the brick plinth, and ribs cross the roof on a 2 m grid (`CONS_FRAME`, built and drawn as rods so they keep at least a cell's width and stay continuous lines at distance; none cross the waterfall's rock). Between them: leaves pressed to the glass on the tropical side, the city's towers on the desert side with lit windows as colour rather than dots, and the sky. The waterfall falls in narrow strands at their own speeds, bright where it meets the pool and still water-coloured there, so the existing continuity test holds. The plinth has filtered brick courses; sand ripples, pool, rocks, the boardwalk's boards, moss and drifting mist are plain surfaces whose detail fades with distance; the roof's vines are colour, kept to the conservatory. The divider displays keep their artwork and lettering, as colour only, identical from both sides. Footsteps still resolve as `conservatory`. New `city-conservatory-quality` browser test: under 0.5% glyphs (lettering aside) from seven views by day and night with the plants out; bars on the walls, none across the falls, roof ribs, boardwalk free and pool blocked; strands that move and calm with distance. Putting painted roof ribs back, or bars over the falls, fails it. The waterfall, display, Botanical Gardens and conservatory animal tests pass. Render cost, warmed medians at 161×66, two ABAB runs, main → this branch: door 4.6–5.5 → 4.1–4.3 ms, desert 4.2–4.4 → 4.0–4.1, palms 4.7–4.8 → 3.7–4.4, with long rails drawn in 1.2 m lengths (one rod down a whole wall seen obliquely tests its entire screen rectangle and cost 7–8 ms). Local observations, not an FPS guarantee. Next: the plants (palms, ferns, bananas, saguaros, barrels, agaves and rocks) are still ASCII sprites, 13% of the full view.

- Leisure interiors (pass 10, Claude, claude/conservatory-plants), the conservatory planting: the last ASCII sprites in the glasshouse are modelled, and with everything in, the room is 0.1% glyphs (its sign lettering). New `city/conservatory-plants.js` builds each plant like the animals and people (figures.js): parts cast into a cached sprite from the side you see, painted as solid colour, so they share the people's baseline look and cost. Palms are slim leaning trunks in four tapering pieces with a crown of nine arching fronds, ferns are low rosettes, bananas have a pseudostem, broad leaves and a hanging bunch, saguaros a column with two upturned arms and a flower, barrel cacti a squat globe crowned in magenta, agaves a rosette of stiff pointed leaves, and the rocks are two-lump boulders. Every plant keeps its old spot and size and is turned by its own seed. Palms, bananas, saguaros, barrels and rocks block at their trunks; ferns and agaves stay walk-through. The lizard still basks on its rock, whose top is unchanged. `PLANT_ART` is removed. `city-conservatory-quality` gains a fourth test: counts per kind, no sprites, collision by kind, nothing in the pool, each in its own house, drawn as shaped models at their heights, the lizard on its rock, and under 1% glyphs from five views. Dropping the palms' collision fails it. The Botanical Gardens test asserted `||` characters as its evidence of cacti; it now checks for the modelled saguaros in the desert house and green in view. Render cost, warmed medians at 161×66, two ABAB runs, shell → this branch: door 4.0–4.7 → 4.9 ms, desert 4.2–4.5 → 4.4–4.5, palms 3.8–4.2 → 4.3–4.4. Local observations, not an FPS guarantee.

- Leisure interiors (pass 10, Claude, claude/cathedral-shell), the cathedral shell: 17% glyphs, now 0.3% (the chandeliers' chains, built in an earlier chunk). In `city/cathedral.js`. The arcade's fourteen square painted piers are now round clustered pillars (`CATH_PILLAR`): a core with four shafts traced together up to the vault, a stepped base on the floor and a gilt capital and abacus at 7 m, lit by their normals, with the same 1 m collision. Coursed stone, oak panelling, the doors' boards, the organ pipes (rounded, their mouths darker), the tower door, the stations of the cross, the reredos' niches and saints, the window and rose tracery, and the glass leading are colour only, with joints and leading filtered so they thin out with distance instead of breaking up. The vault keeps its ribs, bosses and gold stars: drawn a little wide and kept to a cell, because the vault is always far off and at a slant and true-width ribs filter away to nothing. Flagstones keep their joints and their alternating tones settle to one far off, so they no longer stripe; the runner has a gold border. The sun's coloured pools are unchanged. Footsteps still resolve as `cathedral`. New `city-cathedral-shell` browser test: under 0.2% glyphs from six views by day and night; the pillars' count, collision, core and shafts to the vault, base on the floor, capital, roundness and normals; leading thinning, flagstones settling, and the vault keeping its ribs and stars. Keeping the flagstones' alternation at all distances fails it. The cathedral visit and stair tests and the leisure fittings suite pass. Render cost, warmed medians at 161×66, two ABAB runs, main → this branch: doors 5.3–5.7 → 6.0–6.2 ms, aisle 4.9–5.3 → 5.4–5.5, looking up 3.4–4.1 → 3.7–3.8, sanctuary 4.5–4.7 → 5.4–5.6. Local observations, not an FPS guarantee.

- Leisure interiors (pass 10, Claude, claude/aquarium-shell), the aquarium shell: 12% glyphs, now 0.4% (tank and gallery names). In `city/aquarium.js`, the fish logic untouched. The tanks' cabinets are panelled with a trim, the hoods trimmed, and inside the glass the bubbles, swaying kelp, coral heads (lighter toward their tips), seagrass, rocks and sand are colour instead of `o ( ) Y * @ % & | # . ,`; the surface ripples as a moving bright band and the specks and motes are gone. The hall's wave frieze is a filtered band that moves, the plain walls lose their dots, the carpet's caustics are lighter patches instead of `~`, the downlights are small cyan discs at least a cell wide, and the tunnel's ribs are kept to a cell's width far off (its 8 m length means they mostly showed anyway). The facade outside is untouched (pass 26). New `city-aquarium-shell` browser test: under 0.2% glyphs, lettering aside, from seven views at three times; a reef tank's water, sand, coral and bubbles as colour; a surface that changes over time; kelp; tunnel ribs. The tank, touch pool, gift sign, fish species and Velvet Rope/aquarium fittings tests pass. Render cost, warmed medians at 161×66, two ABAB runs, main → this branch: door 5.8–8.1 → 5.5–6.6 ms, tunnel 3.7–4.0 → 3.6–5.9, reef 6.3–8.4 → 5.3–5.5 (noisy). Local observations, not an FPS guarantee.

- Leisure interiors (pass 10, Claude, claude/casino-shell), the casino shell: 11% glyphs, now 0.6% with everything in (the chandelier and cage wires from the fittings chunk) and none in the shell. In `city/casino.js`. Gold pilasters, the dado's gold beads, the rails and the mirrors' twinkles are colour, filtered with distance. The carpet keeps its gold lattice and diamonds up close; where the lines would be narrower than a cell, which only breaks them into dashes, they warm the red instead. The chandelier ceiling was already glyph-free. Footsteps still resolve as `casino`. New `city-casino-shell` browser test: under 0.2% glyphs with the props out from four views; each wall band's colour; a gold-and-red carpet up close that's plain red far off. The casino games, break-in and leisure fittings tests pass. - Leisure interiors (pass 10, Claude, claude/cinema-club-arcade), cinema, arcade and Velvet Rope walls: the last painted wall glyphs in pass 10's rooms. The cinema's movie is colour only, still moving through its four colour reels, and the red curtains fold softly up close and settle to plain red far off (`city/rooms.js`). The arcade's neon zigzag is a band of colour kept at least a cell wide at any distance, on plain dark walls without the old dots. The Velvet Rope's chasing light strip is colour, its mirrors glint only up close, and its velvet is buttoned on a diamond grid whose buttons and tufts settle into it far off; the velvet is a level brighter so the buttons show at all (`city/stripclub.js`). The arcade's cabinet screens still show their games' characters: that's the games, not the room. With the props out each room is under 0.2% glyphs; with them in, the cinema is 0%, the club 0.6% and the arcade 1.6% (its screens). New `city-leisure-walls` browser test: glyph shares; a moving movie; curtain folds near and plain far; the zigzag present at 1, 6 and 12 m; a moving chase strip; buttons near and plain velvet far.
