- Pass 10 closing audit (Claude): arcade, casino, cinema, club, aquarium, conservatory, aviary and cathedral all have completed first passes, with fittings from #233-era chunks and shells and planting from #366-#372 (and the skate shop's ramp, #365). Scanned on main after the last merge, with everything in: cinema 0%, aviary 0%, conservatory 0.1%, cathedral 0.3%, aquarium 0.4%, casino 0.6%, club 0.6%, arcade 1.4%; with props out, all at or under 0.3%. The remainders are lettering, the chandelier, cage and stage wires from the fittings chunk, and the arcade's game screens, which are the games. All eight pass 10 suites (21 tests) pass on main; day and night views of the cathedral and glasshouses were reviewed with no page errors. Pass 10 is ticked. Known remainders: the aviary's trees are the game's first modelled trees and pass 18 can adopt or replace them; wires drawn by `rod()` still use its line glyphs everywhere, a global question; the aquarium's facade outside is pass 26.

- Checklist audit, 26 October fixes and passes 11 and 12 (Claude): ticked after checking them on current main rather than on their wording. The six 26 October rows were implemented by Codex (stable shop-section wall materials, taco and coffee washroom approaches, street entrance and stair clearance, the aquarium gift-shop sign, the walk-up roof landing; their notes are under "Completed chunks (continued)") but never ticked; `city-shop-wall-materials`, `city-washroom-access`, `city-street-entrances`, `city-aquarium-gift-sign` and `city-walkup-landing` pass, 16 tests. Pass 11's list (hydrants, bins, mailboxes, bollards, drains and grates, utility cabinets, shelters, vending machines; benches from pass 1) and pass 12's (lamps, signals, downpipes and meters, service hatches, pole lines) were completed by #236 and #238–#246, all merged, with the closing notes in `AGENT_COORDINATION_ARCHIVE.md`. On main, `city-kerb-fixtures`, `city-street-drains`, `city-street-vending`, `city-street-lamps`, `city-street-signals` and `city-facade-utilities` pass, 30 tests, and none of those modules paints a character. Street views by day and night show them built; the street around them is still heavily glyph-painted, but that is roads (16), facades (15), signs (13) and planting (18), which are later passes. Next: pass 13 (signs and shopfronts).
- Roads and ground (pass 16, Claude, claude/road-surface), road and pavement surfaces: the street was a `.` dither on every other cell, with `,` pavements, `=` zebra crossings, yellow `=` centre dashes and `+` plaza joints. New `city/road-surface.js`, called from `floorBaseCell` in `city/city-render.js`: asphalt is plain colour; pavements are 60 cm slabs in a running bond with filtered joints; a kerb line runs along the pavement edge; the centre dashes and zebra stripes are paint that settles to a tint once a cell spans a stripe, so they don't break up far off; plazas keep their 5 m flags with filtered joints. Surfaces carry their own brightness, a little darker at night so the streetlamps' pools (a warm tint now, not added dots) read. Bridges keep their plates and railings for pass 17; parks, gardens and the causeway are pass 18. In three street views the share of characters on screen fell from 58–64% to 26–33% (facades, signs and planting, later passes). Manholes, gratings, puddles, snow, chalk and fire and muzzle light are unchanged. New `city-road-surface` browser test: no characters on street floor cells from three views; pavement, kerb and asphalt in order of brightness; centre dashes near and none far; darker asphalt at night. Restoring the dither fails it. All Node tests (365) and the drains, kerb fixtures, life polish, gun, street lamps and street/night/snow/chalk tests pass. Render cost, warmed medians of 41 frames at 161×66, two ABAB runs, main → this branch: 15.3–21.6 → 13.2–20.5 ms across three street views, no consistent change. Local observations, not an FPS guarantee. Next in pass 16: crossings at the ends of bridges, kerb faces, patching, slopes, walls, fences and retaining edges.

- Signs and shopfronts (pass 13, Claude, claude/shopfront-glass), shop glass, frames, doors and shutters: on the brownstone, midtown, downtown and housing fronts (`city/architecture-render.js`, `city/residential-render.js`) the ground floors were `:` glass, `|` and `=` frames and `_` coursing. A shared `facadeLine` decides each frame, mullion, transom and stile: wider than half a cell it is drawn in its own colour, thinner it only darkens the glass, so a far front no longer breaks into dotted lines. Shop and door glass is plain colour (cool by day, warm when lit), the door's kick plate, stiles and push bars are colour, and the brownstone walk-up doors are panelled leaves under a fanlight in a stone surround. The walls round them came along: quoins and party piers, string courses, cornices, and brick and stone coursing filtered so a wall settles to its colour far off. `brownstoneWindow` is shared with the upper floors, so their windows changed too (pass 15 overlap). Roller shutters (`city/shop-shutters.js`) keep every part as colour: lit lips and shadowed creases up close, an even panel far off, the rails, bottom bar and padlock. Ground-floor glyph share, as surveyed from the street: brownstones 42.8 → 0.9%, downtown offices 68 → 0.3%, midtown 56.5 → 2.5%, housing 66.8 → 1.3%. Chinatown, the Shotengai, Belle and industrial fronts are other painters, next. New `city-shopfront-glass` browser test: under 2.5% glyphs per family from the street, day and night; glazing bars and door stiles up close and none far off. Putting `:` back on the housing shop glass fails it. `city-shop-shutters` now checks the shutter's parts by colour instead of characters. The fascia, generic door, shop window, storefront sign, street entrance, architecture and facade tests pass. Render cost, warmed medians at 161×66, two ABAB runs, main → this branch: 13.6–20.9 → 12.0–19.9 ms across three street views, no consistent change. Local observations, not an FPS guarantee.

- Signs and shopfronts (pass 13, Claude, claude/shopfronts-generic), the other ground floors: the generic fronts (`baseFacade` in `city/city-render.js`), Chinatown and the Shotengai's merchant fronts (`merchantFacade` and their built awnings, roof tiles, eaves, air-conditioners, lattices, boards and blade signs in `city/pavilion-render.js`), the Shotengai's own shop fronts (`city/shotengai.js`) and the Belle Époque fronts were `:` glass, `|` piers, `=` transoms and bands, `#` doors and goods, `o@*` produce, `O` lanterns, `v` valances, `x` lattices and `_|.` coursing. All of it is colour now, with the same `facadeLine` filtering: window glass lit or reflecting the street, goods on shelves as coloured blocks up close that blur into the lit window far off, produce in slatted crates, warehouse roll-up doors with filtered slats, brownstone stoops and apartment doorways with their lamps, paper lanterns ribbed under their caps, noren split at the doorway with the shop's mark, Belle's gilt sign borders, arched windows, keystones and ashlar, and ivy as leaves round a stem. Sign lettering stays letters. Ground-floor glyph share from the street: industrial warehouses 92 → 0%, generic midtown 100 → 0%, Belle 41.7 → under 2%, Chinatown 26.2 → under 2%, the Shotengai 23 → under 2%. The remainders above 5% are landmark fronts (museum and the like), pass 26; fire escapes and balconies above are pass 15. `city-shopfront-glass` gains a third test for these districts (under 4% per district, landmarks aside, day and night); putting goods glyphs back in the merchant windows fails it. All Node tests (1071) and the fascia, shutter, storefront sign, street entrance, facade utilities, Belle home doors, balconies, cornices and windows, generic door, shop window and arcade boundary browser tests pass, with the district, market and shop tests in the main suite. Render cost, warmed medians at 161×66, two ABAB runs, main → this branch: 13.7–22.7 → 15.1–19.7 ms across three street views, no consistent change. Local observations, not an FPS guarantee. Remaining in pass 13: the Chinatown vertical signs' typography and mounts, then the closing audit.

- Pass 13 closing audit (Claude): signs and shopfronts across every district have completed first passes: fascias and awnings, generic fronts, window displays, shutters and doors (#248–#254), then glass, frames, doors, coursing and shutters as colour (#375) and the generic, Chinatown, Shotengai and Belle fronts (#376). Sign lettering is the global block lettering; blade signs and fascia boards are built geometry on their mounts. Ground-floor glyph share surveyed from the street on main after #376, per district and style: every shopfront style is at or under 4.3%, most under 1%; the remainders are sign lettering cells and fascia edges. The only styles above that are landmark fronts (the museum, sty 23, and sty 6), which are pass 26. The pass 13 suites (shopfront glass, fascias, shutters, shop windows, storefront signs, generic doors, street entrances) pass on main, 19 tests. Pass 13 is ticked. Known remainders, each in its own pass: upper-floor fire escapes and balcony rails (15), landmark fronts (26), and the old single-cell Chinatown vertical sign in `city-render.js` sty 10, which the merchant buildings' built blade signs have replaced in practice.

- Signs and shopfronts follow-up (Claude, claude/storefront-relief, at the user's request: stores need more unique-looking fronts, and solid colour looked too flat; Cabin Cove's house is the reference): new `city/shopfront-kit.js`. Every shop takes a style from its name: a painted timber front (raised panels in the stallriser, fluted pilasters with capitals, transom lights), a glazed-tile front (a glossy highlight on each tile, grout between), polished granite (flecks and a sheen), aluminium modern (brushed panels and seams), or brick with arched transom lights, each in its own paint from that style's palette. As at Cabin Cove the depth comes from tone: frames and bars are bevelled, lit along one edge and shaded along the other; the sill catches the light and casts a shadow on the glass under it; glass reflects the sky, lighter toward the top, with a soft streak or two, and the reveal's shadow under the head and down one side; it's lit warm from inside after dark. Brick is real brick (7.5 cm courses), each brick its own tone with light mortar; generic piers and the wall by the board are brick, painted render over a plinth, or stone blocks. All of it fades to calm colour with the cell's footprint. The kit is used by the generic fronts, the brownstone shopfronts, downtown's shop bays and the housing shopfronts; Chinatown, the Shotengai and Belle keep their own characters. The window displays (`city/shop-windows.js`) got the same treatment: rounded goods lit from the upper left, a back wall of shelves stocked with goods instead of a flat wall, the glass's streaks over everything behind it, and no leftover characters. `city-shopfront-glass` gains a fourth test: at least five styles and six paints across 600 shops, fixed per shop; glass brighter toward the top by day; stallriser and pilasters in the shop's paint; a bevel up close and none far off; a sheen that settles; brick toned up close and plain far off. Making every shop the same style fails it. `city-shop-windows` and the storefront glazing test accept the stocked shop behind the glass. Render cost, warmed medians at 161×66, two ABAB runs, main → this branch: 10.0–12.7 → 10.4–12.1 ms across three street views, no change. Local observations, not an FPS guarantee.

- Market streets and the Shotengai (pass 14, Claude, claude/shotengai-street), the covered street: the arcade roof (`city/shotengai.js`) was clear glass in a `#`, `=` and `-` steel frame, so through it you saw every busy upper floor above the roofline. Its panels are now what the comment always said they were: ribbed translucent panels, milky and lit by the daylight (brighter down the middle, away from the eaves), warm under the lamps after dark, corrugated up close; so the street reads as covered and calm, as a real shōtengai does from inside. The steel frame is colour: beams, ribs with a lit edge and a shadowed web, and bars. The upper storeys below the roofline (`shotengaiUpper`) are colour too: the vertical neon signs keep their letters and get chasing bulbs down their edges, lit signboards have a frame and a brighter face, air-conditioners show a casing and a grille, small windows sit in frames with a glazing bar, and the tiled render is small square tiles each its own faint tone with filtered grout. A Shotengai street view went from 8.4% to 2.4% characters by day and night (the rest are vending machines, bikes and props). New `city-shotengai-street` browser test: under 4% from three spots and three views, day and night; no sky through the roof looking up, plenty of roof and steel; panels lamp-lit at night; beams, ribs, bars and panels all present. Making the panels clear again fails it. The arcade boundary, streetscape, world, storefront sign, shutter and Shotengai district tests pass. Render cost, warmed medians at 161×66, two ABAB runs, main → this branch: 10.7–13.8 → 9.5–13.6 ms across three Shotengai views. Local observations, not an FPS guarantee.
- Market streets (pass 14, Claude, claude/night-market), the Chinatown night market: the stalls' goods were rows of characters (`oO`, `([=])`, `Y|`, `o@`, `[#]`, `(O)`, `#==`, `(~)`, `~>`), the counters `=` and `|`, the poles `|`, the daytime tarps `\` and `~`, and the lanterns `o`, `@` and `=`. In `city/city-sprites.js` they're colour now, and each stall's goods are shapes: steamer baskets with their rims at the back of the food stall, buns and skewers at the front; red tassels, knots and gold coins; boxes and glass jars that catch the light; a glowing crystal ball and tarot cards laid out; bags with a fish in each and a tub of darting goldfish, all on the lantern-lit cloth, with a glint on each. The counters are plank fronts under a top rail, the poles steel, the tarps roped down. The paper lanterns, on the market's string and across Chinatown's streets, are round: brightest down the middle, darker to the sides, ribbed up close, dark caps, glowing after dark; their cords are plain colour. The share of characters at the stalls fell from 3.5–6.4% to about 1–3%; the rest is the strings' thin wires, drawn by the shared `rod()` everywhere in the game, left as line characters for now. New `city-night-market-stalls` browser test: under 4% at every stall, day and night; each stall's counter top in at least three colours with no characters; five stall kinds. Night market clearance, coastal props, goods, world and the night market and stall tests in the main suite pass.

- Pass 14 closing audit (Claude): market streets and the Shotengai have completed first passes: the covered roof and upper storeys (#379), the night market's stalls, goods and paper lanterns (#380), with the market hall's fittings and the night market's clearance from earlier chunks, and the shopfronts below from pass 13 and #378. On main after #380: a Shotengai street view is 1.8% characters by day and night (1.3% looking up); the night market's stalls are 0.7–0.8%. What's left is block lettering on signs and the thin wires and cords drawn by the shared `rod()` (line characters, deliberately, for now). The Shotengai street, night market stalls, night market clearance and arcade boundary suites pass on main, 8 tests. Bicycles were already modelled from tubes. Pass 14 is ticked. Next: pass 15, building architecture (upper floors still use oversized painted coursing and `=` bands in several families), then the rest of 16.

- Building architecture (pass 15, Claude, claude/upper-facades), upper floors with depth: above the shops, downtown's offices were 75–92% characters, warehouses 97% and brick industrial 86%. A new `facadeWindow` in `city/shopfront-kit.js` gives every upper window what the shopfronts got: a bevelled frame lit along its outer top and left, a sill whose lip catches the light, glazing bars, and glass that reflects the sky (lighter toward the top, a soft streak, the head's shadow) or glows when the room's lit. It's used by the generic families in `city/city-render.js` (brownstone with stone lintels, the Chinatown shophouse with its balcony rail and lanterns, art deco with rounded limestone piers, ribbed spandrels and gilt chevrons, the parking garage's decks, columns, sodium lamps and cars, modern apartments' slabs and glass rails, the office's precast panels, the glass tower's curtain wall, the tenement's plaster and fire escape, and brick) and by downtown's towers in `city/architecture-render.js`. Walls are real: brick with every brick its own tone, rendered plaster with a soft mottle, precast with filtered joints, warehouses' corrugated sheet with a lit crest and shadowed trough per rib (`corrugated`). The housing family's fire escapes, drop ladders, balcony rails, glass panels and planters (`residentialDetailShade`) are colour, and so is the lit room seen up close through a window (`litRoom`: bookshelves, beds, a TV, a silhouette, floorboards). Upper-floor glyph share from the street, every district and style: at or under 2.6% (most 0%), the landmarks aside (pass 26). New `city-upper-facades` browser test: under 4% per district day and night; a window's frame, sill, reflecting glass and lit glass, nothing drawn outside it; corrugation near and plain far; no characters in a lit room. Putting `:` back in the glass fails both. All Node tests (1071) and the shopfront, kitchen display, Grand Hotel, facade utilities, balcony rail, home window, storefront sign, shop window, street entrance and Shotengai suites pass, with the facade, window, balcony and building tests in the main suite. Render cost, warmed medians at 161×66, two ABAB runs, main → this branch: 10.2–13.7 → 10.3–11.8 ms across three street views, no change. Local observations, not an FPS guarantee.

- Building architecture (pass 15, Claude, claude/roofs), roofs, parapets, cornices and bays: from above, every flat roof was a `.`/`:` gravel dither inside a `#` parapet, and the brownstones' built cornices, band courses and piers were `=` and `|`. In `roofTop` (`city/city-render.js`) each building now has its own roof surface: gravel with a fine grain, a dark tar membrane with welded seams, square pavers each its own tone, or now and then a green roof; the parapet is a coping stone lit along its outer edge, its inner face in shadow; the helipad's ring and H, a frame's ribbed decking and rebar, and the mansards are colour too. In `architectureDetailShade` (`city/architecture-render.js`) cornices and courses have a lit upper edge over a shadowed face, tops are lit, the stoops' iron railings are colour with a lit handrail, and a bay window's sashes use `facadeWindow` (frame, sill, glazing bar, sky or lamp). Rooftop views over downtown and midtown fell from 24–29% characters to 3.8% (the rest are the shared `rod()` wires and street props). New `city-roofs` browser test: no characters across hundreds of roofs and at least three surfaces; a lit coping over a darker inner face; under 6% from two rooftop views by day and night. Putting the gravel dither back fails it. The snow test now checks that the parapet still shows under snow by its colour rather than by a `#`. All Node tests (1071), upper facades, shopfronts, street entrances and the roof, helipad, stoop, bay, brownstone, cornice and snow tests in the main suite pass.

- Pass 15 closing audit (Claude): building architecture has completed first passes: upper floors with framed windows, real walls and fire escapes (#382), roofs, parapets, cornices, courses, bays and stoop rails (#383), on top of the facade utilities, balconies, Belle ironwork and cornices from earlier chunks. On main after #383: every district's upper floors are at or under 2.6% characters (most 0%), landmarks aside; rooftop views over downtown and midtown 3.8%, the rest being the shared `rod()` wires and street props. Chimneys and rooftop equipment beyond water tanks remain as they are (a few built tanks; plain roofs otherwise). The upper facade, roof, shopfront and Shotengai suites pass on main, 8 tests. Pass 15 is ticked. Known remainders: landmark fronts (26); the shared `rod()` wires (a global choice, left as line characters).

- Roads and ground (pass 16, Claude, claude/ground), the ground off the streets: in `floorBaseCell` the waterfront's promenade (`+` and `.`) is big flagstones each its own tone with filtered joints, the sea wall a coping of blocks, dry sand a rippled colour (the surf line and the natural coast are left to the sea work in progress elsewhere); building sites are churned dirt with tyre ruts; yards are concrete with oil stains, hairline cracks up close and yellow bay lines. Road repairs join the asphalt (`city/road-surface.js`): now and then a newer, darker patch cut into a lane or a filled trench across it, settling into the road far off. In `city/city-sprites.js` the construction hoarding (a striped top rail lit along its edge, posts, a kick board), the portapotty (ribs in light and shade, a moulded door, louvred vent, handle) and chain-link's top rail and posts are colour. Chain-link's mesh stays an `x` lattice on purpose: its diamonds are a few centimetres, finer than a cell from anywhere you can stand, and as colour it would either fill the fence in or vanish. New `city-ground` browser test: under 5% characters on waterfront, building-site and yard ground seen from above; no characters on a hoarding, a portapotty or a chain-link fence other than its mesh; repairs that show up close and settle far off. Putting the yard's dither back fails it. All Node tests, the road surface test, and the hoarding, portapotty, yard, construction and waterfront tests in the main suite pass. (One full Node run had `city-minigames` fail; rerun alone it passes 31/31, on this branch and on main: a timing flake under load.) Still in pass 16: slopes and retaining edges, walls and the remaining fence kinds; the parks' ground is pass 18.

- Parks (pass 18, Claude, claude/park-ground), ground, ponds and trees: a park block's ground was 99.4% characters (`"` grass, `.` paths, `~` mud, `|` reeds) and trees were `@`/`%` leaves on a `|` trunk. In `floorBaseCell` the lawn is now colour: mown in long stripes, a slow lift and fall across it, blades only up close; paths are compacted gravel worn darker down the middle where the grass gives way at their edges; mud and reed beds are tone. `pondCell` draws ice cracks, lily pads, ripples and raindrops as background. In `treeCell` bark is shaded and furrowed (birch marks show), the canopy is clumped and lit from above, pine tiers dark under each bough; winter branches are colour too. Park ground 99.4% → 0% characters day and night, park views about 1% overall. New `city-parks` browser test: ground under 3% characters day and night, no characters on a tree, canopy lighter on top than underneath, bare winter branches without characters. Putting a `"` back on the grass fails it. Render cost, warmed medians of 41 frames at 1170×800, two ABAB runs under a concurrent test run, main → branch: park views 9.7–14.5 → 10.3–13.3 ms, no measurable change. Still open in 18: planters, flower beds, picnic props, shrubs.

- Botanical Gardens (pass 18, Claude, claude/park-props): from inside, the Gardens were 58.6% characters: `"` lawns, `:` gravel, `@`/`*` flower beds, `~`/`o` lake, `|`/`=` jetty, `^`/`=`/`|` spear railings, `%` palms behind `|` glazing, a boarded shed. The park's ground is now shared (`lawnSurface`, `gravelSurface`, `reedSurface`, `parkFine` in `city/city-render.js`) and `gardenFloor` draws every surface as colour: lawns a touch greener than the park's; gravel paths (now pale stone rather than orange, the park's too) worn down the middle; flower beds with a dark soil rim and rows of planting, blooms in the bed's three colours, the soil between rows fading into the planting far off; the lake through `pondCell` (never frozen, so the ducks and boats keep it); the jetty's boards with filtered gaps and a rail each side; the bear's boulders shaded by their own slope over bare earth and rough grass; the tortoise pen's sandy earth; the picnic blanket a three-tone gingham with a white fringe. The spear railing's bars, rails and heads are iron as colour, lit on one side, still see-through between the bars up close. The conservatory and aviary (`glassFacade`, `glassDepth`, `pavilionGlassShade`) have glazing bars as filtered colour lines, clumped and lit foliage, the sky sliding across the panes; the shed has a felt roof, filtered boards, a framed door and a window lit at night. Gardens views 58.6% → 1.9% characters by day, 58.8% → 2.1% at night; the ground itself 0.7% (what's left is lamp posts and `rod()` lines standing on it). New test in `city-parks`: every ground kind present, ground under 1.2% and views under 4% characters day and night, the blanket in three tones without characters. Putting `:` back on the gravel (ground 0.7% → 1.6%) or `#` on the blanket fails it. Render cost, warmed medians of 41 frames at 1170×800, two ABAB runs, main → branch: 9.0–18.0 → 8.4–13.4 ms (fewer glyph draws). Pass 18 still open for: street planters and window boxes (pass 15 work aside), shrubs as their own thing, and the far lamp billboard (`lampCell`), which goes with the `rod()` question.

- Belle details (pass 15 follow-up, Claude, claude/belle-details): the Belle facades were colour but their built details weren't: every cornice, balcony slab and bracket was `=`/`|`, the wrought iron `=`/`|`, the copper mansards `/` seams and `=` ridges, and the copper crowns `|` ribs and flutes with a `+` finial. In `belleDetailShade` (`city/belle.js`) the cornice has a crown lit along its top, a shadow under its projection and dentils along its bed (filtered); slabs a lit nosing; brackets shade as they curl in under the slab; the ironwork is iron as colour, a lit top rail, bars filtered with distance and the scroll between, still see-through. The mansards' standing seams are lit lines filtered to a sheen far off, hips and ridges capped brighter. The crowns' drums are round-lit with shaded flutes, the domes' ribs stand proud and lit, the finial gilded. Belle street views 3.3% → 0.3% characters (close up 7.8% → 1.1%). New `city-belle-details` browser test: every part kind drawn without characters in at least two tones, near and far; ironwork see-through; street views under 1.2%. Putting `=` back on the cornices fails it. Existing Belle, copper crown, mansard, placement and balcony-rail tests pass (11). Render cost, warmed medians of 41 frames at 1170×800, two ABAB runs: 7.3–9.2 → 7.3–8.4 ms, no measurable change.

- Bridges (pass 17, Claude, claude/bridges): bridge views were 18% characters by day (`,` walkways, `=` centre line and rails, `|`/`=` girder faces, `_` posts, `#`/`=`/`|` tower steel, `=`/`-`/`|` footbridge boards). In `drawRoadBridges` (`city/world-bridges-render.js`) the deck uses the street's surface rules (`ROAD_SURFACE`): asphalt with a filtered grain, darker at night, a yellow centre dash filtered with distance, a kerb, walkways of precast slabs with filtered joints, and a steel expansion joint across the deck every 60 m. The girders have a lit fascia under the deck edge, stiffeners down the web filtered with distance, a shadowed soffit; the rails are lit, the top one brighter. The suspension towers are riveted plate with lit splice bands and lit crossbeam flanges; the necklace lights and the red beacons glow as colour. `STEEL` (shared with the el, signals and stairs) is now colour: faces shaded, tops lit. The Lighthouse Walk's boards are 25 cm planks each in its own tone with filtered gaps, lamplight pooling on them; its rails are a lit top rail over posts that close up into a parapet far off, still see-through up close; the gateway posts and sign board are colour (the lettering stays). Bridge views 18.0% → 1.9% by day, 19.4% → 3.4% at night; what's left is stars, the sea, the Grand Hotel and `rod()` cables. New `city-bridges` browser test: views under 3% by day and 4.5% at night, the deck's centre line present, the footbridge boards in more than one tone, no characters on `STEEL`. Putting `,` back on the walkways fails it. Render cost, warmed medians of 41 frames at 1170×800, two ABAB runs: 6.1–10.4 → 6.1–11.1 ms, no measurable change. The cables and hangers are `rod()` (the open question).

- Cars (pass 21, first chunk, Claude, claude/cars): every car, taxi, police car, ambulance and fire engine was drawn with characters on colour: `@` wheels, `_` sills, `O` headlamps, `]` tail lights, `=` grilles, `-` beltlines, `|` pillars, `%`/`~` dents, `*` cracks and light-bar strobes, `|`/`=` lockers and ladders. In `drawVehicleBody` and `drawAmbulance` (`city/city-sprites.js`) they are now colour with some modelling: round wheels (black tyre, a rim lit on its upper half, a pale hub) in shadowed arches; a sill with a lit lip; a creased beltline lit above and shadowed below; door shut lines where the cabin's pillars fall, filtered with distance; a bonnet and roof lit by the sky with a crease down each side; headlamps white at full after dark, dim by day; a grille of filtered slats; a lit bumper; tail lights that brighten when braking; a boot shut line. The glass has the sky in it, brighter toward its top; damage shows as dents in shadow and scrapes to bare metal, cracked glass as white; a wreck is scorched black and grey. The taxi sign, light bars (white on their strobe), lockers (roller shutters), the ladder (rails and rungs) and the ambulance's cross, stripe, rear doors and windscreen are colour too. Vehicle characters in ten close street views 5,811 → 0. New `city-cars` browser test: each kind, plus a damaged car and a wreck, drawn on its own from two sides by day and night with no characters and a tyre showing; tail lights red; headlamps white at full at night; glass in more than one tone. Putting `@` back on the wheels fails it. Render cost near a taxi, warmed medians of 41 frames at 1170×800, two ABAB runs: 7.2–10.4 → 6.0–9.1 ms, no measurable change. Still to do in pass 21: dashboards and interiors from the driver's seat, the bus, and fuel equipment.

- Cars (pass 21, Claude, claude/car-dash), the driver's seat: from behind the wheel the view ended in a row of `_` over a grey strip with `.--------.`/`|---()---|` for a wheel. `dash()` (`city/hud.js`) now draws, over the bottom `DASH_ROWS` (8) rows, a bonnet in the car's own colour (its crown lit, the sky along the centre crease and the shoulders, falling away toward the wings, the road showing either side of it), the dashboard's top lip catching the light, the dashboard darkening toward you, and a round steering wheel with a rim lit along its top, spokes out to each side and a hub, two dials behind it with bright bezels whose faces glow after dark. The speed, taxi and damage readouts stay as text on the dash. The prompt and a bonnet fire now sit above the taller dash (the chase camera and aircraft keep theirs). New `city-car-dash` browser test: no characters on the dash but the readouts, the bonnet in the car's colour, a lit wheel rim, the dials lit at night. Putting the old `_` row back fails it. It's a screen-space overlay, no per-frame cost of note.

- Fuel stations (pass 21, Claude, claude/gas-station): station views were 25% characters: a `.` dither and `+` joints across the forecourt, `%` oil, `/` hatching, `+` over the canopy's soffit and lights, `=`/`_` on the canopy, `-` pump louvres, `J`/`|` nozzles, `#` kick plates, `|`/`=` sign frames, and on the shop `|`/`_` brick, `|` columns, `_` shelves and `o#=@%` goods. `gas-render.js` now draws it all as colour. The forecourt is concrete slabs with filtered joints and a fine grain up close, oil darker where cars stand, yellow hatching by the door, white under the canopy after dark. The canopy's soffit has filtered panel joints and light panels in dark housings; its fascia is lit along its top with a drip edge in shadow. Pumps have a glowing header edge, louvres down the sides, a blue nozzle hung in its holster when idle and a kick plate in the brand's colour. The price sign has a white frame, the ice chest frost on its lid, and the shop has round steel columns lit down their middles, a lit kerb, glass with the sky in its top, poster print as bands, lit shelf lips, and goods lit along their tops; its side is brick (`brickFace`). Brand, price, display and FOOD MART/OPEN 24H/CAR WASH lettering stays as lettering. Station views 25.2% → 0.55% characters day and night. New `city-gas-station` browser test: views under 1.2% (lettering aside) by day and night, no characters on the forecourt, the forecourt brighter at night under the canopy than by day. Putting `.`/`+` back on the forecourt fails it. Render cost, warmed medians of 41 frames at 1170×800, two ABAB runs: 7.5–9.5 → 7.0–8.0 ms.

- The el (pass 22, first chunk, Claude, claude/el-cars): el views were 30% characters: the stair posts as `|` rods, `#` cross ties and `=` girders under the deck, `X`/`o` lattice and rivets on its side, `|`/`-` flanges, battens and leaning braces on the pillars, `=` treads, `_` footings, and on the cars `=` underframes and stripes, `#`/`|` roofs, `O` lamps, `|` pillars and gangways, `#` door windows and `o` passengers. Now the deck's underside is girders lit along their flanges over ties in shadow; its top is ballast, sleepers, rails with polished heads, and platforms of concrete slabs with a yellow edge; its side girder has lit chords and a lattice and rivets filtered with distance. Pillars have a stone footing, lit flanges, battens and lattice braces in colour, and a cap. The stairs' treads have lit nosings (the gaps close up far off); their posts and far flights are steel rods in colour (`elSteelRod`). Cars: a lit sole bar, ribbed roof and grilled AC unit, red stripe lit along its top, lamps, bellows, door seams and windows lit warm after dark with the odd passenger's head and shoulders against the light. Riding (`elFrame`): ribbed rubber floor with a paler aisle, diffuser light strips, yellow-edged doors and threshold plates, window frames, ad cards, moquette seats with sewn divisions, the end door's glass and frame. El views 30.0% → 3.0% characters; the rest is `rod()` handrails and wires. New `city-el` browser test: views under 5% (lettering aside) by day and night; the deck's painters without characters near and far; a car drawn on its own without characters, windows lit at night. Putting `=` back on the car's stripe fails it.

- Lamp posts and drainpipes as solid colour (Claude, claude/lamps-pipes, the user's call on `rod()`): lamp posts and drainpipes are now never a line of characters. The street lamp model's and the drainpipe model's thin-tube fallback (`lineShade` in `street-lamps.js` and `facade-utilities.js`), which drew `|`/`/`/`\`/`-` once a tube was thinner than a cell, is now a solid line of steel or pipe colour, never thinner than one cell. The far lamp billboard (`lampCell`) draws its pole, collar, swan neck, drop, cap and finial as steel colour (the collar and neck's top lit) and its glass glowing after dark. Belle lamps: globes glow after dark and catch the sky by day; poles and bases are iron, the bands gilded. Park lamps and the fair's bulb posts stand on a solid steel post (`postRod`). The water off a drainpipe's shoe in the rain is colour. Overhead wires, cables and handrails stay as line characters (`rod()`), as asked. Beside the street lamps, line characters 2.3% → 1.05% of the view. New `city-lamps-pipes` browser test: the billboard drawn without characters at several cell sizes by day and night; `|`/`/`/`\` under 0.6% beside the lamps; drainpipes from 12 to 22 m without line characters. Putting the old glyph back on the billboard's pole or the drainpipe's fallback fails it.

- Shop types behind the glass (pass 13 follow-up, Claude, claude/shop-types, the user's ask): restaurants showed the same window as a shop of goods (a sushi bar looked like it sold books). A new `city/shop-scenes.js` gives the important types their own room through the window, in colour and at depth (`glassSlopes`/`boxBehind`, so it slides as you pass), from `shopWindowBehind`: a sushi bar's wooden counter, the glass case of fish on ice, chefs in white, a split noren, customers on stools; a noodle, ramen, pho, dim sum, izakaya or Thai place's tables and diners, steam off the pots, menu boards, red lanterns; a pizzeria's tables with white cloths, a wood-fired oven glowing in a tiled wall; a diner's red booths, counter, stools, pie case, steel pass and lit menu board, on a checked floor (also tacos, burgers, chicken, kebab); a café's small tables with cups, an espresso machine lit down one side, a pastry case and chalkboard; a bar's back bar of bottles lit from below in front of a mirror, its counter and stools; a market's produce crates stepped up behind the glass and shelves to the ceiling with a drinks fridge; a laundromat's washers and dryers, the wash going round; a barber's chairs, caped customers and mirrors; a bank's marble, counter, glass screens and clock; a gym's weights, bench and mirrors. Diners are seated people (head, hair, shirt), more of them in the evening; candles on the tables after dark; the ceiling's lights (pendants, lanterns or tubes) by type. Shops of goods keep their displays. These scenes show in every facade family: the generic and brownstone/downtown fronts, the residential ground floors, the pavilion merchants (`merchantFacade`), the Shotengai (under the noren) and neon-sign fronts, out to 5.6 units; a restaurant or bar in a warehouse (style 8) now has its roll-up door rolled away and glazed in instead of an orange panel. New `city-shop-scenes` browser test: in front of up to three open sushi bars, ramen shops, pizzerias, diners, cafés, bars, laundromats, barbers, banks, gyms and groceries, each window draws its room; under 2% characters in the windows; bookshops and record shops keep their goods. Turning the scenes off fails it. Render cost in front of three restaurants, warmed medians of 41 frames at 1170×800, two ABAB runs: 7.1–11.1 → 7.5–8.7 ms. Next: shop exteriors beyond the window (signage and awnings by type), and the industrial district's buildings.

- The docks and yards (pass 25 start, Claude, claude/industrial-2, the user's ask: the industrial district lacked unique exteriors): three quarters of the industrial buildings were one warehouse (style 8: corrugated sheet, a band of high windows, orange roll-up doors). A new `city/industrial-facades.js` makes each style-8 building one of four kinds from its seed. Sheds: corrugated sheet (rusty or grey), a canopy over each loading door with a lamp under it after dark, high steel-framed windows, the firm's name painted under the parapet. Brick mills: round-headed tall windows on stone sills with a ring of headers over each, steel glazing (some panes newer, the odd one hinged open), a stone string course at every floor, timber loading doors up the middle bay under a hoist beam, a faded painted name. Cold stores: white insulated panels with filtered joints, a blue band, plant louvres, dock doors in black rubber seals with striped bollards. Concrete works: a frame of columns and floor beams with every bay a steel window, goods doors in the ground floor. Open doors are rolled up and show what's inside (pallet racking with wrapped loads, or the shop's own room: a garage's car up on its lift with a mechanic under it and a tool chest and tyres; or a restaurant's room); shut ones show their shutters. Firm names come from a list per kind (HARBOR SUPPLY, UNION MILLS, DELTA COLD STORE, IRON WORKS...). Buildings drawn by `architectureFacade` are unchanged. New `city-industrial` browser test: each kind's face sampled whole (no stray characters; a mill is brick, a cold store white, a works concrete and glass, a shed corrugated sheet) and all four kinds present in the docks. Making every building a shed fails it. Render cost on three industrial fronts, warmed medians of 41 frames at 1170×800, two ABAB runs at the same positions: 6.0–7.1 → 6.4–7.8 ms (about half a millisecond more for the glazing and the interiors). Still to do in pass 25: roofs (sawtooth north lights, roof plant), chimneys, cranes and gantries.

- Industrial roofs, cranes and stacks (pass 25, Claude, claude/industrial-roofs): the industrial roofs were plain parapeted flats, the tower cranes a lattice of `/`, `|`, `-` characters with `#` counterweights, `:` cab glass, a `J` hook and `I`/`=` beams, and the smokestacks' iron bands `=`. Now (`industrialRoofs` in `city/industrial-facades.js`, from the sprite pass) every roof cell of a works, and of half the sheds, carries three sawtooth teeth (convex prisms through `rayMansard`): a steep north light of glazing in steel bars facing north, the sky in it by day and the works lit warm behind it after dark, and a long south slope of corrugated sheet (grey or rusty, lit by the sky, taking snow); a building's teeth run on unbroken across its cells. Cold stores carry louvred refrigeration units with two fans turning on top; mills carry turbine ventilators under caps. The cranes' lattice members are drawn in yellow lit by their faces with the sky between them (far off, a yellow beam); the counterweights are concrete blocks with joints; the cab's glass has the sky in it by day and is lit warm after dark; the hook block is yellow; the slung beams show an I in each end; the apex legs are solid steel; the warning lights glow red. The ties and the hook's cable stay wires. The smokestacks' iron bands are lit steel. New test in `city-industrial`: roofs of each kind exist; from above a sawtooth roof its glazing shows by day and after dark; a crane drawn on its own has no characters on its steel; a smokestack has none. Turning the lattice back into `#` fails it. Render cost over the docks and at a crane, warmed medians of 41 frames at 1170×800, two ABAB runs: 5.3–9.3 → 5.8–9.2 ms, no measurable change.

- Shops told from the street (pass 13 follow-up, Claude, claude/shop-exteriors, the user's ask for distinct shop exteriors): a new `city/shop-exteriors.js` puts a fixture by the door of the important shop kinds, one per shop at the end of its first fascia board (`ARCH_DETAILS`), drawn from the sprite pass up to about 9 units off. Barbers: a striped pole on a bracket, red, white and blue turning up it while open (lit after dark), chrome caps. Pharmacies and drugstores: a green cross hung out over the pavement, glowing (and pulsing gently after dark). Sushi, ramen, noodle, pho, dumpling, dim sum, izakaya, yakitori, Thai and tea houses: a ribbed red paper lantern with black caps and a yellow tassel by the door, glowing after dark (the Shotengai keeps its own). Cafés, coffee shops, bakeries, bagels, donuts, ice cream and juice bars: a sandwich board out on the pavement, the menu chalked on slate in a timber frame. Florists: three zinc buckets of blooms over leaves. Bars and karaoke: an oval board on a bracket, a gilt rim, the shop's colour, a lamp over it after dark. All colour; closed-down shops get nothing. New `city-shop-exteriors` browser test: every kind present on the right kind of shop, each drawn on its own (from in front, along the street or above as it hangs or stands) without characters and in its own colours (the pole red, white and blue, the cross green, the lantern red, the board slate, chalk and timber, the buckets zinc and leaves, the bar sign's gilt), one per shop, none on a vacant shop. Putting `/` back on the pole fails it. Render cost on a shopping street, warmed medians of 41 frames at 1170×800, two ABAB runs: 7.5–12.2 → 6.8–9.6 ms, no measurable change.

- Boats (pass 23, Claude, claude/boats): the ferries were already colour; the marina's boats, office and bollards and the Gardens' swan boats weren't. In `drawBoat3D` (`city/marina.js`): the rubbing strake along the gunwale lit on top; the boot-top dark, the water breaking white along it underway; a tug's old-tyre fenders round with a hole in each; the go-faster stripe; teak decks in planks with filtered seams; windows in white frames with a glint of sky (lit warm after dark); cabin tops with seams; round portholes in brass rings; the mast steel, its masthead light after dark; the sails' panel seams; seat piping; the outboard's cowling; navigation lights red and green. The marina office's roof seams, eaves in shadow, framed door and windows and clapboard laps; cast-iron bollards with lit caps. In the Gardens: swan boats' waterline lapping, layered wing feathers, head; the duck bread, the gate iron, the sign's edges, the bait box lid. The shared `tube()` (bikes, swan necks and a few dozen other thin parts) now fills its cells with colour and no longer draws a line character over it. New `city-boats` browser test: each kind (speedboat, sailboat, cruiser, tug, ferry) drawn on its own moored and underway, by day and after dark, and a swan boat, without characters (a boat's name on her transom is lettering). Putting `=` back on the gunwale fails it.

- The airport (pass 24, Claude, claude/airport): airport views were 24% characters: `"`/`,` grass, `.` tarmac, `#` piano keys, numbers and helipad marks, `=` edge and touchdown lines, `-` centre lines, `+` apron joints, `|` stand lines, `%`/`o` rocks, `*`/`o` lights; the tower's `=` windows and `|` mullions, the terminal's `|` mullions, `/` reflections and `.` figures, the hangars' `=` ribs, `|` cladding seams and door frames, the workbench `=` and `/|o` tools; the aircraft's `.`/`o` cabin windows, `*` lights, `:` prop blur, `=`/`-` rotor blades, `/` canopy streaks and `o` tyres. `airportFloor` and `causewayFloor` (`city/airport-render.js`) now return surfaces like the streets': tarmac with a filtered grain, darker at night, rubber laid down where the wheels land; painted markings (edge lines, piano keys, the 09/27 numbers, touchdown bars, the centre line, the taxiway's yellow line, stand lead-ins, the landside road's line) as colour filtered with distance; apron slabs with filtered joints and oil; helipads' white ring and H on green; rocks rounded by their light; grass mown (`lawnSurface`); the runway, taxiway and helipad lights glowing cells after dark. The buildings and aircraft are colour throughout (the tower cab's raked glazing, the terminal's figures and reflections, the hangars' cladding seams, framed doors, workbench and tool board; cabin windows lit after dark, lights, prop and rotor blur, canopy streaks of sky, black tyres); landing gear legs, skid struts, engine pylons and the rotor mast are solid (`postRod`). Airport views 24.0% → 0.1% characters by day (0.65% at night: the stars). New `city-airport` browser test: the ground sampled over the whole airport near and far, day and night, all surfaces and no characters; the views under 0.8%; each aircraft drawn on its own without characters. Putting `#` back on the piano keys fails it.

- Landmark exteriors (pass 26, first chunk, Claude, claude/landmarks): the Grand Hotel's front was 47% characters, the stock exchange's 24%, the casino's 19%, the museum's 10%. Now: the Grand Hotel in pale stone with its courses filtered and rusticated low down, a gilt string course under each floor, round-headed windows in gilt frames with keystones and stone sills (rooms lit warm, unlit panes with the sky in their tops), a main cornice lit on its crown, the pediment's raking cornice lit and its tympanum in shadow, broad steps with lit nosings, fluted columns lit down one side, gilt revolving doors lit inside, and the dome's drum with gold pilasters and lit windows. The casino: chasing bulbs as light, gold columns round and lit, gilded canopy and doors, neon down the dark glass, CASINO in solid lit blocks, gold mullions over black glass with a chandelier's glint. The exchange and the museum: steps with lit nosings, fat fluted columns round and shaded, architraves, bronze panelled doors, raking cornices lit, stone in courses, tall windows (lit late at the exchange), the museum's copper dome with standing ribs, its oculus, and its lead roof's rolls and seams. Lettering stays (the ticker, the friezes, the banners). New `city-landmarks-colour` browser test: each of the four seen from the open in front of it, by day and at night, under 1% characters (lettering aside). Putting the old `=`/`.` back on the hotel's stone fails it. The towers, the cathedral's exterior and the lighthouse are next.

- Sunset Pier (pass 25, fairground, Claude, claude/fairground): fair views were 37% characters: the pier's `=`/`:`/`|` boards, the Ferris wheel's spokes and rims as line characters, `@` hub, `*`/`o` bulbs, `^`/`_`/`=`/`|` cars, `|`/`=` platform; the carousel's `|`/`=` turntable, `*`/`:` mirrors, `v`/`*`/`o` valance and canopy rim, `~` horses; the booths' `~` sides, `*` sign bulbs, `|`/`=` counters and `i`, `(o>`, `~`, dart and prize characters; the gate's `|` posts, `=` edges and `*` bulbs. Now the pier is boards across it each its own age with filtered gaps (`floorBaseCell`), the wheel's steel is solid colour even thinner than a cell, its bulbs lit and chasing white-hot, its hub barrel, platform planks and cars (roofs lit on top, windows between pillars, lit warm after dark) colour; the carousel's turntable boards and gilt edge, mirrors with lights running up them, its bulbs and painted horses; the booths' striped canvas, chasing bulbs, lit counters, and their prizes, ducks and dart boards drawn in their own colours where the characters were; the gate's posts, gilt edge and bulbs. Fair views 37% → 1.4% characters by day (2.7% at night with the stars and search beams). New `city-fairground` browser test: fair views under 2% by day and 5% at night, lettering aside; the deck without characters. Putting the plank characters back on the deck fails it.

- Landmark exteriors (pass 26, second chunk, Claude, claude/landmarks-2): the stone towers were 10% characters, the cathedral 15%, the screen tower 14%, the construction site 17%. In `city/landmark-render.js` the ashlar's joints are shadow between blocks each in its own tone; lancet windows have lit stone surrounds and dark leading against the stained glass; the cathedral's buttresses are lit on one side and stepped, its string courses, plinth and parapet lit on top, its rose window's tracery in stone round a gold centre, its portal's orders in shadow over planked oak doors; clock dials have a gilt bezel, black hands and grey hour marks; the shafts' corner piers stand proud; the lanterns' openings glow after dark; bells and finials are bronze and gilt; balustrades have a lit rail and round balusters; courses, portal hoods and piers are lit on top or by face; slate roofs show their seams. The screen tower's plinth and crown are steel panels and its screens' highlights white; the construction site is primed steel, beams lit along their tops, columns lit on one side, braces, the slab soffits and next columns deeper in. The lighthouse: its door under a white lintel, its slit windows lit on the stair after dark, the gallery's deck lit with its edge in shadow, a white railing, the lamp white-hot at its heart, the lantern's glazing in astragals. Towers, cathedral, screen tower and construction now at or under 0.3%. New `city-landmarks-towers` browser test: each from the open in front of it, by day and at night, under 1% characters (lettering aside), and the lighthouse by day under 3% (at night, the stars and its beam on the sea). Putting `_`/`|` back in the ashlar fails it.

- Lamplight on the ground (Claude, claude/lamp-light, the user's ask: the pools looked like flat circles): once the ground became surfaces, a lamp's pool replaced the ground's colour with a single warm level inside a radius, a flat disc. Now (`floorBaseCell`) the light is added to the ground: the ground keeps its own tone and texture, the level is raised by the light, and the warm comes in through an ordered dither on the screen (thick under the lamp, thinning to nothing at its edge, so it falls off instead of ending in a ring), with a scatter of dim warm `.` and `,` glints where it dies away. New `city-lamp-light` browser test: in several lamps' pools at night, the pool is a mix of warm and the ground's colour (neither nearly all one nor the other) in several levels, and its body is never solid warm. Putting the flat disc back fails it.


- Closing audit, passes 16, 17, 21, 23, 24, 25 and 26 (Claude, claude/closing-audit): each has a completed first pass across its scope (notes above: roads and ground #374/#385/#406; bridges #389; cars, the driver's seat and fuel stations #390/#391/#392; boats #402; the airport #403; the docks, roofs, cranes, stacks and Sunset Pier #396/#400/#408; landmark exteriors #404/#407), each with a browser test that fails when the old characters come back. Ticked, with these known remainders: (16) retaining walls and fences not separately audited; (17) the suspension cables and hangers stay `rod()` line characters by the user's call on wires; (21) car interiors seen through the glass are flat colour, and traffic damage beyond dents, scrapes and wrecks is not modelled; (23) ferry terminals and their food counters not re-audited; (24) aircraft cabins and the terminal interior not re-audited; (25) gantries and moving industrial assemblies beyond the cranes not built; (26) the aquarium's and conservatory's exteriors not re-audited (the conservatory's glass was done with the Gardens, #387). Passes 18 (street planters, window boxes, shrubs as their own objects) and 22 (the subway: platforms, trains) stay open: those parts are untouched.

## Handoff (Claude, 2026-11-03)

Where things stand, and how to carry on.

**Current checklist state.** Coast/islands (19) is merged through #472/#477 after full green CI on main 780a2d8. Cabin Cove's builders are retained. Water/weather (20) now has its first pass implemented in the water/weather chunk below; its stack also requires current green CI. Presentation/controls (31) now has its inventory/shop-preview pass below, completing its first implementation pass alongside the existing map, placement, prompt, touch and scaling coverage. Final integration (32) now has its complete integration audit below; the corrected quality stack is merged through #456 after full green CI. Abandoned shops (33) now include the ground-dressing completion below across nine facade families, alongside the reviewed shutter/graffiti and boarded-patch work. All numbered passes now have an implementation first pass; the water, previews, abandoned-frontage and final-tour PRs still require current green CI before merging. Inventory (27) and activity feedback (29) are merged through #447 and #449–#452 after full green CI, retaining the original hand/rod and restored splash strokes. Soundscape (30), phone menus (#454), abandoned shutters (#455) and boarded repairs (#456) are merged on main 7334fef. The lodging/controller/bridge fixes (#458) and seated-arrest/helicopter-light fixes (#457) are merged on main f44f4c4. Coast/islands (19) and water/weather (20) were reserved for the sea/shore session at the original handoff. The user released both areas for Codex on 2026-11-04; check current branches/open PRs for overlaps before changing shore, waves or foam.

- Integration tour checkpoint (pass 32, Claude, 2026-11-06), on main after the stadium (#461): an automated tour of the whole game in a headless browser. All 111 interiors open just inside their door and draw without errors (slowest the grocer, about 30 ms a frame). All 92 dev-tools places, the islands among them, run at about 15 ms a frame on average (worst about 28 ms, the radio tower). Every car model and flyable aircraft type runs through the real frame loop at 8 to 11 ms. Every season by day, at night, in rain and in snow over a third of the districts and landmarks shows no errors, worst frame 32 ms. A save holding money, tickets, items, a home, a car, the day, the time, needs and the fish log comes back identical after a reload. Found and fixed: three shop jumps (ICE CREAM, DUMPLINGS, FIRE DEPT) put you on a kerb fixture or a parked fire engine; `gotoShop` now slides you along the pavement to the nearest clear spot. `test/browser/city-integration-tour.browser.test.cjs` keeps the interiors and the shop jumps covered. Pass 32 stays open: rerun the tour once the coast (19), water (20), presentation (31) and abandoned-shop (33) passes close.

**User style corrections (5–6 November local dates).** Preserve the original ASCII held hand and fishing rod. Fishing splash/ripple/spray strokes also keep their earlier light ASCII appearance, with modest distance visibility support. Avoid large smooth canvas silhouettes in held work. These explicit preferences take precedence over the general surface rule below.

**The standing decisions.** Everything is colour, not characters: a cell's background carries the surface, `set(i, ' ', 0)`. Lettering (signs, names, tickers, prices) stays as lettering. Overhead wires, cables and handrails stay `rod()` line characters (the user's call); lamp posts, drainpipes and other posts and struts are solid (`postRod`, `elSteelRod`, `tube()`, the lamp and pipe models' `lineShade`). Depth through tone: lit tops and edges, shadowed undersides, joints and seams as shadow lines filtered with distance (`coveGroove`, `roadLine`, `roadBands`, `facadeLine`, `homeBalconyPavingFootprint(d, eye)` for the ground's footprint, `t / projY` for a box's). Integer colour levels: a variation under about one level doesn't show. Keep variation in bands, not per-cell noise, on the canvas path (each colour change along a row is a draw call).

**Useful shared pieces.** Ground: `lawnSurface`, `gravelSurface`, `reedSurface`, `parkFine`, `pondCell` (city-render.js), `roadSurface`, `plazaSurface` (road-surface.js); a floor painter returns `[base, level]` and `floorBaseCell` paints it as `surf` (lamplight is added over it, dithered). Facades: `shopfront-kit.js` (`facadeWindow`, `brickFace`, `shopWall`, `corrugated`, `shopfrontBay`), `industrial-facades.js` (four kinds of industrial building, their roofs), `shop-scenes.js` (rooms behind shop windows by type; `SHOP_SCENE_FOR`), `shop-exteriors.js` (fixtures by the door by type).

**Workflow that worked.** Branch from `origin/main`; edit `city/*.js` (never the bundle), then `node tools/build-city.cjs` and `node --check ascii-city.bundle.js`; a new file goes in `tools/city-manifest.json`. Survey first: a Playwright script that renders views and counts characters, and (to find who draws them) a copy of the bundle with `const set` made `let` so it can be wrapped to record stack traces. In page scripts, set `tod` and call `env(0)` before the first `render(0)`, or it hangs. For each chunk: a browser test that fails when the old characters come back (check it by mutation), an ABAB timing against `origin/main`'s bundle, a note in this file, a PR, then wait for green CI and merge. When two PRs both touch the bundle and this file, the second needs main merged in: resolve this file by keeping both notes, rebuild the bundle (don't hand-merge it), check for conflict markers in `city/*.js`, run the touched tests, push. CI runs each shard twice; a lone failure in one run of a long timing or stateful test (paint-sound's eraser timing, the car-damage test) with the other run green has been load flakiness. Don't force-push; fix forward.

- Residential planting (pass 18, first remaining chunk, Codex, 2026-11-04): balcony pots now have a lit rim, dark soil and filtered ribs; three overlapping ellipsoid foliage clumps replace the clipped leaf boxes, shaded above and below, orange in autumn and subdued in winter, with snow on exposed crowns. Tenement window boxes use the same planting helper with green containers and two solid wall brackets. Boxes sit below window sills and skip the fire-escape bay. Reviewed close, wide, oblique and night browser views. New `city-window-boxes` browser regression covers existing balcony pots, new mounted boxes, rims/soil, foliage lighting and seasons, rounded rendering and exposed snow; it fails against exact main's bundle. Existing parks/Gardens and four nearby housing/balcony regressions pass. Local serial ABAB warmed medians (41 frames after 10 warmups, 1170×800, normalized 177×66 cells): main 9.0/10.2 ms, change 10.7/10.5 ms; a small local render cost, not an FPS guarantee. Pass 18 remains open for street planters and standalone shrubs. Coast, water/weather and skating are untouched.
- Shotengai roof and seated NPC occlusion (Codex, 2026-11-04): the canopy painted over a wall retained the wall's farther depth, letting upper-storey fixtures draw through it. Facades now return the canopy's forward ray depth and the world renderer records that surface depth. Seated people also used a single depth plane through their hips, so the front of a chair clipped knees and shins that physically project ahead of the cushion. Sitting sprites now cache their body surface depths (including the ordinary body pixels reused in fine sprites); the figure painter uses those depths for geometry and background occlusion. Standing figures retain the existing path and wholly hidden seated bodies are culled before sprite generation. No seat-height or people-model redesign. New `city-arcade-depth` and `city-seated-depth` browser regressions fail against main: the roof test checks day/night and several covered streets/angles plus geometry above the canopy; the seating test checks coarse/fine knees ahead of the ferry cushion and a nearer opaque obstruction. Existing Shotengai street, five figure-visibility and all 13 ferry browser checks pass, as does the figures unit suite. Reviewed matching before/after roof and ferry views. Local serial ABAB warmed medians (41 measured frames, 1170×800, 177×66 cells): against main `4268e9a`, roof 11.8/11.2 → 12.2/11.4 ms; ferry 13.8/13.8 → 13.6/14.0 ms. Rebuilt and reran both new regressions and the Shotengai street test after merging that main. These are local render-cost observations.

### Street skating additions

Eight mapped street lines add bank-accessed stairs/handrails, loading docks,
manual links, angled bars and ledge transfers in clear park edges and service
yards. Existing trees, benches, traffic lanes and paths are preserved. Native
collision checks connect every mapped approach to a street, including yard
gates. Ground, drawn slopes/tubes and grind paths share geometry, with cell
buckets for hot ground/paving queries. See `SKATE_IMPLEMENTATION.md` for checks
and local serial ABAB timings. The numbered quality programme remains paused;
reserved world-bootstrap, DINER and bathroom work is untouched.

- Subway station surfaces and stair alignment (pass 22, first station chunk, Codex, 2026-11-05): platform tiles, warning/tactile band, rails, sleepers, ballast, station tiles and tunnel lights are colour surfaces with filtered joints and markings; station names remain lettering. The old stair flight had ten 26 cm risers but walking followed a continuous ramp below their tops. A shared 18-step specification now supplies 14.4 cm risers, 33.3 cm treads, visible tops and walking heights. Yellow nosings and riser shading are surfaces. Station auto-exit reach permits the final tread before leaving, including longer movement increments; the upper landing retains three metres of headroom. New `city-subway-platform` browser checks sample day/night and near/far surfaces, preserve the name band, compare actual step boxes against walking support, walk the flight and leave via the street exit. Both checks fail against main; an isolated restoration of the old exit reach also fails. Existing subway seating/arrival, stair/EXIT-sign and all-mode render checks plus bundle freshness pass. Pass 22 remains open for train exteriors/cabins, doors, seats, poles, station handrails, physical track/deck joins and ceiling fittings.

- Video-store secret and playable VHS (requested feature, Codex, 2026-11-05): behind the existing 18+ bead curtain are two supported display units with twelve bad movie-parody sleeves, a red bulb and a brown-coated customer. - Subway track and bogies (pass 22 completion, Codex, 2026-11-05): the track now sits in a 1.2 m recessed bed with grounded sleepers, rail feet/webs and polished heads split by physical joints. Platform fascia, tunnel returns and end/back faces close the pit. Floor rays record the lower bed depth so they cannot invisibly cover rails or wheels. Both tunnel mouths and the back wall clear the complete train envelope. Each car keeps two supported bogies with round bevelled wheels, inner flanges, axles, bearings, crossmembers, bolsters, body pivots and suspension blocks; wheel bottoms and rail heads share a common datum and gauge. Couplers meet adjacent cars. Platform/door heights, train timing and boarding access are preserved. New `city-subway-track` browser checks cover native joint/fascia rays, recessed floor depth, tunnel clearance, round wheel silhouettes, rail contact throughout travel, support joins and actual wheel raster depth. Exact main `ddad413` and isolated flat-floor, lifted-wheel and narrow-tunnel mutations fail. The existing platform regression now samples physical sleeper shaders alongside floor materials. Pass 22 is checked as a first pass; inventory/held objects (27) is next.

  Validation: all ten station/platform/door/cabin/track browser checks pass, with four existing render/seating/stair/transit checks and both bundle freshness/parsing checks. Final serial ABAB against `ddad413` at 1170×800/177×66 (41 measured frames after ten warmups): empty main 1.5/1.1 → change 2.5/1.7 ms; stopped 2.5/2.6 → 4.1/3.5; oblique 3.4/3.2 → 3.7/3.8; night 2.5/2.5 → 6.0/5.8. - Grocery/meal CI fixtures (Codex, 2026-11-06): the older all-food depletion regression compared only characters, so it missed food disappearing through colour on a persistent plate. It now compares both occupied cells and their colours, retaining its coverage of every food/drink and both picture paths. The Airport dev-jump fixture also suppresses actual pointer capture: headless pointer recentering could turn the camera immediately after the UI jump and race its exact-facing assertion. The actual menu click, forecourt/door/clearance checks and E entry remain. Both targeted browser checks pass.

- Bought foods and drinks (pass 27, Codex, 2026-11-06): all 37 bought-food and 17 drink pictures use solid colour surfaces, including fruit, street food, desserts, takeaway containers, cans, glassware, straws, picks, chopsticks and steam. Printed labels remain lettering; the takeaway noodle box gets a complete NOODLES label in place of its repeated-glyph mark. Bites, shrinking heaps, piece counts, foam, liquid levels and existing container creases remain visible. Glasses now have continuous rims and side edges at the native sampling pitch. The sake cup keeps its ceramic silhouette while the liquid recedes inside it, rather than taking bites out of the cup. Can openings and the coffee sip hole use dark colour; waffle grooves and rice/sesame detail also read through colour.

- Held weapons and tools (pass 27, Codex, 2026-11-06): the revolver now has a colour silhouette, bore, open trigger guard, fluted cylinder, joined reload crane and six separately coloured chambers. - Requested lodging/controller/bridge fixes (Codex, 6 November): ordinary and after-hours VHS tapes now play/eject with Q at motel, hotel and suite TVs, using the existing screen and music system. - Controller follow-up (requested, Codex, 6 November): Y/Triangle Actions now filters out jumping/ollies, crouch/seating, interaction, item use, camera, inventory and map because they already have controller bindings; game directional actions and aircraft power duplicates are filtered in those contexts too. Drop, empty hands, reload and other unbound extras remain. L3 now steps off a grounded moving skateboard through the normal item-use/dismount path, retaining the board in hand; the skating tip shows it. Holding that click cannot leak into on-foot sprint, RT remounts, and aerial L3 clicks leave tricks/grabs alone. Controller typing now has staggered QWERTY rows, a number row, punctuation/space and separate erase/clear/done keys; numeric inputs use a keypad. Initial letter focus is Q, and shifting retains the visible space label. - Coast/islands (19), distinct island landmarks (requested, Codex, 6 November): Cormorant Rock gains an asymmetric eroded rock outcrop with irregular shoulders, varied depth, rubble and a genuine off-centre passage; Crescent Sands' flat plank skiff becomes a beached open hull with buried keel, separate sides/ribs and a remaining thwart, moved clear of the main trail. - Hunger/thirst clarity and pacing (requested, Codex, 6 November): full water now lasts60 real minutes instead of30, and full food80 instead of40. With one meter empty, draining100 health takes10 minutes instead of4 (both empty still doubles the rate). Eating/drinking effects, recovery, gym benefits, protected driving/activities, hospital bill and nurse/dev refill behavior remain. A persistent coloured HUD banner names low food/water at20%, critical supplies at10%, and starvation/dehydration plus falling health at zero. It names both resources when needed, adds a fainting warning while empty and below35 health, and tells you to eat/drink. Text never blinks away and remains with help disabled or after transient messages expire. Space is reserved below the meters and above tasks, navigation and messages; touch layouts wrap within their available width. The one-off stage announcement uses the same reason, and injury alone retains a medical-help message instead of reporting starvation. No save-format changes; warnings derive from restored resources.
